## 2026-09-25 (~6:40 → ~7:15 PM PT) · Transaction history: pack links, missing pack images, pack pages naming the wrong pack · Claude Code (web)

- **Report (Trevor's phone screenshot):** the two newest packs on `/dashboard/history` "error out". They linked the simulator, which dead-ends on "Drop pool not indexed" for any dist with no indexed pool. Shipped `8f426ff12`: pack rows link `/pack/dist/<id>`; long titles truncate instead of running under the amount.
- **Missing images (49 dists, 8734–8869):** the PDS contract carries no image for them, and URL-pattern guesses (`packs/<tier>/<uuid>.png`, title slugs) all 404. The source is the PackNFT's `MetadataViews.Display` thumbnail, `media.nbatopshot.com/packnfts/<id>/media/image`, which redirects to the distribution's own image. Read via `pg_net` (the sandbox's egress blocks Flow REST, the DB's does not). Backfilled in migration `20260926015925`; the daily on-chain route keeps it true (`lib/packs/topshot-dist-images.ts`).
- **Wrong pack name:** `get_pack_lifecycle` guessed a pack's dist from its pulls' drop pools and ignored `pack_purchases.pack_dist_id` / `pack_rips.dist_id`; the guess disagreed with the record on 188 of 203 rips. Same migration splices the record first.
- **Lesson (promote if it recurs):** the PDS contract is the NAME source for Top Shot dists; the IMAGE source is the pack NFT's media redirect. Neither alone is complete.
- **~7:45 PM PT, the other collections:** Golazos (65) and All Day (11 real products) filled from Studio Platform `searchDistributions.images` (control 80/80; migration `20260926022452`). Left NULL on purpose: 34 internal All Day dists (Dapper's own image is `""`) and all 154 Pinnacle dists — Studio `images: []`, PDS `images: []`, and the Pinnacle PackNFT Display is one generic collection image; no per-pack art exists publicly. Open for Trevor: whether the 34 internal All Day dists should leave the packs table / sitemap.
- **~8:00 PM PT, decided ("leave all the packs out there if they're legitimate"):** 33 of the 34 hidden from `/api/packs` + the sitemap via `v_pack_distribution_exclusions_active` (no art AND no rip/purchase/sale, re-derived per read; migration `20260926023037`). The 34th, Wideout Wonders Trade In Reward (553 rips), stays. Lesson: a suppression list should be a VIEW that re-checks its own evidence, not a table of ids.
- **~8:10 PM PT, reverted:** Trevor's rule is "visible if legitimate and holds any moments" — all 33 hold moments (drop pools 1–342 editions), so nothing is hidden. Exclusion code reverted; table + view dropped (`20260926024300`). Lesson: "internal" is not "illegitimate" here — the test Trevor wants is whether the pack holds moments, not whether collectors have touched it.
- **~8:15 PM PT, history gap:** Golazos + Pinnacle pack opens (own tables, not `pack_rips`) and Golazos marketplace pack sales were absent from the transaction history — Trevor's 14 Golazos opens + 7 buys among them. Spliced in (`20260926025039`), delta-checked exactly.
- **~8:30 PM PT, All Day catalog hole:** 151 early All Day dists (ids 1–179) were absent from `pack_distributions` — 453,796 opens rendered as "Pack". Added from Studio Platform (`20260926030150`), plus 2 stray titles and 5 background images corrected. Still open: 483 Top Shot dists (3,061 opens) are missing the same way.
- **~8:45 PM PT, Top Shot:** the 483 added (`20260926030602`, from `audit_20260925_ts_dist_backfill_source`: Studio 419 + PDS/media 64). 0 pack rips estate-wide now point at a missing dist. Root cause for NEW dists: nothing creates a Top Shot `pack_distributions` row for a PDS-era dist (the on-chain route only names rows that exist) — 8870 appeared today and was missing.
- **~9:05 PM PT, "Moment" rows:** moments bought then sold (no wmc row) and team moments (edition has no player) titled "Moment". `20260926040921` resolves them via their newest `sales` row and `editions.team_name`; Trevor 9 left (no data anywhere).

## 2026-09-25 (~4:10 → ~5:40 PM PT) · Candy wallets and Panini usernames saveable to profiles; free cap 1 → 5 · Claude Code (web)

- **Finding:** 27 users, all Flow; `saved_wallets` held **0** Candy and **0** Panini rows. The Candy paste path had worked since 09-06. The free cap `saved_wallets_max = 1` 402'd every second wallet, and every user already had a Flow one. **A cap written when "one wallet" meant "one chain" silently became a one-chain limit.**
- **Shipped (`ba6c0a524`, migration `20260925233206`):** the free cap is 5 (Trevor: "allow 5 wallets per user"), and linked usernames share it. `countDistinctWallets` no longer folds base58. New `saved_collector_identities` table, `panini_owner_summary(text)` and `/api/profile/collector-identities`: an unseen username → 404 and nothing stored, a failed read → error, the summary read is bounded. Dashboard `PaniniUsernamesPanel`, labelled listing-based. The one-field banner stops advertising Panini: that field resolves non-address input on **Top Shot**, so a colliding handle would attach someone else's Flow wallet. Candy collection tab "Save to my profile" button.
- **CI:** component coverage fell 90.83% < 90.85% on the feature commit (two new components had no tests). The tests (`2fb40ffae`) took it to 91.1%; the two commits between (another session's) inherited that red.
- **Corrected a stale doc:** the roadmap's "a Candy wallet totals $0" was already false (25,375/25,375 priced); marked resolved in `roadmap-2026-08-03.md`.
- **Open:** Panini data is listing-fed, so a linked username shows "cards seen listed", not holdings. Whether the Panini panel should wait for Panini's publish is Trevor's call.

### Lessons promoted
- **A DROP/CREATE of a relation inherits the schema's DEFAULT PRIVILEGES** (anon read). `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` does not remove them. Revoke anon/authenticated by name and assert the ACL in the same migration → ledger + the migration header.
- **A delegated judgement call on a public surface should re-derive its premise.** The exclusion lapses on the first trade instead of standing as a permanent claim → migration header.
- **Vercel builds only the tip:** a commit with other commits pushed on top gets no deployment of its own. Prove it shipped with `git merge-base --is-ancestor <yours> <READY sha>`.

- **A changed `supabase/tests/*.sql` is unexecuted until CI on the Windows box**, and both vitest and the drift guard stay green on invalid SQL. Run it first in a rolled-back scratch schema through `execute_sql` → tooling-gotchas.md "Windows box, 2026-09-23" §1.
- **A backslash in `node -e` is eaten, and GNU `grep`/`sed` read `\s` as whitespace, so they cannot check it** → the same section, §2.

## 2026-09-20 (~4:0x PM → ~4:4x PM PT) · Claude Code, cloud — "do all you can to not leave anything unresolved, then update CLAUDE.md"

**Verdict: the last open residual closed, and the mutation run caught a clause I had shipped THIRTY MINUTES earlier that was covered by nothing.** Then CLAUDE.md took four rules, each paid for by displacing existing text verbatim.

### Shipped

- **`20260920230950`** — both writers of `pack_rips.pull_value_usd` gain the WHOLE-PACK check in ONE migration, closing **#128 residual (b)**.
- **Both DB-invariant pins** re-pinned and both PINS entries re-pointed; `P-AD-SHORT` added; `moments_pulled` added to the rollup pin's fixture.
- **`CLAUDE.md`** — 4 rules added, 6 bullets displaced verbatim. **39,996 chars against the 40,000 limit** (measured with `node .length`, never `wc -c`).
- **`docs/reference/{key-files-and-honesty,cron-and-schedulers,database}.md`** — the displaced text plus the full case histories.

### Re-derived over the population, because a sample is not enough to REMOVE rows

#128's residual quoted 1,436/1,436 from a sample. A predicate that can only remove rows needs the population: **419,233 of 419,233** All Day rips holding any `allday_pack_pull` row have pull-row count = `moments_pulled`; **0 mismatches, 0 valued on a short pack.** So it is a **ban at zero** and it removed nothing (smoke: `allday_resolved 199`).

⭐ **One migration, not two, and that is the whole point:** these are the two writers of one column, and #128 IS the record of what their disagreeing costs. Fixing the Top Shot arm alone would have recreated that divergence one property over — and `PackHistoryClient.tsx` already records that the 2026-09-12 basis change had to move both together *"because changing one alone makes them fight, which is how the first attempt at this failed."*

### The find, and it is against my own work from half an hour earlier

🚨 **The mutation run showed the backfill's ALL DAY arm clause was COVERED BY NOTHING.** Dropping it passed every assertion in its own pin, while the identical mutation on the generic arm reddened instantly — the All Day fixtures have `moments_pulled` equal to their pull count either way. ⚠ **I had even written a header line claiming the All Day assertion lived in the sibling pin. It did not** — that pin covers the *rollup's* clause, not the backfill's All Day arm. Added `P-AD-SHORT` in its own wide-`p_limit` scenario so property 6's forced-choice block stays undisturbed; all three clauses now caught.

⭐ **A clause shipped in a migration and covered by nothing is this register's recurring shape. Assert the arm, do not describe it** — and run the mutation matrix on the arm you just added, not only on the ones you inherited.

### CLAUDE.md, by displacement

Headroom was **2 characters** at the start, so every rule had to displace one. Added: the read-layer/write-layer fabrication rule (honesty); the generalised leg-`ORDER BY` rule (which absorbed the old queue-walk bullet); a **fifth** way a measurement lies (a correctness probe is silent about cost by construction); and *a filed CORRECTION is a hypothesis too — read the ITEM, never an excerpt*. Plus two extensions: `relallvisible`/`relpages` beside the `last_vacuum` rule, and *measure even a blessed pattern* on the `LATERAL` rule.

Displaced verbatim (nothing deleted, each with a one-line pointer): differential upsert · `EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS`/57014 · `.range()` needs a deterministic `.order()` · batch `.insert()` all-or-nothing · `apply_migration` PGRST002 burst → `database.md`. The four measurement-lies ways · the queue-walk bullet · CADENCE AND BUDGET · the 600 s pg_cron reader → `cron-and-schedulers.md`.

### State at close

- Drain **confirmed in production** at the raised `p_limit`: the 22:53Z tick handled **300 zeros in 9,782 ms** of a 50 s budget. 79,234 left at ~7,200/day → **~11 days**.
- ⏳ **One verification owed and it is clock-gated, not a judgement:** `mv_topshot_pack_rip_values` still holds the pre-repair snapshot (20,931 zero rows) because pg_cron 208 refreshes at `5 */6 * * *`. After 00:05Z then 00:42Z, the nine dists should all carry a non-zero `realized_mean`. ⛔ Deliberately not forced — two concurrent MV refreshes to save three hours is not a trade worth making here.
- **#128 residual (a)** stays open and small: ~3.6 % of positive Top Shot rows keep a legacy partial sum, preserved on purpose. **#118's lane-coverage gap** stays open with its numbers. **#8** is dormant until the NBA season.

`npm test` · `tsc --noEmit` clean · `lint:ratchet` 712/712 · local DB-invariant suite · migration parse check · `claude-md-stays-under-the-memory-file-limit` green · `register:check`, `memory:retired:check`, `docs:issues-index` (129) · both prosrc md5s identical across prod, migration file and pin · ACLs and `proconfig` re-read after the apply · ledger `^### ` +1, swallowed 3, future 0.

## 2026-09-20 (~3:4x PM → ~4:0x PM PT) · Claude Code, cloud — "keep going" (continuation: #121 closed, #8 triaged)

**Verdict: #121 closed on a mechanism, not on "it got better" — and the obvious attribution was wrong.** `rpc_ops_snapshot()`, the function the daytime monitor and night pass read, was filed 09-14 as exceeding a 45 s budget. It now returns in **659 ms warm / 2.7 s cold** while doing MORE work (three migrations since each added a guard caller).

### The attribution, which is the whole value of the entry

The instance went Small→LARGE at 10:39:56 AM PT, so "the resize fixed it" is the tempting read and I nearly took it. ⭐ **`pg_stat_statements` says 2,200,180 blocks per call pooled over 131 calls; my warm calls touch ~52,945 each — a 41× drop in BLOCKS TOUCHED. A bigger cache changes the hit/read ratio; it cannot change how many blocks a plan touches.** So it is a data-layout change, and there is exactly one candidate: `fmv_snapshots_2026` was autovacuumed at 11:29 AM PT and now reads **100.0 % all-visible** (33,820/33,820 pages) against a previous vacuum of 2026-08-30. The hotspot is an Index Only Scan over the covering index #121 had already correctly ruled in; on a degraded visibility map it heap-fetches most of 1,106,025 index rows, on a fresh one 37,561 (3.4 %).

⭐ **So #121's own "IT IS NOT A MISSING INDEX" was right and one word short — it was a missing VISIBILITY MAP**, the same family as #108's `wallet_moments_cache` finding. ⚠ Half of that is inference and is labelled so in the register: I have no historical `relallvisible` for 09-14, so the degradation is inferred from the blocks drop plus the vacuum timestamp. The blocks figure is the load-bearing half and is cache-independent.

### An optimisation I tested and did not ship

The function traverses ~1.1M index rows to produce 14,016 distinct editions — exactly the shape CLAUDE.md says to replace with a per-key `LATERAL`, and the covering index carries the aggregated columns, so the precondition genuinely holds. Warm, Top Shot: `DISTINCT ON` **85,071 buffers (9,129 real reads) / 555 ms** vs per-key `LATERAL` **105,321 buffers (0 reads) / 179 ms**. 3.1× faster in time and **24 % worse in buffers**, which is the metric this repo judges on; 27 % of the LATERAL's work is probing the empty 2027 partition once per edition. Output verified identical on all seven confidence values.

⭐ **Recorded with its numbers precisely so the next session does not re-spend the afternoon.** A blessed pattern whose precondition is met is still not automatically an improvement, and a function at 1.5 % of its budget is not where to find out.

### #8 triaged rather than worked

`sync-nba-projections` is still 16 runs / 0 ok in 48 h, 485 rows, **62.2 days stale**, and on no cadence arm. Two things checked before reaching for the register's suggested fix:
- **The user-facing half is already safe.** `/api/fast-break/today` filters projections on `game_date = <today>`, so 62-day-old rows are excluded by construction rather than published as current — and `api-fast-break-failed-read-is-not-an-empty-slate.test.ts` already pins the failed-read-vs-empty-slate distinction.
- ⛔ **The register's "cheapest real fix is an arm" is the one thing not to do now.** An arm on a lane that is known-dead and cannot be fixed from here would sit permanently breached, which CLAUDE.md names as no alarm at all (and #116 records the same refusal). The cause is the sports-proxy egress block: measured 09-17 from a residential box, ESPN 200s there and 403s from Supabase edge (fixable by a Worker), while `cdn.nba.com` 403s residentially too behind Akamai. Neither is reachable from this sandbox.

⭐ **The reframing worth keeping:** it is the NBA offseason, so a dead projections lane has near-zero impact today and becomes critical the moment the season tips off in mid-October. The item currently reads as urgent-and-parked; the honest shape is **dormant now, hard deadline in ~3 weeks**.

### Verified

Docs-only change, no DB and no code: `register:check` green, `docs:issues-index` 129 items, ledger `^### ` +1 exactly with swallowed-headings 3 and future-dated 0.

## 2026-09-20 (~2:2x PM → ~3:4x PM PT) · Claude Code, cloud — "keep going" (continuation: register #118)

**Verdict: I shipped the one change the register explicitly forbids, caught it by finally reading the whole item, and the correction is better than either the original or what #118 proposed.** The board's "N packs would otherwise qualify" is now adjudicated live rather than assumed — and the honest number was 2 against a published 3.

### Shipped

- **`20260920220641`** — the staleness view recomputes each candidate's EV live (`compute_pack_ev_per_edition_weighted`, the same function the snapshotter uses, same `slots` expression, at the live ask) and adds an availability predicate; new `candidates_considered` / `candidates_adjudicated` columns so the 60-row recompute cap cannot be a silent truncation.
- **`20260920222056`** — rebuilds that predicate to **FAIL OPEN**, plus `dropped_delisted` / `dropped_no_longer_positive_ev` so every removal is auditable.
- **Route + client** — `verdict_complete` passed through; copy changed from *"N packs would otherwise qualify"* to *"N packs still qualify on a live re-check"*, prefixed *"at least"* when the cap was hit.
- **7 test arms**, mutation-proven in three directions (old copy reds 3, dropped hedge reds 1, `verdict_complete` defaulting to true reds the absence arm). This leg had **no test at all** before, which is how an unchecked counterfactual survived on a buy/no-buy board.

### Three mistakes, and they are the content of this entry

1. ⛔ **I did the thing #118 forbids, twice in bold: "do NOT add an availability predicate."** Cause: I worked from an excerpt of the item, not the item — I had grepped its first paragraph early in the session and never went back. The prohibition's *reason* is what matters and I only understood it on reading the whole entry: this count reaching 0 flips the copy to *"No +EV packs right now"*, a claim about the MARKET, so a predicate that drops rows on MISSING data lets our own read failure publish it. The fail-open rebuild gets both halves — a delisted pack stops counting, an absence can never suppress a true warning. ⭐ **It produces the same output as the fail-closed version on today's data, which is exactly why no test of the output would have found the difference.** The difference is in what happens when an input goes missing.
2. ⚠ **My first measurement was wrong and would have made a much louder, false finding.** I probed the EV function with `slots = 1` hardcoded and got −320.09 / −556.79 / −1.68 — "all three packs are negative, the banner is a fabrication". `461` and `474` are **six-slot** Premium Packs. That understated them sixfold and inverted the conclusion; the production-shaped query says two of the three really do still qualify. I was one step from filing a fabricated-buy-signal report.
3. 🚨 **I repeated the prior session's own recorded mistake, in the same item.** #118 says, of the `last_checked_at` trap: *"THE ANSWER WAS ALREADY WRITTEN ON THE COLUMN — I DID NOT RUN col_description()."* Neither did I. I reconstructed it from the upsert's `WHERE` clause over several queries; the column comment has said *"Do NOT hang a staleness or freshness monitor on this column"* since 2026-08-26. **A rule this repo has written down twice and missed twice is a rule to run, not to admire.**

### What the parse check caught

My header edit left one line without its `-- ` prefix — `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW\` resets` — making the whole migration invalid SQL. Same shape CLAUDE.md records for `20260913032000`. The cause is worth knowing: a `python .index("CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW")` matched a **backtick-quoted mention inside the header**, not the DDL, so the rebuild spliced at the wrong offset. `scripts/check-migration-sql-parses.sh` named the file and the line; the fix is to anchor on `^CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW public.<name>`.

### Still open, and it is the bigger number

Of **1,988** Top Shot dists with a live ask, only **56** are in the `pool_source = 'atlas'` population the live-ask lane iterates, and only **150 of `pack_ev_latest`'s 1,210** Top Shot rows are fresher than 48 h — 1,060 are over a week old, the oldest from 2026-04-22. The EV function works fine for gql-pool dists, so widening is viable; it needs a population-equivalence argument and a cost measurement (`pack_ev_history` would take ~45k rows/day against ~1.3k) and is #118's real exit, as the 09-14 chain already concluded. Also filed in passing: reading `pack_ev_latest` at all costs a Seq Scan over 368,771 rows plus a **33 MB external merge sort** (~1.58 s) on every uncached public request — my change adds ~27 ms to that.

### A near-miss on a CORRECTION, which is the subtlest thing in this entry

While rebasing I found a concurrent session had edited the `focus.md` steer I left, with a commit message saying it corrected two of my claims. I started editing my own migration headers and ledger entries to match. ⛔ **One of those two "corrections" had itself been WITHDRAWN a commit later, and my original statement was right.**

- **Accepted, and it is a fair hit:** my steer led with a WALL-CLOCK pair (5.8 s vs 7.4 s at `p_limit` 500 vs 2000) and concluded *"rows are nearly free"*. On blocks/call, 2000 costs **3.26×** for 4× the rows — so the headline number pointed the wrong way, and the section claimed *"the measurement is already done"* when it was not. ⭐ The steer did name the pgss-pooling problem and ask for exactly the clean blocks/call pair they then took, so it worked as a handoff — but a section whose headline figure argues the opposite of its own conclusion is a bad steer regardless of the footnotes.
- **Withdrawn by its own author, and I nearly propagated it:** the claim that *"#126 was not resolved, it was outrun"*. The next commit reverts it — *"I contradicted its entry without reading it"* — and #126 really is closed as RESOLVED in the register on a two-change-point natural experiment. **My original line was correct and I left it alone.**

⭐ **The rule: a correction is a finding, and CLAUDE.md's standing rule applies to it unchanged — re-derive what it measured before acting.** I had the commit message and not the commit history, which is the same mistake in kind as reading an excerpt of #118 instead of the item, twice in one session. **Read to the end of the thread before editing on its strength.**

### Verified

`npm test` 1,575 files / 17,934 tests · `tsc --noEmit` clean at `--max-old-space-size=3072` · `lint:ratchet` 712/712 baseline · local DB-invariant suite 195/195 · 1,220 migration files parsed, 0 syntax errors · `register:check`, `docs:issues-index` (126) green · `security_invoker=on` and the ACL re-read after each apply (anon SELECT false, authenticated false, service_role true), pre-existing columns unchanged in name, type and order.

## 2026-09-20 (~1:2x PM → ~2:1x PM PT) · Claude Code, cloud — "look through known problems for things you can work on and resolve"

**Verdict: the register pointed at #93 (a backfill stamping its own failures as done) and re-deriving it surfaced a bigger, unregistered defect one column over — 82,864 Top Shot pack rips publishing a fabricated `pull_value_usd = 0`, which had defeated a fix this repo already shipped for that exact defect on 2026-08-01.** Four migrations, a new DB-invariant pin mutation-proven in nine directions, #93 partly closed, and a cost regression I introduced mid-pass and caught from the production lane's own `duration_ms`.

### Shipped

- **`20260920203815`** — `backfill_pack_rip_metadata`'s generic arm becomes ALL-OR-NOTHING and WHOLE-PACK; `COALESCE(SUM(fc.fmv_usd), 0)` gone; new `zero_repair` (15 %) and `unpriced_retry` (5 %) legs; `COMMENT ON COLUMN` on `pull_value_usd` and `metadata_updated_at`.
- **`20260920204056`** — the All Day repair leg's ORDER BY moves off the immutable `sealed_at DESC`.
- **`20260920204628`** — the drain `LIMIT` cannot go negative.
- **`20260920210651`** + **`20260920205900`** (index repo record) — `idx_pack_rips_unvalued_stamped` and the leg predicate that makes it usable.
- **`supabase/tests/backfill_pack_rip_metadata.sql`** (new pin, registered in `db-invariants-drift-guard`), 12 assertions / 7 properties.
- **Register:** new **#128**; **#93** → partly resolved with its numbers re-derived and its own framing corrected.

### How it was found, which is the part worth keeping

Not by reading the function. By **diffing two writers of one column**. `rollup_allday_rip_pull_value()` has been pinned all-or-nothing since 2026-09-12 — its pin file literally says *"a rip with no pulls at all is left NULL, NOT WRITTEN AS 0"* — and its header describes the Top Shot path as *"the same source and same shape"*. The other writer did the opposite on precisely that case. ⭐ CLAUDE.md already names this: **a header saying it MIRRORS another implementation is a claim with no test.** The estate had the test for one side and the prose for the other.

### Two rules this pass earned

1. ⭐ **A FIX AT THE READ LAYER DOES NOT CLOSE A FABRICATION THE WRITE LAYER CAN RE-CREATE.** `20260801204912_..._never_fabricate_zero` made `get_pack_lifecycle_row()` return NULL when nothing is priced, and it is correct. It was defeated within weeks because **a fabricated 0 is not absent**: `count(pull_value_usd)` counts it and `sum()` adds it, so the reader treats it as a measurement. `/nba-top-shot/pack/dist/7738` published `avg_realized_value_per_pack 0.05` over 1,213 opened packs. 👉 **When you delete a `COALESCE(x, 0)` from a view, grep for the WRITER of the column it reads.**
2. ⭐ **A CORRECTNESS PROBE IS SILENT ABOUT COST BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE PRODUCTION LANE IS NOT.** My ORDER BY fix was right and carried a **28× buffer regression** (195,112 shared + 22,834 temp against 6,908 — the new order had no index, so the leg became a parallel seq scan plus a 16 MB external merge). The before/after that proved the fix (`allday_resolved` 0 → 10) could not see it. What caught it was the next `pipeline_runs` row: **`duration_ms` 37,000 against 4,746 an hour earlier, same box, same `p_limit`.** 👉 **After changing an ORDER BY on a hot lane, read the next production row's duration — not only your own output.**

### A spent lever caught before it was pulled

`metrics-latest.json` carried a pending action for this pipeline: *"remaining fails are batch-size (route QUEUE)"*. Split on `pg_postmaster_start_time()` (10:39 AM PT — the LARGE resize restarted Postgres, CLAUDE.md #126): **before 45 runs / 16 ok (36 %) / avg 28,745 ms; after 3 / 3 ok / avg 4,959 ms.** The timeouts were the SMALL tier. Cutting the batch would have reduced throughput to fix a problem the resize had already removed. ⚠ n=3 — re-derive over a full day before closing it.

### What I deliberately did not do

- **Did not squash the four migrations**, although nothing was committed yet and it would have read more tidily. Three of them are about what the function publishes and one is about what it costs; a bisect has to be able to tell them apart, and the DB had already accepted them in that order — squashing would have made the repo lie about what was applied.
- **Did not make the value overwrite unconditional.** A stored 0 is cleared to NULL; a stored POSITIVE is preserved when it cannot be recomputed. `fmv_snapshots` is delete-then-insert, so "unpriceable right now" can be a momentary absence, and the tidier rule could blank a large share of the column on one unlucky tick.
- **Did not widen the All Day arm's whole-pack check.** It needs BOTH writers changed together plus a re-pin; measured 1,436/1,436 equal, so it is latent. Recorded in #128 residual (b).
- ~~**Did not touch the route** that drops the three new `extra` counters on the floor.~~ ✅ **Reversed later in the same session and it was the right call** — a count the caller does not forward is not an instrument. `app/api/cron/backfill-pack-rip-metadata/route.ts` now forwards `zero_cleared`, `zero_repriced` and `value_newly_written`, with `?? null` rather than `?? 0` (against an older function body those keys are ABSENT, and a 0 would read as "the leg ran and found nothing"). Both arms mutation-proven. ⚠ Left as a struck-through line rather than deleted: the reason I first skipped it was "scope", and scope is not a reason to ship a drain nobody can watch.

### The follow-on, and the shape of it is the point

The nine dists reading exactly `realized_pull_value_usd 0.00` turned out to be only **365 rips** in total — `mv_topshot_pack_realized_ev` gates on `n_opens >= 10`, so those are small dists whose entire attributed set was fabricated. That is the whole user-visible USD 0.00 symptom, and it was worth clearing now rather than over the drain's six weeks.

⛔ **The obvious way to do it was the wrong way.** A targeted `UPDATE` reproducing the all-or-nothing pricing rule would have been **a second copy of the logic whose first duplication is the defect this entire pass was about.** Instead: backdate those 365 rows' `metadata_updated_at`, which puts them at the head of the `zero_repair` leg (ordered `metadata_updated_at ASC`) that already shipped. One tick consumed all 365, and `metadata_updated_at = '1970-01-01'` returning to 0 rows is the completion check.

Result, per dist: `still_zero` 0 on all nine, new means USD 0.24 → **80.19**. ⭐ `8753` had been publishing **USD 0.00 realized value for a pack whose pulls average eighty dollars** — on a buy/no-buy board that is an inverted signal, not an imprecise one. The boards correct on pg_cron 208 then 245 (~00:42Z), deliberately not forced: two CONCURRENT MV refreshes hours after #126's fleet slowdown was resolved is not a cost worth taking to save three hours.

⚠ **What is still open and is in `focus.md` rather than here:** the DILUTION. 162 of 295 dists keep an understated `realized_mean` for ~46 days and no surface says so. Raising the route's `p_limit` would cut that to ~12 days and 4× the rows cost only 1.28× the wall time — but wall time is not IO, and the only `pg_stat_statements` figure available is pooled across two compute tiers and four body changes, so it is not a reading of now. I left the decision with its numbers rather than taking it on a measurement I could not clean up.

### The control that actually closed it

Everything above was proved with MCP calls I made myself. The row that closes the pass is one I did not make: the **21:53Z production tick**, read from `pipeline_runs` after the Vercel deploy went READY.

```
ok true · rows_found 500 · duration_ms 4,194
zero_cleared 7 · zero_repriced 68 · value_newly_written 76
```

Three claims settle on that single row: the deployed route really does forward the new counters (they appear in `extra` for the first time), the 28× buffer regression really is gone **under the production caller** and not only under an `execute_sql` call — 4,194 ms against the 20:53Z tick's 37,000 ms on the same box an hour earlier — and the zero drain is running at ~75/tick in production. ⚠ The 20:53Z row is the last one without those keys, so a reader comparing `extra` across that boundary is looking at two route builds, not at a lane that stopped reporting.

### Verified

`npm test` 1,574 files / 17,912 tests · `tsc --noEmit` clean at `--max-old-space-size=3072` · `lint:ratchet` 712/712 baseline · local DB-invariant suite **195/195** plus `check-migration-sql-parses.sh` 1,218 files / 0 syntax errors, on a throwaway Postgres 16 built with the CI recipe · `register:check`, `memory:retired:check`, `docs:issues-index` (126) green · ledger `^### ` count +1 exactly, swallowed-headings 3, future-dated 0 · prosrc md5 `db0798a87fad8de84215f839ebe013f7` identical across production, the migration file and the pin.

## 2026-09-20 (~12:3x PM → ~1:1x PM PT) · Claude Code, cloud (Opus 5) — "further the Pinnacle roadmap and feature parity vs Top Shot"

**Verdict: the documented parity gap ("`sets`: all except Pinnacle") was not an unwired feature — it was a live honesty defect wearing a feature gap's clothes, and it had been answering a public endpoint for 159 days.** Shipped the Set Tracker for Disney Pinnacle end-to-end, removed the route entry that had been publishing a confident zero, and recorded a twelfth fabrication shape the four layer-helpers cannot see.

### Shipped

- **`app/api/pinnacle-set-progress/route.ts` (new).** 169 `pinnacle_catalog.set_render_id` checklists × `wallet_moments_cache.render_id`, cost-to-finish from the render-grain floor map, both truncation flags treated as a 503 rather than a smaller answer.
- **`lib/collections.ts`** — Pinnacle's `pages` gains `sets`, the last per-collection gap on that tab.
- **`app/api/sets-db/route.ts`** — the `disney-pinnacle` entry REMOVED (see below).
- **`lib/entity-href.ts`** — `pinnacleRenderHref()` and `setEntityHref()`, the latter returning `null` where a set has no page.
- **`lib/sets/display.ts`** — the 15 live Pinnacle variants get stripe colours (they were all falling back to COMMON grey).
- **Tests:** `api-pinnacle-set-progress` (12 arms), `entity-href-set-and-render` (9), `FeatureTabGate` re-pinned + a new pass-through arm, `sitemap-data` 76 → 77 with presence and absence assertions.
- **Docs:** the twelfth shape into `key-files-and-honesty.md`; `routes-and-surfaces.md` corrected and given the four-backend Set Tracker map; ledger.

### The finding

`/api/sets-db` joins `editions` + `sets` on `collection_id` and its map carried `disney-pinnacle` since 2026-04-14. **Pinnacle has 0 rows in both tables** — it is catalogued render-keyed by design. So the endpoint answered every wallet `{"totalSets":0,"sets":[]}` with a 200 and a public cache header.

What makes it a new shape: **every read succeeded.** No `error`, no `?? 0`, no swallowed catch, no timeout — the zero is arithmetically correct for the rows the route was handed. The claim is made by the ROUTING, one line above the query, and it is falsifiable only by counting rows in the target table. ⛔ **A slug→UUID map asserts that the id has rows in the table that map is consulted for**, which is a different fact from "the UUID is real" — the registry's own Pinnacle UUID was correct the whole time.

Three reasons nothing caught it, each worth keeping: the page was gated off (**a feature flag in front of a surface is not a guard on the route behind it**); the route's own test mocks the tables to `[]` and asserts `totalSets === 0` **as the happy path**; and the registry-consistency guard asks whether a LINK resolves, never whether the TABLE has rows.

### Verified against production, not fixtures

169 sets · 2,600 renders · **0 rows with a NULL set id** · set_id→set_name **1:1** · 50,806 Pinnacle wmc rows over 144 wallets · **0 owned renders absent from the catalog**. Ground truth for `0x8bc1c0249e2ebb3e` — **169 / 82 complete / 45 in progress / 42 not started**, *Mandalorian Vol.1* 51/54 at **$58.00 to finish** — computed in SQL and matched by the route's logic.

### Three things kept honest on the way

1. **Truncation is a failure, not a smaller answer.** A short catalog inflates completion; a short holdings read deflates it. Both 503. Ceilings sized from measured growth (~190 renders/month) and the measured largest holder (9,882 rows / 1,527 renders), not guessed.
2. **No link to a page that does not exist.** `get_set_detail` returns NULL for every Pinnacle slug, so `setEntityHref()` returns `null` and the heading renders as text — the 54-dead-links lesson applied before shipping rather than after. Piece links go to the canonical `/pinnacle/moment/<render_id>`, not the `/edition/` spelling that 308s.
3. **The cost gate is derived from its writer.** `floor_ask` is rewritten daily, so the gate is 48 h on the map's own stamp — reusing `ASK_STALE_HOURS` (12 h) would have fired on a healthy lane and marked every set "unpriced".

### 🧹 Close-out — what shipped, what was left, and why

**Shipped after the naming work:** the Set Tracker's checklist re-keyed to CHARACTER grain (a variant is a parallel — 445 hidden completions across 57 of 144 wallets); the Market tab's Set/Series/Player/Min-price filters, dead on four of five collections, fixed at the source for Pinnacle and Candy; both Candy boards de-duplicated by migration; "SHOP THIS SET" where a collection has no set page.

**Left open, deliberately:**
- **#129** Top Shot + All Day Market filters. Needs `DROP`+`CREATE` on two RPCs (a `CREATE OR REPLACE` with extra args makes an *overload*, not a replacement). The register now carries the full recipe read off the live functions. Not shipped because it touches the flagship's Market *and* Sniper, needs a 5,700-char plpgsql body reproduced exactly, and the thread was about to be archived — unmonitored, on a day that had already produced one outage.
- **#131** ingest half: `candy_listings` holds two active PDAs for one mint. Not masked by the view fix on purpose.
- The `/pinnacle/moment/` → `/disney-pinnacle/edition/` URL move stays reverted; it doesn't serve the accuracy gate and the house-shaped URL already resolves via 308.

**Docs:** CLAUDE.md gained the deploy-gate rule by displacement (39,977 → 39,989; headroom 23 → 11, stated rather than rounded); the completion-grain canon went into `parallels-variants-data-model.md` beside the pricing rule it is the opposite of — which is how I got it wrong in the first place.

**The through-line for the day:** four defects, three invisible to a green suite, and the two best finds both came from checking something I was about to *reuse* rather than something I was building.

### 🔄 Then: the naming, which turned out to be inverted rather than merely different

Trevor asked for Pinnacle to match Top Shot's nomenclature. The divergence was `/pinnacle/moment/<render_id>` — the only published collection whose canonical entity page sat outside `(collections)`, under a slug that is not the collection's URL slug, using a noun the page never used for itself. The house-shaped URL existed **and 308'd away to it**. Inverted, not missing.

Moved to `/disney-pinnacle/edition/<render_id>`; the old route is a permanent redirect and **must never be deleted** (125 visitors / 173 pageviews in 30 d — the 11th busiest route — and ~2,600 sitemap URLs).

Three things this turned up that were worth more than the rename:

1. **The move nearly smuggled in a caching change.** The shared edition segment is `revalidate = 600`; the route Pinnacle moved from had no segment config. Landing the body there would have put its "this pin didn't load" branch behind a ten-minute ISR cache — in a file the diff does not touch. `await connection()` keeps the Pinnacle arm dynamic. **A rename must not change rendering semantics.**
2. **The refactor shrank a guard's population on a green run.** `server-pages-error-vs-absent-sweep` walks `page.tsx | layout.tsx`; 600 lines of reads moved into a co-located body and left the sweep silently. Widened to every `.tsx` under `app/` — measured first: files 183 → 303, candidates 25 → 26, findings 0 → 0.
3. **A constraint survived with a new reason.** `next.config.ts` excludes `moment` from its `/pinnacle/:page(...)` allowlists. That used to protect a live page; it now protects the redirect from a two-hop chain. Deleting it because the old reason expired is how the chain comes back.

### 🚨 And then I shipped the backend behind the auth wall

`/disney-pinnacle/sets` is anon-public and sitemapped; `/api/pinnacle-set-progress` was **not** in proxy.ts's `PUBLIC_READ_APIS`. An anonymous reader's only fetch would have 307'd to `/login`, `.json()` would have thrown, and the tab would have rendered an error for a wallet that resolves fine.

⛔ **Every instrument said ship**: 17,911 tests green, `tsc` clean, ratchet at baseline, deploy READY with aliases attached. It was caught by **fetching the deployed route** and reading `x-matched-path: /login` on a **200** — this repo's own "rendered DOM, not HTTP 200" rule, arriving one step later than it should have. **Fifth instance of one shape**, three of them Pinnacle, all listed in `proxy-is-public-path.test.ts`'s own header.

The fix is not a fifth hand-kept row: `set-tracker-backends-are-anon-public.test.ts` **derives** its population from the client's dispatch, so a sixth collection's backend reds it unless the reader who can see the tab can reach it. Mutation-proved both directions before shipping — with the proxy line removed it fails and names the route.

### ⚠ The test that went green for the wrong reason

`FeatureTabGate.test.tsx`'s "second collection/page pair" arm was `disney-pinnacle` + `sets` — so adding the tab satisfied it **vacuously**, and it would have stopped exercising the gate entirely. Re-pinned by DERIVING the pair from the registry with a vacuity guard, plus a new arm pinning Pinnacle's Sets tab as a transparent pass-through, so a registry revert reds something that names the surface.

## 2026-09-20 (~11:0x AM → ~12:0x PM PT) · Claude Code, Windows box (Opus 5) — "the moments ownership sweep is keyed to `seeded_wallets`"

**Verdict: the fix had been deferred as "a cost decision for Trevor", and the cost was simply never sized. The marginal set was 5 wallets and +1 %.** Handed a filed diagnosis with an explicit instruction to re-derive it before acting; it held, with two corrections. Shipped the fix, verified it end-to-end live, and recorded the remainder as #127 rather than sweeping it.

### Shipped

- **`app/api/seed-wallet-refresh/route.ts` + `__tests__/seed-wallet-refresh-saved-sweep.test.ts` (20 arms).** The recurring cohorts were selected `seeded_wallets.id % N = K` and never read `saved_wallets`, so a user's portfolio was ownership-re-verified only if the wallet happened to also be a seeded demo wallet. The sweep now rides the four existing cron cohorts — no new schedule, no DB object — excluding wallets an ACTIVE seeded row already walks.
- **Docs:** CLAUDE.md gains the marginal-cost / cap-is-not-a-rate rule (**displaced**, not spent: 39,923 → 39,926 of 40,000, three items moved verbatim to `database.md` and `cron-and-schedulers.md`); the full canon into `cron-and-schedulers.md`; **#127** into the register; the new knobs into `operations/cron-schedule.md` — where **`SEED_REFRESH_BACKSTOP_FRESH_HOURS` was documented as 3 h and had been 12 in code since 09-13**.

### The measurement, and why it proved a POPULATION bug rather than a capacity one

All 27 saved wallets / 135 (wallet, collection) pairs: **saved AND seeded → 22 wallets, 0.18–0.71 days; saved, NOT seeded → 5 wallets, 5.86–42.87 days.** ⭐ **The ranges do not overlap and 5 × 5 is exactly the stale set.** That shape is the finding: a capacity ceiling produces a CONTINUUM of ages, a wrong population produces TWO DISJOINT CLUSTERS with membership as the only predictor. No amount of capacity fixes the wrong set.

⭐ **The falsifier the brief named was run and did not fire.** Writers of `last_scanned_at` are the five per-collection backfill children; their only orchestrator is `wallet-backfill-multicollection`, whose callers are this route plus three on-demand ones. **`cron.job` was queried rather than assumed — no pg_cron job dispatches a wallet backfill** (jobids 259/497 re-derive stats FROM the cache and cannot advance a scan stamp). Corroborating: the 5 wallets carried `scan_count` 1–3 against a herd walked twice daily.

⭐ **One premise checked that would have made the whole fix the wrong shape.** A cheap `skip_cached=true` walk DOES prune departed moments — `deleteUnseenWmcRows` runs every pass and `skipCached` suppresses only the re-WRITE of an already-cached id, never the on-chain enumeration. Had that been false, re-scanning would not have fixed stale ownership at all.

### Two corrections to the filing, both narrowing, neither refuting

1. **The saved share of the 47 flagged rows is 5 rows / 4 wallets, not 25.** `check_wmc_ownership_freshness()` takes `p_max_age_days DEFAULT 7` and counts only pairs holding cached rows — so reading "47 flagged" as "47 stale pairs" overstates ~5×.
2. **"Every genuinely new user arrives saved-but-not-seeded" is too strong.** `lib/allow-list/prewarm.ts` INSERTS an early-access signup into `seeded_wallets` as a side effect, which is *why* 22 of 27 are seeded. The claim survives in its true form: a wallet reaching `saved_wallets` by the ordinary in-app save is never seeded.

### Four things I got wrong

- ⛔ **I claimed `npm test` was green off a `[exited with code 0]` that was the WRAPPER's.** The real summary was `2 failed | 1568 passed`. Both are 30 s timeouts under full-suite load (476 s wall, 1,404 s import), pass in isolation 186/186, and neither imports this route. **This trap is already written in CLAUDE.md and I hit it anyway** — the same shape as the session that pushed CLAUDE.md 8 chars over the limit the day before.
- ⛔ **The first exit check was taken MID-WALK.** The freshness function is per (wallet, collection) PAIR and the orchestrator walks the five collections sequentially at ~10 s each, so a wallet's `max(last_scanned_at)` read fresh while its All Day leg was still in flight. It looked like a partial failure and invited a fix for a bug that was not there.
- ⛔ **I appended register item #127 to the END OF THE FILE, outside the `### Open` window the index generator parses** — so `--check` **exited 0 and printed "index is current"** while #127 was in no index row. Caught only by grepping for the row I expected. ⭐ **The guard's population is bounded by a section heading, and it is silent about anything outside it** — assert the count RISES (124 → 125), never that the check passed.
- ⚠ **I nearly filed "the unswept remainder is all truncation stubs" off a BIASED SAMPLE.** The ten worst by age are all exactly 50 cached rows; across all 31 the distribution is 15 at 50, 7 at 1, and a tail to 3,874.

### Verified live

Deploy `defc1d55e` READY with aliases. Backlog cleared via `workflow_dispatch` of the GHA backstop — the only secret-free lever, since the route needs `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN` and harvesting it would print a live secret. **`check_wmc_ownership_freshness()` → zero rows for any saved wallet** (47 → 42), worst saved-only pair **42.87 days → 0.011**. Per-cohort fired **1 / 0 / 3 / 1** — the address-hash partition dispatching each wallet exactly once. ⭐ **The same workflow's PRE-deploy run 45 min earlier wrote `saved_sweep_state=NULL` on all four cohorts — a free positive control** that the fields came from this deploy. ⭐ **And the first re-verify found real drift:** one wallet's Top Shot cache went **2,850 → 2,941**, `last_found_count` now equal to `cached_rows`.

⏳ **NOT verified on an UNFORCED wave** — the cadence gate executes only UTC hours 0/1/12/13, so the first ordinary primary to exercise this code lands **00:45 UTC (5:45 PM PT)**. The sweep is not gated by `forceWave`, so a primary should behave identically; that is an inference, not a measurement.

### Left open, deliberately

**#127** — 41 wallets with cached moments that nothing re-verifies, worst 66.2 days, neither saved nor seeded (no `user_profiles`, no `allow_list`): drive-by lookups. The marginal-cost argument does not transfer — the set is **unbounded** (any anonymous lookup joins it) and none of it is a logged-in user's portfolio. Estate-wide, **776 of 1,545 cached wallets sit at exactly 50 rows**, 810 on some `SUSPICIOUS_COUNTS` value. Exit is an eviction policy or a staleness disclosure, not a third sweep.

## 2026-09-20 (~08:1x AM → ~11:4x AM PT) · Claude Code, cloud (Opus 5) — "further the Panini roadmap and feature parity"

**Verdict: the roadmap gate cleared on its own falsifier, and the three things actually blocking Panini were all in artifacts that claimed to be about something else.** Open-ended Panini brief; ended shipping 4 migrations + 3 code changes + 2 new guards, and correcting three circulating claims — one of them my own, mid-session.

### Shipped

- **`lib/collections.ts` — `dbChain: "ethereum"` → `null`** (+ `__tests__/published-flip-cannot-widen-the-site-wide-chain-claim.test.ts`, 2 re-pins). `publishedChainsBadge()` builds the **site-wide** footer/OG provenance claim from the `dbChain` of every `published` collection, so the pending Panini `published` flip moved every page from `BUILT ON FLOW + SOLANA` to **`BUILT ON FLOW + ETHEREUM + SOLANA`** — measured by flipping the flag in a probe. That named the OpenSea bridge plane, which #64 declined and which RPC holds **zero** rows from. Zero user-visible change today (Panini unpublished; its static route dirs mount no `CollectionBanner`).
- **`20260920155903`** — `sync_panini_editions_to_shared` enforces the accuracy gate **in code** and **fails closed**, and stops mapping `nation` into `team_name`. Its staleness gate was a `note` STRING; a live call the day before would have written 1,265 stale editions into the shared catalogue, under a header forbidding exactly that. `nation` holds host cities ("Dallas", "Vancouver"), "FIFA" and `Brazil | Brazil` — it would have minted team pages for a city and for FIFA.
- **`20260920165032` + `20260920170139`** — `check_panini_editions_missing_card_stats()`, a **330-buffer** instrument for R120's class, plus a COMMENT-only correction of my own refuted root cause in it.
- **`20260920175228` + board client + tests** — the squeeze board's ask-only disclosure now shares its KPI's denominator (51.7% published vs **53.8%** true, in the flattering direction).

### The part worth re-reading — three claims that were about the wrong artifact

1. **The 09-19 go-live doc's 🚨 gap 1** said `collection_chains` is "the canonical chain join, so every bridged row would be labelled Ethereum on every surface", and pointed the fix at a migration. **`collection_chains` has ZERO consumers** — 0 of 176 views/matviews, 0 `pg_proc`, 0 code paths. The real Ethereum defect was a hardcoded TS constant, and it fired at the `published` flip (step 3), not the bridge (step 2). ⭐ **I inferred a blast radius from a view's NAME. It has no callers.** ⚠ My first probe used `information_schema.views`, whose `view_definition` is NULL for views you don't own — it returned a clean `[]` having inspected **nothing**.
2. **The 07-19 parity assessment's retraction** — *"Nothing in any RPC research names Sawtooth; that was invented"* — **is itself false.** Two research docs name it, one under *Verified*. The over-broad half propagated into `PROJECT_HEALTH` and then the 09-19 doc restored the original error. ⭐ **Retract the claim, not more than the claim.**
3. **R120's own two proposed fixes were both no-ops**, shown from the code before a concurrent session revised it: the upsert already keys on `external_id`, and there is no `__<span>_<cap>` *edition* convention to repair to — it is the **per-serial** suffix, proven from the site's own payloads in `panini_card_serials.raw`. Implementing both would have rewritten three primary keys to a value derived from a convention that does not exist.

### And one I got wrong myself, mid-session

I attributed R120's root cause to `ingest-panini-runner.mjs:543-545` counting **cards OR serials** as walk success. A concurrent session's measurement refuted it — the cards arrive; the `DO UPDATE`'s PK rewrite is rejected by an FK and the chunk aborts. **I had already shipped the refuted cause into the function's own comment**, which is the one place an operator reads before acting, so a second comment-only migration corrects it and reds if the wrong text survives. ⭐ **A comment on an instrument is not documentation, it is the instrument's answer to "why".**

### Measured, so it is re-derivable

- Step 1's falsifier discharged: **45d-stale 1,265 (24.9%) → 1 (0.0%)**, age p50 276 h → **56.9 h**, walked ≤7 d 32.9% → **64.9%**, same day.
- The probe counterweight, three forms of one question, all cold: `group by` **22,095 buffers / 11.6 s** · bounded `LATERAL` **15,814 / 43.8 s** · read one table **330 / instant**. ⭐ **A per-key probe is not automatically cheaper when the index does not carry the aggregated column** — the inverse of the rule CLAUDE.md carried, now corrected there.
- Disclosure: hc ASK_ONLY is **364 of 4,053 editions (9.0%)** but **53.8%** of the sealed value.

### Open

- **R120 residual — 1 of 3.** The FK fix healed two at `17:15:53.411Z` (30 and 25 serials captured in that batch); **Khuliso Mudau captured ZERO and was not walked at all**, so the FK explanation is excluded for it. Needs the logged-in browser. ⭐ **Three rows sharing a symptom were assumed by three sessions to share a cause; the fix cleared two.**
- **Panini step 1: day 1 of a 7-day hold**, exit ~09-26/27. Bridge still inert (0 rows) by design.
- **Trevor's, not code:** the squeeze board's headline tile (promote sale-backed?), the OG card's population, `panini-ingest` severity `info` → `medium`, and the `published`/`is_active` flips. ⚠ The 07-19 **editorial** objection to bridging — a listing-gated ~35%-trustworthy index becoming a full citizen of shared surfaces — is still open and is **not** addressed by the staleness gate.

## 2026-09-20 (~09:00 AM → ~10:2x AM PT) · Claude Code, Windows box (Opus 5) — "splice the Panini walk report" → R120

**Verdict: the report's headline held, its diagnosis did not, and neither did my first two.** Started as a ledger splice for a cloud pass that could not commit; ended shipping 1 migration + a route honesty fix after the three "stale" editions turned out to be walked every four hours. 4 pushes, CI green, `main` un-redded on the way through.

### Shipped

- **`20260920165800`** — `panini_fmv_snapshots_edition_id_fkey` gains `ON UPDATE CASCADE` (was `confupdtype='a'`). This is the whole defect: the walker's `DO UPDATE` re-asserts `panini_editions.id` (upstream's `sku`) every pass, which for three hand-seeded rows CHANGES the primary key, and the FK rejected it — aborting the entire multi-row editions statement twice per walk for 66 days. **Revert SQL in the ledger entry.**
- **`app/api/cron/panini-ingest/route.ts`** — `ok` is DERIVED from whether the writes landed instead of hardcoded `true`, and every count is paired with its own `_error` field. Editions, serials and the per-sku sale UPDATEs all carried the identical swallow. A third bug fell out: an ERRORED sale update was being counted in `sales_missed`, contradicting its sibling test's own comment.
- **Two tests INVERTED, one control added.** They asserted `p_ok === true` on a rejected upsert and `sales_missed === 1` on an errored update — they pinned the defect. Mutation-controlled both directions (reverted route ⇒ all 3 new arms fail).
- **Three anon-exec markers** on other sessions' migrations that had `main` red on arrival. All `CREATE OR REPLACE` of existing functions ⇒ marker, never a REVOKE; values READ via `has_function_privilege`, not assumed.

### The part worth re-reading — three wrong diagnoses, each from one column

The cloud filing said the three editions were **delisted**. I said they were **never walked**. Then I proposed **re-keying the write on `external_id`** — which is already exactly what the code does. All three were wrong, and all three came from concluding off `last_seen_at` alone, a column that carries **no `col_description`**.

What settled it was the table next door: `panini_card_serials` held **113 rows for those editions captured that same morning**, with `for_sale_count` 8/11/1 and every serial `is_listed`. The discriminator was then exact — **`id = external_id` on exactly 3 rows of 5,074**; the other 5,071 carry upstream's `__<span>_<cap>` suffix.

⭐ **Naming a column's other writers is a JOIN, not a project.** Three sessions' worth of wrong answers would each have cost one query.

### Measured, so it is re-derivable

12 of 459 runs in 24 h wrote `editions: 0` while serials AND fmv landed — **exactly two per walk**, timestamps matching the seed rows' serial `captured_at` to the tenth of a second. Cost **93 edition-walk records/day, ~4.8% of writes**. Repairing the three ids by hand was rejected on evidence: `id` embeds a point-in-time value (span = `min(serial_number)` on only 3,447 of 5,070) and **seven of ten dependent objects read it**.

### Open

The three rows **self-heal on the first walk after 09:58 AM PT** — not yet observed at 10:1x (the 10:00 tick had written only its heartbeat). Exit: `editions_stale_45d` → 0 and `editions: 0`-with-serials runs → 0/day. Register row **R120**.

## 2026-09-20 (~01:03 AM PT) · Cowork cloud (Opus 4.8) · rpc-nightly-autonomous-pass — quiet honest night

## 2026-09-19 (~10:00 AM → ~2:30 PM PT) · Cowork cloud (Opus 5) — "pipeline failures and the alerts sentinel is sending out"

**Verdict: 2 dead ingest lanes revived, 3 sentinel arms added, 1 structural blind spot closed, 1 fix RETRACTED. 8 migrations, ~10 commits, CI green on every code push.** Two of the four headline items ended as corrections rather than ships, and those are the entries worth re-reading.

### Shipped and verified

- **Two pack-sales ingest lanes had been dead 6 and 7 days** behind a terminal `done` latch, with `cron.job_run_details` marking all 478/477 daily dispatches `succeeded` — because `net.http_post` succeeds when the POST is **ENQUEUED**. Unlatched (Top Shot recovered 1,187 sales; All Day held 4 min as a **no-change control** that stayed at 0), then `unlatch_pack_sales_cursors()` + pg_cron jobid 526 self-heals it and writes a `pipeline_runs` row every tick — the first coverage those lanes ever had.
- **The structural blind spot behind it:** 5 of 12 active edge-function cron lanes write no `pipeline_runs` row, so every sentinel pipeline arm was blind to them BY CONSTRUCTION. `edge_lane_watch` + `check_edge_lane_observability()` read `cron.job` directly — **a ban at zero, so a new edge lane is unobserved-by-default and announces itself.**
- **Sentinel arm starvation (R113).** Arm order was fixed, so the wall budget refused the SAME contiguous tail every sweep: `pg_net Dispatch` 25/83 (30.1%), `Ops Probe Cost` 25/83, `Wall Kills` 20/83 — and **every arm above that block 0/83.** Rotated the five rotatable tail arms; `Ops Probe Cost` deliberately stays pinned last and KEEPS its ~30%, because its contract requires lastness and a silently wrong number is worse than a measured blind spot.
- **The row could not reproduce its own verdict.** `isBlind()`'s `didEvaluate` override lived only in memory; re-deriving from stored text scores `Trust Health` blind on 38/83 sweeps and `Pipeline Success Coverage` on 24 — both evaluated fine. `blind`/`refused` are now persisted, **computed from the RAW detail before the clamp.**
- **R110 residual closed** as a DECISION: jobid 26 has no pending work (All Day `pack_rips` is 2,816,781 rows with ZERO null `dist_id`), so an outcome check would be permanently stale. `edge_lane_watch` now carries zero "could not identify" rows.

### 🚨 The retraction, and it is the most useful thing in this entry (R114)

I exempted seven `wallet-backfill*` lanes from `Cadence Collapse` on the evidence that their run count is demand-driven, and **retracted it 40 minutes later.** The evidence came from a 72 h window that sat **entirely after a change point.** The 24-day series shows a dated STEP — `09-13: 433` then a flat `322/273/321/313/312` — from the deliberate 2026-09-13 `SEED_REFRESH_BACKSTOP_FRESH_HOURS` ship. **Demand does not settle onto 313/312/321.**

⭐⭐ Worse: **another session had already diagnosed it and acked the arm to 2026-10-01, and that date is exactly calibrated** — the baseline window is `current_date-17..-3`, so it goes fully post-change on precisely 2026-10-01. **The reading corrects itself on the day the ack expires.** That ack text was sitting in the arm's own `detail` on every sweep and I did not read it before building a fix for what it already covered. Keeping the row would have been worse than doing nothing: suppressing until 12-19 something that resolves on 10-01, and replacing a visible ack with a silent `ok`.

**The mechanism was kept at a population of zero** — reasoned, expiring, fail-loud suppression, pinned with 11 assertions — for the next lane that genuinely needs it.

### Instruments and capabilities learned

- ⭐ **This sandbox CAN provision Postgres and run the DB-invariant suite (194 files) + the migration parse check (1,146 files) locally.** `initdb` refuses to run as root — run it as `postgres`. It caught a pin whose fixtures could not run against a changed body, before CI did.
- ⚠ **Main was RED on code for ~20 min while the CI badge read GREEN**: the breaking run failed, then two DOCS-ONLY commits went green because every code job is gated on `needs.changes.outputs.code == 'true'`. Check the last CODE run, not the last run. Verified "not mine" by reproducing both failures on a clean `origin/main` worktree rather than assuming.
- ⚠ **A background-task notification's "exit code 0" is the WRAPPER's**, not the command's — a run printing `VITEST=1` was reported as exit 0. And do not `| tail -N` a suite you may need to diagnose; it truncated its own failure detail and cost a re-run.

### Still open, stated rather than dropped

- `Detector Health (GitHub Actions)` — **needs Trevor, not code** (operator `*_GATE_KEY` secrets); now acked to 2026-10-03 by another session with that reasoning.
- `Ops Probe Cost` keeps its ~30% refusal rate by design; the lever is a cheaper sweep or a dedicated reserve.
- `daily-portfolio-snapshot` permanently lost 09-12/09-18/09-19 for ~27 users (R109's second victim).

**Verdict: HEALTHY-UNDER-LOAD in a daytime IO spell. 3 migrations + 1 Sentinel ack shipped, 10 commits, CI + Smoke green on every one, nothing reverted.** Security invariants 0 · secdef drift 0 · stalled pipelines 0 · 0 invalid indexes · 150/152 cron active · 63/63 + 8/8 pages pass the rendered-DOM smoke from the sandbox · vitest 1,556 files / 17,507 tests · `tsc` clean. The spell (io_wait 7–20 of 8–23 active all morning) is `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` (jobid 303, #1 reader: 411 MB physical/call, 240 h exec since 08-12) plus the ~500 s market-index MV refresh; every "failing" pipeline this morning failed on `statement timeout` — victims.

### ⭐⭐ R101 v2 / v2b / v2c — the Atlas listing tick, and the lesson that outlives it

The tick (`atlas_listing_verify_tick`, jobid 466) was the largest job on the box by worker time and read **927,297 buffers/call**. The 09-18 v1 found ~160k in the open-book builds and was reverted the same evening on a wall-clock reading; v2 found the other ~700k: **two "differential" `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE … WHERE (row) IS DISTINCT FROM (EXCLUDED row)` arms pushing the whole ~55k-row wanted set through ON CONFLICT to write ~60 — the guard writes only the delta but PROBES every offered row.** Fix: LEFT JOIN the target first (compared as the target's column types), offer only new/changed rows, keep the conflict guard verbatim. Equivalence proven on prod read-only before the apply (55,655 = 55,655, EXCEPT 0; 725 offered instead of 55,655). Then a sized, bounded memory grant after the first reading showed the residual cost was temp-file spills (`temp_buffers 48MB` on the tick, `work_mem` 16/32 MB on the syncs; the last spill EXPLAIN'd to one 23 MB sort).

📏 **Measured on the tick's own pgss counters, pre vs wholly-post windows:** blocks/call **927,297 → 196,978 (−79 %, n=35)**, physical reads/call **26,438 → 17,919 (−32 %)**, temp written/call **3,998 → 0 (n=21)**. Both pre-registered falsifiers cleared by a wide margin.

⛔ **Not scored on completion rate, deliberately** — 9/26 ticks completed in the post window with control 464 at 5/10 and 463 at 25/26: the rate measured the estate. A concurrent Cowork cloud session filed the same conclusion independently the same hour ("the lever is estate IO, not this function"), and its retraction entry and mine are reconciled in the ledger. **Three sessions have now micro-optimised this tick; the next lever is jobid 303, which is FMV-path and Trevor's call.** Promoted: CLAUDE.md (Database traps + Measurement discipline), database.md (full case), register R101 (CLOSED as a per-tick cost item).

6 of its 7 "degraded" lanes were the `wallet-backfill*` family reading the **deliberate 09-13 backstop-drift fix** (~403 redundant re-refreshes/day removed) against a 14-day baseline still mostly pre-fix; the 7th is `ts-listings-atlas-sync`, whose killed ticks write no row (R101). Acked to 2026-10-01 with mechanism and falsifier in `ack_reason`. 10:04 run: WARN, no critical arm (the arm itself read INCONCLUSIVE/db-saturated on that run, so the ack was not yet exercised — 11:04 run the same: INCONCLUSIVE again, ack in the config row, renders on the first conclusive run).

## 2026-09-18 (~18:0x → 19:3x PT) · Claude Code (cloud, Opus 5) — "Do what you can" → "keep going", after the docs-refresh pass in the same thread

**Input:** an audit-drain pass with push confirmed working. **Everything below came from pulling on ONE thread — re-deriving R29 — which unravelled the shape of the whole day's outage.** Seven pushes, all CI-green, all docs-only: no migration, no code, no prod state touched.

### ✅ The owed run-5 security set, completed

Run 5 could not run its DB probes (#122 outage). §10 caught most up at 12:55 PM PT; three were still owed and all three are now done.

- **The anon write-grant SET diffed to membership** — §10's own deferred item. Exactly 5 objects; the row's conclusion HOLDS. Four are bounded INSERT-only. ⚠ **The fifth, `portfolios`, is `cmd ALL` for `{public}` and fails closed BY ACCIDENT** — its policy reads a `wallet` JWT claim nothing sets. 0 rows, 0 repo refs. **Not revoked:** jobid 490 is live in its dependency neighbourhood and CLAUDE.md's revoke rule warns that shape fails as silence.
- **`cron.job.command` credential scan** (the known-blind corpus, read REDACTED) — 13 of 149 jobs, 0 JWT, 0 Bearer. **14 → 13 is the paused/retired delta, not a fix.**
- **PII over anon-readable surfaces** — none reachable; every genuine-PII holder is service-role-only. ⚠ **One anon-reachable path the row never named:** `support_conversations.anon_read_own_support` gates on a **client-supplied `x-session-id` header as a bearer secret**.

### ⭐ Then R29, and the chain it started

- **R29's headline REFUTED.** *"`job startup timeout` is 67–80% of pg_cron failures"* → **31.0%**. `statement timeout` holds the majority at **68.9%**; the two **swapped rank**. The denominator had also doubled (~4,000 → ~9,000 dispatches/day), so the old percentages were wrong twice over. **Found only because I took a positive control on the classifier — enumerating every failure class, not just the named one.**
- **Attribution:** the dominant class is **not fleet-wide**. **Three Atlas lanes are 87.7% of every statement timeout**, one of them **57.5%** alone.
- **R62 RESOLVED** by its own stated exit, read from the real caller: two consecutive sentinel ticks carrying `email`. The flip is datable to inside one hour.

### 🚨 R77 — the finding of the session (P2 → P1)

Measured over the outage window against the **same clock window the previous day**: **116 pipelines lost 4,951 ticks, 106 went completely silent**, and `alert_notifications_sent` holds **ZERO rows for the entire outage**. First alert: **14 minutes after recovery.**

⭐ **And the mechanism is not R77's.** It was filed as a threshold problem (gaps shorter than `max_silent_minutes`). **That is not what happened — the sentinel never executed.** Clean 60.0-minute cadence, then **one 420.0-minute gap, exactly seven hourly slots**, last tick before onset, first after recovery. **The alarm is dispatched over the same outbound path the outage killed.**

### ⭐⭐ The three durable lessons

1. **AN ALARM SHARING ITS SUBJECT'S *FAILURE MODE* IS NO ALARM.** CLAUDE.md already carries this for a shared *scheduler*; R77 is the same rule one level deeper — a shared **dependency** — and it bought a seven-hour blind spot exactly coincident with the worst event in the retained window. **The plane is a post-hoc reporter for this class, not a detector.**
2. **"DID EVERY LANE COME BACK?" HAS A FALSE-POSITIVE MODE.** 105 of 106 resumed; I was one step from filing the exception as an outage casualty. **jobid 453 had dispatched 6/6 successfully** — the lane only logs when it corrects something, so zero corrections reads identically to dead. **The discriminator is `cron.job_run_details`, not the pipeline's self-report.** Both cheap-checks that caught this already existed; I reached for them second.
3. **A POST-OUTAGE WINDOW IS NOT A BASELINE — AND IT CAN BE ANOMALOUSLY *GOOD*.** The Atlas lane read "0 timeouts, 3 s average" during the outage because it was **short-circuiting, not going fast**; then its six post-recovery hours were its **best stretch on record** (4.3% / 21.9 s vs a 7-day 7.2–55.2% / 25–85 s). Either one, used as a baseline, manufactures a result.

### ⓘ A control for another session's fix, and it was borne out

Filed (**not** as a challenge; R101 was theirs, worked <1 h earlier, so the row was **not touched** per the claim convention) that tonight's autovacuum fix had a contaminated baseline and that *"timeouts since apply = 0"* would over-credit it. **Confirmed an hour later: pooled since apply, 16 of 38 = 42.1%, with no phase under 38%.** ⛔ **Cause deliberately NOT asserted** — the window straddles two concurrent changes, the post-revert sample is 7 ticks, and the revert commit itself records an IO spell.

### ⏳ Not done / handed on

- **The sentinel self-gap detector** — the cheap partial for R77 (have the alarm detect its own missed slots on recovery and date the blind window). **Well-specified in R77; a 2,690-line alerting file deserves its own focused session, not a tail-end push.**
- **R77's real remedy** — an alarm whose dispatch path is independent of the estate it watches. **Architecture with a cost: Trevor's.**
- **`portfolios`** — revoke or retire, with jobid 490's role checked in the same migration.
- **A `cron.job` census.** Named for the third time: population 104 → 149 in 16 days, still nothing to diff membership against.
- **#22** and **#55** unchanged, both still Trevor's.

### ✅ Shipped (one ledger entry, docs-only)

- **CLAUDE.md — four stale claims corrected**, each against a live read: the Sentry SDK is out of the tree (not merely "off"); #69's beacon finding was RETRACTED as a crawler and the file still asserted it; the two 2-hourly Routines are 17 days dead, not 8 (full `list_triggers` this pass); and the headline shares were single legs.
- **roadmap-status.md — a new dated block built on a 27-LEG SERIES** from `rpc_trust_health_history` (jobid 488), with the 09-10 block marked `[SUPERSEDED]`. M1 mean 53.1, 23/27 over bar (MET); M2 mean 28.7, 6/27 over bar (NOT MET).
- **go-live-2026-09.md** — M1 and M2 rows restated as series verdicts.
- **known-issues.md — #22's lead rewritten** so the derived index stops reading an open security item as closed; #55 re-verified with both trigger ids and fire timestamps; index regenerated (60 open · 15 partial · 45 closed).
- **claude-md-condensed-originals.md** — all eight displaced fragments, verbatim.

### ⭐ The three durable lessons, promoted rather than left here

1. **READ THE SERIES, NOT THE LEG — and it falsified my own draft.** I wrote the new headline block from one precompute leg plus a hand-derivation and claimed *"M2 is above the bar for the first time."* The 27-leg series says 09-15 12:48 PT hit 31.7 and five other legs cleared 30. ⭐ **The instrument gap is the finding: on All Day a hand-derivation runs 1.7–4.4 points ABOVE the leg. Never mix them in one series; never book a gate off the higher one.** (Now in roadmap-status.md.)
2. **A DECISION'S SAMPLE EXPIRES AS FAST AS A MEASUREMENT'S.** The 09-10 block concluded M2 was liquidity-gated and unmovable by code. It was right — and the 938-sale week it read as "the new level" was a trough; the next week was 5,269. **Re-test a stated exit condition, never re-read it** — CLAUDE.md already carries that rule, and this is its cleanest instance yet.
3. **A BODY DOES NOT GOVERN A HEADLINE, AND GENERATORS READ HEADLINES.** #69 recorded this in June's shape; #22 was the same defect pointed the other way, and it hid an open **security** item behind a ✅ in the at-a-glance table. ⭐ **When a derived row disagrees with an item, the item's opening words are the bug.**

### ⛔ One correction shipped, not a trim

CLAUDE.md said *"Deleting a REMOTE branch 403s from the sandbox — hand the GitHub-UI deletion to Trevor."* **False.** #22 records that a VM device-flow token deleted `claude/todo-implementation-e4tib3` on 09-08, and that **the 403 was the cloud proxy, not GitHub**. ⭐ *"The sandbox cannot do X"* is the claim shape in this file that expires most often — it is worth re-testing on sight.

### ⓘ Method note, because the file is at its ceiling

CLAUDE.md went **39,993 → 40,000 characters** (Node `String.length`, the binding instrument). Every addition was paid for by a displacement chosen by the person adding it, per the file's own rule. **Four of the eight shortenings were legitimate only because the receiving doc ALREADY held the full text — verified by grep before each cut, not assumed.** The other four are verbatim in `claude-md-condensed-originals.md`.

## 2026-09-18 (PT, evening) — wallet packs: "0 sold" was structural, sealed packs now say what they are

Trevor: "my wallet shows 0 sold packs when I've sold hundreds" + sealed TS packs with no name/thumbnail/market data.

- **Root cause of 0 sold, platform-wide:** `pack_purchases.seller_address` is the tx PAYER = Dapper's escrow on 103,396 of 103,398 TS secondary rows. Both wallet RPCs filtered `seller_address = wallet`. Fix: UNION `topshot_pack_sales_history` / `allday_pack_sales_history` (`storefront_address` = seller, every row has `dist_id`) into `get_wallet_pack_history` (v4) and `get_wallet_pack_summary` (v3). Trevor's wallet: **0 → 502 sold** (396 TS $26,252 + 106 AD $2,038), buys 133 → 253 (marketplace rows back to 2023-12).
- **Fabricated zeros removed:** buy/sell/pull value were `COALESCE(.., 0)`; now NULL, primary drops priced at retail with provenance, P&L only when both legs known.
- **Sealed identity:** dist resolves from rip → own rows → ANY marketplace row for the pack; 9 of 94 for this wallet, the other 85 are honestly "not recorded until opened or resold" (no source on the platform has it).
- Five indexes on the two marketplace tables (seller/buyer/pack); seller lookup was a 1.3 s seq scan. Warm RPC: history ~100 ms, summary ~230 ms.
- Settlement lag measured: on-chain trails marketplace by median 4 h, p90 9 days → buy rows within 30 days clustered as one purchase, bought_at = earliest (pinned: `P10` case).
- Worker: TS secondary seller now = same-tx `PackNFT.Withdraw.from` (payer fallback) — committed, **needs `wrangler deploy`**.
- Pinned both RPCs (`supabase/tests/get_wallet_pack_{history,summary}.sql` + PINS). Ran green on a scratch Postgres 16 in the sandbox (`/usr/lib/postgresql/16`, run as the `postgres` user under `/tmp`).

**Second sitting ("Do it. Keep doing all you can"):**
- ⛔ The worker deploy is genuinely Trevor's: no Cloudflare token in the sandbox env, none among the 58 Vercel env keys, no GHA deploys workers.
- ⭐ **Sealed packs ARE nameable.** Introspected Dapper's studio-platform GraphQL from the DB via pg_net: `searchPackNft` returns dist_id / status / current owner / distribution title+image for any pack id (100 per request, ids as strings). Shipped the `pack-nft-identity` pg_cron lane (`20260919021500`), then rewrote its candidate scan into a seeded queue (`20260919031500`) after the FIRST tick measured 27 s per batch scanning all 87k unresolved rows — paused the job by hand meanwhile. Trevor's sealed TS packs: 9 → 93 of 94 named, all with market data. 2 of the 94 were OPENED by another wallet → new status `transferred` (v5 RPC, pinned P11–P13).
- ⭐ Historical `pack_purchases.seller_address` re-attributed from the marketplace tables where exactly one seller matched (pack + buyer, 30-day window): **68,889 rows** (67,215 TS + 1,674 AD), audit table row-for-row (`20260919011500`). The apply_migration client timed out at 60 s while the server finished the transaction — checked `pg_stat_activity` rather than re-running.

**Third sitting ("keep going"):**

Shipped the per-wallet holdings sync (`20260919041500`, RPC v6 with `identity_sync`, route fires it in `after()`, hourly sweep over saved wallets, UI freshness line).

### ✅ Shipped

- **The tree is caught up.** 4 upstream commits pulled; the nightly pass's uncommitted ledger entry re-spliced at the first `^### ` (guards **2107 / 3 / 0**); 5 untracked docs committed; inbox INDEX reconciled **507 → 510** with three hand-written entries, counts re-derived by `scripts/fix-inbox-index-counts.mjs` and never hand-edited.
