- **`lib/api-error.ts`: a Supabase-edge 522 is now a retryable 503, not a permanent 500** (`upstream_unavailable` → 503 + `Retry-After`). Found by the weekly `rpc-surface-qa` handoff. **Verified LIVE in production during the outage** — the confirmation that handoff said would have to wait for a blip.

### ⭐ The measurement — a positive control taken a different way

Three ledger entries flagged the `<!DOCTYPE html>` error bodies as *"the discriminator to chase first"*, and both 09-18 filings stopped at *"cannot discriminate saturation from a connection-layer incident, because `pg_stat_activity` needs the connection that is refused."*

**`/auth/v1/health` and `/auth/v1/settings` are the substitute control, and they need no DB connection.** GoTrue does not read our tables and holds its own pool, so it **cannot** be starved by `fmv-recalc` or a wallet-backfill wave — and it returns Cloudflare **522 after ~19.6 s**, identically to the REST path. Controls both directions: the edge answers `/rest/v1/` **401 in 195–335 ms**, and `api.github.com` → **200** rules out my own network. Reproduced with both the legacy `anon` JWT and the `sb_publishable_…` key. `status.supabase.com` lists **"API Gateway — Degraded Performance"**.

And the HTML is named: **`<title>supabase.co | 522: Connection timed out</title>`** — Cloudflare's own page. ⛔ **A CF 522 means Cloudflare could not OPEN a connection to the origin; query-load exhaustion returns a JSON error from a REACHABLE PostgREST, never a 522.** ⇒ platform-side. **Do not throttle pipelines or re-tune `fmv-recalc` for this event** — the nightly pass's "first suspect" is not supported.

⚠ **What I wrote down rather than buried:** Kong, PostgREST and GoTrue share the instance, so *instance-wide* starvation could produce the same 522 and **my probes alone do not exclude it**. What argues against it is the nightly pass's instrument, not mine — its postgres logs show pg_cron lanes completing normally through 14:30Z with only 2 statement cancels. Two independent instruments, neither sufficient alone.

### ⚠ Four corrections, three of them mine

1. **`git add -A` swept in a concurrent session's handoff file.** It happened to be complete — and it is what surfaced the 522 misclassification defect I then fixed — but it was not mine to commit. Staged explicitly for every commit after that, which is also how `docs/overnight/flow-ecosystem-watch.md` (another session's live edit) was correctly left alone.
2. **The FULL SUITE caught what a targeted run did not.** All **19** files importing the classifier passed; `api-collection-stats` failed. It asserted 500 for a thrown `connect ECONNREFUSED …:5432` on the grounds that *"an unrecognized failure is not assumed retryable"* — right when written, wrong once that string is recognized. ⭐ **INVERTED, not loosened:** both load-bearing properties still asserted (**NOT 200**, the D11 property; **no driver text in the body**), plus a **new control** that a genuinely unrecognized throw is still a non-retryable 500. **A targeted run would have shipped it.**
3. **The INVERSE of the documented curl rule.** CLAUDE.md says *"`curl` fails silently for Vercel REST — always PowerShell"*. Reading an **error BODY** it is the other way round: `Invoke-WebRequest`'s exception path reported the 522 with **`BODY_LEN=0`** while `curl` printed the full Cloudflare page. Promoted to [tooling-gotchas.md](../reference/tooling-gotchas.md). ⛔ **An empty body and a body your HTTP client discarded are the same reading** — the ordinary failed-read-rendered-as-an-answer shape, aimed at my own instruments.
4. **One of my own probes was void:** `/api/public/insights/movers` → 404. **There is no `movers` route** (29 exist; that is not one). A 404 from a route you invented is not a finding. Recorded so nobody chases it.

### ⛔ Attempted and still owed — not quietly dropped

- **The `pg_stat_activity` positive control was RE-ATTEMPTED at 09:2x PT and still refused.** Owed to the first session that gets a connection, along with the self-clear window and the pinning reader. The incident was live for the whole session (**522 / 500 from 08:04 → 09:2x PT**, ~3.5 h).
- **Inbox archival is NOT mine** — `INDEX.md` is explicit that archiving is Trevor's call, not a chore, because the night pass DRAINS that directory.
- ⚠ **The 09-18 handoff's closing line asks for a "CLAUDE.md 'Recent sessions' line", which is a RETIRED RULE** — `scripts/check-retired-rules.mjs` bans that exact phrasing, because CLAUDE.md sits tens of characters under a hard 40,000-char ceiling and appending there can silently destroy the project's memory. The correct target is this file. Recorded because the handoff will be read again.

### ➕ Continued to ~10:0x PT — four more, after the tree was already clean

- **`lib/api-error.ts` 522 → retryable 503, VERIFIED LIVE.** Found by the weekly `rpc-surface-qa` handoff *during* the outage. The outage then supplied the end-to-end confirmation the handoff said would have to wait: cache-busted, `x-vercel-cache: MISS`, `/insights/squeeze` → **503 `upstream_unavailable`** where it had been a 500 with `retryable:false`, and `/insights/pack-reality` still **503 `timeout`** as a live no-change control. ⚠ **The FULL SUITE caught a regression a targeted run missed** — all 19 files importing the classifier passed while `api-collection-stats` failed. **INVERTED, not loosened:** both load-bearing properties kept (NOT 200, no driver leak) plus a new control that a genuinely unrecognized throw is still a non-retryable 500. Later pinned **across the seam** (`boundedRead` → `safeApiError`), because that composition has broken before in exactly this way and per-module tests both pass while the pair is broken.
- **The skill packer no longer needs `zip`.** It shelled out to a binary Git for Windows does not ship, so a red bundle guard could not be repaired from this box — *"the guard works, the documented repair does not."* New `scripts/lib/zip-one-file.mjs`, shared by the packer and the guard's fixture builder. ⭐ **It closed a gap the test file itself had flagged**: 5 of 9 arms were skipped here with the note *"an environment gap on this machine, not a passing guard."* **Now 9/9**, and the suite went 17,365 + 5 skipped → **17,373 + 0 skipped**. Controls both ways: guard shown **RED** on drift, **GREEN 10/10** after repack; determinism proven by two packs giving identical md5. That unblocked the #114 bullet telling Cowork the live estate is **15 artifacts, not five** — re-derived today, unchanged, audit exit 1.
- **⛔ Refuted the wallet-search "windowing" fix, against deployed mainnet.** The 09-14 filing's §5 is headed *"The fix already exists in this codebase."* It does not: `wallet-search`'s helpers do exactly one thing — `return col!.getIDs()` — and the "windowed variant" they'd point at **still calls `ref!.getIDs()` in full** and only then slices. **A fix copied from it would have shipped, changed nothing, and looked like it should have worked.** All three accessors the deployed `TopShotShardedCollection` exposes fail with 1110, `collections` is `access(contract)` so no shard is reachable, and `forEachID`'s early-exit stops only the current shard — a request for the first 5 ids returned **754**. 🚨 **And the "0.13 % over" figure was never a measurement:** `used:` in a 1110 error is the **abort point, not the requirement** (today: 100,001 for `getIDs()`, 100,002 for `forEachID` — both just the ceiling). ✅ Positive control relocates the cause to **sharding, not size**: the same script on the largest non-sharded wallet returns **38,642** ids fine.
- **📏 #122's owed baseline, taken (it needs no DB — which is why that entry named it first).** `get_runtime_errors`, both windows relative: **7d 21,485 / 24h 13,038**, so today holds **61 %** of the week. ✅ **Shapes are chronic exactly as #122 said** — only **153** of the 7-day events are in groups first seen today. 🚨 **Volume is ~9× elevated** — the part #122 could not yet claim. ⭐ **Then I checked my own denominator:** the top-50 includes `DEP0169`, a WARNING not an error (7d 1,781 / 24h 92), and stripping it moves it to **~11.5×** — the noise was *dampening* the signal. ⚠ Both windows returned exactly **50** groups, which is the CAP, so these are top-50 totals and the multiple is directional only.
- **⛔⛔ AND I WITHDREW MY OWN RECOMMENDATION AN HOUR AFTER PUBLISHING IT — the most useful thing in the stretch.** Off that ranking I wrote that `[pack-detail]` (13 groups, **40.6 %** of all volume) meant *"consolidating that fan-out is the single largest lever."* **`lib/pack-dist/fetchers.ts` already carried the refutation and I had not read it:** measured 09-03, `get_pack_lifecycle` runs **5–32 ms warm against a 5,000 ms budget**, so an overrun is **instance IO contention (R46, capacity decision already taken)**, not the queries — the file says ⛔ *"DO NOT TUNE THESE RPCs."* ⭐ **And the 08-23 onset is the INSTRUMENT being installed, not a regression:** `e2538adc7` bounded the thirteen *bare* reads on **08-22**, and before that they **hung instead of erroring**, so the slowness was invisible. The bound then cut affected users **7–20×**. 🚨 **So the 40.6 % measures a page that was MADE HONEST, not one that is broken.** ⭐ **The reusable lesson: ranking by error COUNT rewards the surfaces that REPORT, so the top of that list is partly a map of where instrumentation exists** — I recommended an action off a ranking without reading the measurement sitting in the file I was ranking.
- **The outage ran the whole session** (522 / 500 from 08:04 → 10:0x PT, ~4 h+). 🚨 **Supabase has posted NO incident for the symptom** — read live from `history.atom` — so the platform may not know, which makes reporting it the action. ⚠ One correlation recorded as a **lead, not a cause**: that status page says fleet deployment changes begin *"Friday, September 18"*, and today **is** Friday (verified against the clock). The `pg_stat_activity` control was re-attempted three times and refused every time; it stays owed.

⚠ **Two things I checked and did NOT file, because they were clean** — `rpcWithRetry` already treats a 522 as transient, `warmBoard` only writes a snapshot on success (which is *why* the honesty layer held today), the new signature route's `.ilike` is safe because `normalizeFlowAddress` is `/^[0-9a-f]{16}$/`, and `market-pulse` renders a blank grid rather than a false claim. **A negative result is a result; manufacturing a finding is the failure mode.**

### 🚨 The outage RESOLVED, and what it actually was

**#122 ran 05:19:30 → ~12:00 PT (~6 h 40 m by the earliest marker).** It ended on an **operator restart** (Trevor-approved, 11:58:48), not a self-heal — though log lines resumed unaided at 11:42.

⭐⭐ **POSTGRES WAS NEVER DOWN, and this is the measurement that settles it.** In the exact window its log stream shipped **zero** lines (05:19:30 → 11:42), the database wrote **938 `pipeline_runs` rows across 50 distinct lanes**, continuously, to the minute logs resumed. **The "silence" was log SHIPPING — an outbound network operation.** 🚨 **Mechanism, measured:** every failing outbound `pg_net` request carried `Timeout of 20000 ms … (DNS time: 20021.669 ms, TCP/SSL handshake: 0.000000 ms, HTTP: 0.000000 ms)` — **all 20 s in name resolution, zero handshake. The request never opened a socket.** ✅ **Control that excludes the chronic Atlas-WAF class:** 30 h hourly on both Atlas lanes — **0 failures/hour for 23 h, then 100 % for six.** A 0→100 % step, not an elevated rate. 📏 **And it was PROGRESSIVE:** log shipping 05:19:30 → inbound errors 05:48 → outbound pg_net 06:05, ~45 min from first symptom to full outbound failure.

### ⛔ Three of my own mechanism claims were wrong, in order

1. *"A CF 522 means Cloudflare could not OPEN a connection to the origin"* — refuted by a concurrent session's **Storage `544` in 5.1 s** through the same hostname.
2. *"`/auth/v1/*` excludes our query load"* — weakened: GoTrue reads `auth.*` in the **same Postgres**.
3. *"The instance lost DNS"* as the whole story — narrowed: **outbound** DNS is what is measured.

⭐⭐ **The pattern is worth more than any of the three: ON AN INCIDENT, THE NEXT PROBE BEATS THE BETTER ARGUMENT.** A service answering with its OWN error outranks four that time out identically.

⚠ **Two traps recorded from my own errors:** *"the last line before the silence"* (a 267 s checkpoint) is the last line **SHIPPED**, not the last event — reading it causally assumes the shipping path was healthy to that instant, **which is what failed**. And an incident has **several onset markers**: I called a missed tick "before the onset" by anchoring to the most VISIBLE marker (12:48Z errors) instead of the EARLIEST (12:19:30Z log silence), **manufacturing a discrepancy that dissolved against the right one**.

⚠ **A positive control has a SHELF LIFE.** `pg_stat_activity` was specified to run DURING the event; taken after recovery it describes the recovered box and **discriminates nothing**. Recorded as a loss, not a pass.

### ✅ Shipped (7 code changes)

`lib/api-error.ts` 522 → retryable 503 (**verified live in production during the outage**) · the `zip`-free skill packer, closing a 5-of-9 skipped-arm gap · `boundedRead → safeApiError` seam tests · the sentinel gateway-blindness fix **plus deletion of a byte-identical duplicate classifier** · `scripts/git-unstick-index-lock.mjs` (the lock hit FIVE times today; it cleared a real one 26 s old) · **the P0 fabricated zeros, per-VALUE, on 2 of 9 boards**.

⭐ **The P0's diagnosis in one line: one of the four KPIs in the SAME STRIP was already honest** (`TOP SALE —` beside `COMBINED $0.00`), so it is a copy-paste defect and the fix is per-VALUE — one granularity finer than *"fix per PANEL, not per page"*.

⛔⛔ **And the fail-open I nearly shipped:** I copied the transport-token list from `api-error.ts` into the sentinel's classifier and **downgraded a genuine page to a warn**. A test caught it — its "hard sniper-feed error" arm uses `ECONNREFUSED` deliberately. ⭐ **Same strings, opposite verdicts: one asks "should the CLIENT RETRY", the other "should we PAGE". Do not unify them.**

### ⏳ Left open, deliberately

**7 of 9 boards** still fabricate (`offer-spread`, `candy-mlb`, `panini-squeeze`, `deals`, `rookie-board`, `serial-premiums`, `cross-collection`) — ⚠ **and a sweep for `initialFailed` finds the wrong set**, because provenance comes in two shapes. **R96** (`/api/allday-pack-ev` ungated service-role writes, with a forward that breaks if you gate only the child). The **Sentry bundle removal** and **IPFS optimizer routing**, both deliberately unshipped mid-incident. The deep audit's register rows are claimed against a **stale `origin/main`**, so its rule-11 re-check is owed. **Falsifier at 14:35 PT:** five 2–3-hourly lanes had not yet had a post-recovery tick when I looked — the one HOURLY lane resumed at 12:53, so the zero proves nothing yet.

### 🧾 Deploy

`dpl_24f5VuHkxCWjKtrqDL8SZKD9aTcD` **READY** — 245 s build, `ready` > `buildingAt`, `lambdaRuntimeStats {"nodejs":19}`, apex aliases attached, `aliasError: null`. ⭐ **The build is a second measurement:** it prerenders `/insights` pages that read the DB and **succeeded while the DB was unreachable** — evidence `insights-server-pages-bound-their-reads` is doing its job. ⚠ Honest consequence of deploying mid-outage: those pages baked the *degraded* state — honest ("PARTIAL DATA", no fabricated zeros) but stale, self-healing on the next ISR revalidation.

Live verification, cache-busted, `x-vercel-cache: MISS` on both: `/api/public/insights/squeeze` → **503 / `{"code":"upstream_unavailable","retryable":true}`** (a 500 with `retryable:false` before the deploy) · `/api/public/insights/pack-reality` → **503 / `{"code":"timeout"}`**, the live **no-change control** proving the new code did not swallow everything into one bucket.

Pointed at our own domain it settled the item in one line, and turned up the real defect one level up (#106). **Before writing "needs egress I do not have", ask whether the instrument you just used on someone else's host works on ours.** Promoted with three caveats that can each make it lie: a binary body is lossy in a text column, our own `Cache-Control` reads back as a bare `public` (Vercel consumes the CDN directives), and the request leaves Supabase's IP, not Vercel's.

**Shipped — a live user-facing defect found by following that thread onto PAGES** (`121b17b2a`). All 518 UFC editions store an `ipfs.io` url (5 of 6 sampled **timed out at 15,001 ms**; a separate probe got **429 with a gateway RETIREMENT notice**), and 2,368 pre-2022 Top Shot editions store an `ipfs.dapperlabs.com` one (**10 of 10 alive** — that half is fine *today*). `proxyIpfsUrl` already existed and was **applied in 9 places and skipped in 13**. Proven rather than reasoned: `/api/search?q=adesanya` returns **3 raw ipfs.io urls and 0 proxied**. Fixed all 13 — two inside the shared helpers (`tsTileImg`, `resizedThumb`) — plus a tree-walk ban-at-zero guard.

**Fixed someone else's red, and did not touch production to do it.** A concurrent session's migration created `public.audit_20260913_suppression_stale_net_claims_backup` with **no RLS and `anon` holding SELECT**. Established as inherited before acting (**the same single assertion, 1 failed / 8,421 passed, on my commit and on the base**), then fixed the repo half only: the `audit_` prefix is covered by `selfheal_audit_table_rls()` (pg_cron 232, `47 * * * *`), which closed it at **9:47:00 am PT — verified by re-test, not by re-reading the schedule** — where an `apply_migration` would have cost a user-facing `PGRST002` burst.

**⭐ Four lessons promoted, each because it cost something here:**
- ⛔ **A 200 on a card route proves only that SOMETHING rendered.** `ufc-strike` is not a url slug (it is `ufc`), so one of my own probes measured the guard card. Discriminate on payload size against an art-less control on the same route. → [tooling-gotchas.md](../reference/tooling-gotchas.md)
- ⛔ **"The tip went green" can be evidence of nothing** — a docs-only push SKIPS both unit shards, TypeScript, the ratchet and the DB invariants. Read the job list. → [testing-and-ci.md](../reference/testing-and-ci.md)
- ⛔ **A guard satisfied by its OWN COMMENT.** `toContain("proxyIpfsUrl")` over raw source survived a mutation deleting the real call, because the comment above it said the word. Strip comments; match the open-paren. → [testing-and-ci.md](../reference/testing-and-ci.md)
- ⛔ **A centred flex line with `overflow: hidden` eats BOTH ENDS** — the first render of the new name strip drew a 44-char UFC title with `"Israel "` gone from the FRONT. Only a rendered PNG can tell a tail clip from a both-ends clip. → [key-files-and-honesty.md](../reference/key-files-and-honesty.md)

**Verified in production across the population, not just asserted:** the player card carries art on **4 of 5 collections** (Top Shot 417,380 · Pinnacle 234,300 · Golazos 226,772 · All Day 216,672 chars against a 65,351-char art-less control) — ⭐ Pinnacle's first production confirmation since the 09-12 site-relative fix. **UFC is the measured exception:** a cold fetch of its art **times out at 4,500.8 ms against the card's 4,500 ms budget**, while the same url over a Top Shot CID returns 200 on 2 of 2 cold fetches inside it. ⛔ So the fallback walk **cannot** rescue UFC — one budget, one shared slow path — and `artFailed` + the 60 s cache make that honest, not fixed.

**Open, stated rather than dropped:** the 2–8 MB IPFS masters painting into 28px tiles ([inbox 2026-09-13T1708Z](../overnight/inbox/2026-09-13T1708Z-the-ipfs-served-art-is-2-to-8-mb-of-png-going-into-tiles-as-small-as-28px.md)) — **filed, not shipped, because the deciding number is unmeasured**: how often those tiles actually render, and whether optimizer spend beats the bandwidth it saves. One Vercel dashboard read settles it. ⛔ **Nothing was added to CLAUDE.md — it is at 39,997 of 40,000 characters** (re-measured after a concurrent session edited it mid-turn; my first reading of 39,991 was already stale), so every lesson above went to a reference doc. ⚠ **Three characters of headroom means the next rule to land there must DISPLACE one, not spend room.**

## 2026-09-13 (10:0x–10:4x PT) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — "don't stop until you've exhausted your capabilities": the hour I spent proving my own morning fix was aimed at the wrong problem

⛔⛔ **I was wrong twice about one lane, and the second time I had already read the evidence that should have stopped me.** This morning I shipped `rpc-ccm-step2-retry` on the reading that the 09-12 timeout was *"a fat tail against its own ceiling — a spell, not steady growth"*, taken from the job's own 14-run duration history (median ~25 s against a 300 s cap). Asked to leave nothing unresolved, I stopped inferring and took a **controlled run**: a pg_cron one-shot with a real 300 s budget, on an instance recorded CALM at the time (`pg_stat_activity` io_wait 4 / active 6 / 38 conns). It **failed at exactly 300.0 s.** The lane cannot finish any more.

⭐⭐ **The transferable lesson is why the history could never have told me: every duration in it is a SURVIVOR.** Runs that outgrow the cap are recorded as failures *at the cap*, not at their true cost, so the distribution is censored at precisely the value in question — and "unlucky nights" and "the lane has died" fit it equally well. **Only a run, at the real budget, on a measured-calm instance, separates them.** ⭐ **And read such a run for more than pass/fail: hitting 300.0 s rather than 120.0 s was the positive control that the in-command `SET statement_timeout = '300s'` I added an hour earlier DOES bind — so one run refuted my diagnosis and vindicated an unrelated fix simultaneously. Two correct conclusions can still leave you solving the wrong problem.**

**Then I turned off my own morning's work.** Gated on staleness that is now permanent, the retry would have fired daily, burned 300 s of guaranteed-failing IO, and changed nothing — on the instance whose saturation is a go-live blocker. ⭐ **A retry is only as good as the premise that the work CAN succeed.** `cron.alter_job(491, active := false)` rather than `unschedule`, so the jobid and its (correct) command survive; the PRIMARY stays **active on purpose**, because it is the only thing that would record a recovery and disabling it removes the signal along with the waste.

**Cost located without ever completing the query** — catalogue reads only: `wallet_moments_cache` is 77.9 % all-visible (`relpages` 120,286 / `relallvisible` 93,742) so the Index Only Scan falls back to heap fetches on ~26,500 pages; **19 indexes, 1,567 MB against a 940 MB heap**; and `idx_wmc_wallet_coll_ek_fmv` leads on `wallet_address` while the only predicate is `collection_id`. ⭐ **The cheap corroboration that located it: a bare `count(*)` on the scan's input also blew past 120 s — so the aggregate was never the problem.** Filed as **#108** with every number; the repair (leading/covering index, rewritten aggregate, or a visibility-map strategy) is real work on the platform's hottest cache table and is NOT started.

⚠ **Left honestly unresolved:** `cross_collection_ts_set_overlap_mat` is frozen at ~42 h and climbing. ⭐ **The board is honest about it** — `CrossCollectionBoardClient` renders the overlap table's own `computed_at` (the 2026-08-25 fix for this exact drift), so users see the age rather than a silent stale number, and `cohort_stats` is unaffected. **Also cleaned up:** the diagnostic one-shot was unscheduled by hand — a FAILED one-shot does not self-clean, which is the trap its own recipe warns about. Docs: three new `cron-and-schedulers.md` sections + the size-function trap in `database.md`; **two memory entries I had written hours earlier were CORRECTED rather than added to, because a wrong memory is worse than none.**

The band was never the variable — the job's fat tail against its own 300 s ceiling was (median ~25 s; two of the last four runs used 55 % and 100 %). ⭐ **And the in-repo precedent could not be copied blindly:** today's `portfolio_snapshots` retry is unconditional, but step2 TRUNCATEs a table read by a public crawlable board whose ACCESS EXCLUSIVE window was deliberately shrunk on 08-21 and is pinned by a test — so the retry had to be staleness-GATED, taking the lock on the ~7 % of days the primary fails rather than every day. Shipped `rpc-ccm-step2-retry` (jobid 491, `37 4 * * *`) with controls in both directions (6 cases, including the empty-mat `COALESCE(…, '-infinity')` honesty guard). ⚠ **Did NOT force a recovery run** — the instance was spelling throughout (io_wait 10→20→17), and the surface renders the overlap table's own stamp honestly meanwhile.

**Eighth pass (4:3x → 6:2x PT), Trevor: "keep working on what you can while we wait" / "anything else?".** Verification and the cleanups the measurements licensed. The pair-based figure read **4.8 h** at the 4:44 PM tick and Portfolio Cache Drain cleared at 5:04 PM. ⭐ **The superseded `idx_wmc_wallet_coll_ek_fmv` took ZERO scans in the 40 min after its tier-covering successor went valid (the successor took 58k)**, so it was dropped CONCURRENTLY by a one-off pg_cron job in 0 s — 281 MB and one index's write amplification gone from the table every FMV refresh updates. A CI red on the pair push was a RACE in a dashboard component test (a snapshot count of stats fetches taken before a second refresh could land); both assertions now bound stats calls by the number of refreshes, which still fails the per-row fetch they guard. Two arms traced to their writers rather than re-filed: the trust precompute's 17 h age was ONE row, the impossible-parallel leg whose 5:48 AM and 11:48 AM runs died at 600 s in the spells — it ran 80 s at 5:48 PM and the arm cleared; `wallet-backfill at 39 %` was a 12-h window over the saturated morning against a 14-day baseline of 375–624 runs/day, and today reads 433 with six hours left. ⭐ **Ran the nightly big-wallet lane's exact command once ahead of schedule** (one-off, 1 s, `max_moments 100000`, nothing skipped) so 3:51 AM is a repeat, and found while doing it that `wallet-backfill-multicollection` had already re-aggregated both whales at 5:47 and 6:01 PM — it calls `aggregate_saved_wallet_stats` for every wallet it touches, so "stale indefinitely" in #111 was too strong and is corrected: the hourly sweep's skip only matters for a wallet the backfill lane misses, and the nightly lane bounds that at 24 h. Also measured and recorded, not acted on: `/api/market` 15/15 ok on the quiet instance (the residual was the spell), the wallet FMV refresh's 1 s / ~100 s alternating tick shape (#36), and #52's remaining population (12 functions, one hot).

**Ninth pass (6:2x → 6:4x PT), Trevor: "keep going".** ⭐ **Caught the workload-only saturation the daytime monitor asked to be re-measured, live and with a name.** At 6:27 PM, no maintenance op running, 13 active backends and 13 in IO wait — every one PostgREST, three concurrent `backfill_wmc_metadata_from_editions` at 21 s, three `refresh_seeded_wallet_stats`, one `upsert_wmc_batch`. Sized from `pg_stat_statements` over 33 days the trio costs **9.50 h/day of execution against `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed`'s 6.62** while reading **less than half** as much from disk — so the register's documented "#1 reader" and the instance's #1 *time* consumer are different lanes, and only one had ever been named. Filed as **#113** with the callers enumerated (one genuinely passes a NULL wallet, so #52's guard cannot simply be deleted), the suspect stated as a hypothesis, and the deciding measurement written down for a quiet window rather than run against a saturated instance. Re-read four minutes later: zero active, zero in IO wait — **it is a burst, not a level**, which makes fan-out as much the lever as any query. ⛔ **Then I corrupted the register and pushed it.** A `rep()` whose replacement was a STRING contained `` `^[0-9]+:[0-9]+$` `` — the trailing `$` plus the closing backtick is the `` $` `` token, "everything before the match" — and injected 950 KB of the file into itself: 1,782 → 3,526 lines, every item duplicated. CI run 5460 caught it; repaired 4 minutes later with a function replacer. **Two of this repo's own rules, both of which I had read today:** the `$`-token trap (five prior instances, mine the sixth and the first to reach `main`, with a NEW trigger — ordinary markdown prose, not SQL pins) and the 09-04 gate rule (my chain *printed* `EXIT=1` and pushed anyway because the `&&` hung off `git add`). ⭐ **The thing that found it is the lesson worth keeping: every cheap check passed** — the pre-replace occurrence assertions run *before* the replace, and the guard's own "byte-identical to a fresh regeneration" test passed because the generator rewrote the first index block and the injected copy matched it. **Only the guard's POSITIVE CONTROL failed.** A tamper test nobody would miss if it were deleted is the sole reason a doubled register was found in minutes.

Measured directly: **8,408 / 2,361**. ⭐ **The subtotal (10,769) and grand total were right the whole time and the errors CANCEL — which is exactly why nothing reddened. A total that agrees with itself is not evidence that its parts do.** (Sibling of this repo's existing *"diff the SET, not the count"*; recorded in #95 rather than promoted, because CLAUDE.md already carries the parent rule and has **36 characters** of headroom.)

The cost is the revert path: a reader reverts one docs commit and leaves the code commit live. **Rule: before ending a turn, re-read the `Shipped:` line of every entry you wrote this session against the actual commit range, and correct it IN PLACE** so the heading-count guard does not report growth for a correction.

**Gates:** `tsc` clean · 89 tests green across the sitemap + doc suites · index regenerated (95 items) · `check-memory-doc-links` 197 · ledger guards **3 / 0**, headings **1919 unchanged** across both in-place corrections. **Revert:** each commit independently, none with a DB half.
## 2026-09-12 (late PT, 22:5x) → 2026-09-13 (00:5x) · Claude Code (cloud) — a stale Telegram alert, the gate it earned, and the column contract that made my own justification false

**Input:** Trevor, with a screenshot: *"This telegram alert seems stale? I bought this moment like yesterday, and floor is above $1 now."* Then *"keep working on anything still unresolved and exhaust all you can do."* **Two pushes:** `66323e035` (the alert gate + migration `20260913061500`) and this one (the correction, the tooltip fix, migration `20260913073000`, and the documentation).

**⭐ THE REPORT WAS RIGHT AND THE MESSAGE WAS HONEST, WHICH IS WHAT MADE IT INTERESTING.** `alert_deliveries` showed the SAME row built on 09-09, 09-10, 09-11, 09-12 and 09-13 — the last three off an `ask_updated_at` frozen at 09-10 04:48Z (21.6 h → 45.7 h → **68.5 h**) — while the live row read `low_ask 1.03, low_ask_serial 8442`. The alert even rendered *"ask seen 3d ago — may be gone"*. **An honest caption does not save a notification**, because a notification is consumed as an instruction to act. The boards report; the alert may not send it.

**⭐ ONE GATE FIXED BOTH HALVES.** `dedup_bucket` is `to_char(now(),'YYYY-MM-DD')`, so **any** row that stays in the pool re-fires nightly for ever — and the rows that do that are exactly the ones nobody re-confirms. Gating on the stamp removed the only rows capable of repeating, so no notification-frequency behaviour had to be renegotiated. The 09-09 alert (ask six minutes old) still fires.

**🚨 THE TRAP THAT WOULD HAVE MADE IT WORSE.** One recency predicate over the whole pool would have deleted **1,937 of 1,956 correct All Day rows** — that arm's stamp is the SELLER's posting time over an index a row LEAVES when the listing closes. So the `CASE` lists the EXEMPT arms and gates everything else, which also makes an unknown slug, the hyphen convention and a NULL stamp fail **CLOSED**. Written the obvious way round, a typo would have disabled the gate silently.

**⛔ AND THEN THE JUSTIFICATION TURNED OUT TO BE FALSE — found by reading the writer while doing something else entirely.** `sync_edition_offers_from_atlas()` upserts under `WHERE low_ask IS DISTINCT FROM EXCLUDED.low_ask`, so `edition_offers.updated_at` is bumped **only when the floor CHANGES**; `raise_edition_offers_from_chain()` bumps it when the **offer** rises, which is not the ask at all. It was a true confirmation stamp until `offers-sweep` died on 2026-08-28 (#81). ⭐ **A column's contract lives in its WRITER, so replacing a writer silently REDEFINES the column — and no guard fires, because the NAME is unchanged.** `col_description()` on it was EMPTY, which is why three separate pieces of work read the name and believed it. **The gate's behaviour stands** (on Top Shot it means "the floor changed inside 12 h", which still removes the defect and still fires on discoveries, since a new cheap listing bumps the stamp when we learn of it); **the stated reason did not**, and it was corrected in prod and in every header the same night rather than deleted. Register #98; contract table in database.md.

**⚠ THE SAME MISREADING WAS ALREADY LIVE ON THREE PUBLIC SURFACES.** The ask-age tooltip said *"We last confirmed this ask Nh ago; normally every edition is re-checked about hourly"* — both halves false for Top Shot since 08-28. `askAgeTitle` now takes a **required** `AskStampKind`, so the compiler named every call site instead of letting one keep the old promise, and `askStampKind(slug)` resolves it per arm (`changed` / `checked` / `listed`, unknown → the weakest). The deals board's **hardcoded duplicate** of the sentence is gone. ⭐ **A test now BANS any re-check cadence claim in any variant: a cadence promise is a claim about a LANE, and a lane can die — which is exactly how that string went false with nobody editing it.**

**⚠ A CONTROL I RAN THAT SETTLES NOTHING, recorded so it is not re-run as if it did.** "Does an open listing exist in `topshot_atlas_market_events`" reads 57 of 3,092 blocked rows and 211 of 769 passed rows as live — but that table covers **2,794 distinct editions in 24 h against 12,940 rows with an ask (~22%)**, so it measures firehose COVERAGE, not liveness. *A control whose population does not cover the set is not a control.*

**✅ GATES, and one mutation that survived first.** 189/189 DB-invariant files on a local PG16 provisioned from the sandbox's own binaries; 13 mutations run, 13 killed — **the first run of the serial-pass mutation SURVIVED because this pin file had no serial-board rows at all**, so the gate sat over an empty population and read as covered. The fix was the FIXTURE. `tsc` clean, lint ratchet 715 = baseline, ledger guards 3 / 0, CI green except the five pre-existing failures.

**⚠ `main` WAS ALREADY RED, AND I MEASURED WHOSE.** Five failures in three files. A worktree at the parent commit `bf02f28a2` reproduces all five, and a set-diff of the heartbeat ratchet's offender list against `38abae0df` (where the budget was last set) names the 36th entry exactly: **`app/api/sentinel/route.ts`** — the same file holding 2 of the 3 unbounded fetches in the sibling ratchet. That upgrades the concurrent session's "the two ratchets look like the new sentinel code" from a guess to a measurement. **Left untouched**: it is inside the 24–48 h window, its owner is active, and both fixes need timing bands that session measured.

## 2026-09-12 (evening PT, 19:0x–21:4x) · Claude Code (cloud) — Cowork (g): every Ultimate published a blank card, one collector's whole case was a blank slab, and a `CREATE OR REPLACE` that nearly ate another session's guard

**Input:** Cowork handoff **2026-09-12 (g)** ("the 4 MB cap strips art from 100% of Ultimates and 0% of Commons"), then Trevor: *"Do what you can"* on its remaining items. **Three pushes:** `0f9196dac` (the optimizer leg), `6d0d94a14` (trophy art + caption geometry), and this documentation commit. Both feature deploys verified READY by `ready` diverging from `buildingAt`, `lambdaRuntimeStats` present and apex aliases attached. CI green 19/19 on each.

**⭐ ITEM 1 — THE CAP WAS A VALUE FILTER.** `ogImageDataUri`'s 4 MB cap drops oversized art to the placeholder, and Dapper's artwork gets richer as the tier rises, so the cap removed art from **exactly the Moments people share**: ULTIMATE 6/6 over (median 6.73 MB) against COMMON 0/6 (median 3.21 MB). Art that no origin will size for us now routes through our own `/_next/image?url=…&w=640&q=75`, with the direct fetch kept as a fallback on ONE shared 4.5 s budget — so the leg can add art and never remove it.

⛔ **Three properties were re-derived from the INSTALLED Next (16.2.9) rather than taken from the brief, and one of them is a landmine.** `images.formats` defaults to `["image/webp"]` and `getSupportedMimeType` negotiates a format **only when `accept.includes(it)`** — `image/*` does not contain that literal, so the optimizer converts nothing and a PNG comes back PNG. **The pre-existing `Accept: image/*` header is therefore load-bearing: naming webp or avif in it would blank every card.** Likewise `qualities` defaults to `[75]` (anything else is a 400) and `w` must be in `deviceSizes ∪ imageSizes`. All three are pinned by importing `imageConfigDefault` into the test, so a Next bump reds the suite instead of silently 400ing back to blank cards.

⚠ **A `width=` param is not a test of whether art is sized** — `hiResThumb()` appends one to every `assets.nbatopshot.com` url including the 2880px statics, where the origin ignores it. Keying on the param alone would have re-opened the defect on both trophy cards, the exact surface it was filed from. The discriminator is a size param **on an extensionless render endpoint**.

**⭐ ITEM 3 — THE WRITER HAS A NAME, AND IT IS A MISSING COALESCE.** `get_trophy_slab_data` resolves every display field as `COALESCE(editions.<live>, trophy_moments.<snapshot>)` — **except `thumbnail_url`, which had no live side at all.** So `blaise_27`'s one stored junk URL (a Top Shot static filename cut off at `capture_` with `/image` glued on) was never repaired by anything, and they have exactly one pinned Moment, so their whole case published as a blank slab. `lib/profile/trophy-thumbnail.ts` had already RECORDED that the field was not coalesced while its own docstring promised a rejected pin "simply falls back" — **a comment that names a gap is not a guard against it.**

⛔ **The consistent-looking fix was a regression, and only measurement showed it.** Live-first COALESCE was checked over all 22 trophy rows: 8 differ from their edition, and **7 of those store ~31 KB per-serial derivatives against 4–7 MB masters.** Shipped as `COALESCE(tm.thumbnail_url, e.thumbnail_url)` — a fallback, not a preference. Both directions pinned; each control fails the OTHER spelling.

**🚨 THE NEAR-MISS WORTH MORE THAN ANY OF THE SHIPPED WORK.** The function was dumped, the migration written on it, and the pin file rewritten to match. Forty minutes later, immediately before applying, the live definition was re-read: **3,077 characters had become 3,873** — a concurrent session had applied the impossible-serial guard to the same function. **Applying the draft would have silently reverted it: no diff (a `CREATE OR REPLACE` is a full-body write), no error, and no failing test, because the same change rewrites the pin that would have disagreed.** Rebased onto their definition and re-derived everything. The fixture collision was the same lesson in miniature — the new slab was named `mC`, `mC` was already theirs pointed at a different edition, and the assertion failed against `edthumb3` rather than passing wrongly. **Promoted to CLAUDE.md (displacing the `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW` bullet, whose text moved verbatim to database.md) and written up in full there.**

**⭐ ITEMS 2 AND 4 — AND A NUMBER I GOT WRONG, THEN MEASURED.** The trophy caption was hardcoded at 10px while everything else on a tile derives from `grid.w`; the hero slab is 240×317 and **the hero is the common case** (4 of the 7 collectors who have pinned anything have exactly one trophy, 3 have six, nobody in between — which is precisely the "4 of 7" Cowork reported). Now `clamp(10, 14, w/17)`, floored so the six- and four-slab cases are byte-identical.

For the Simba wrap I first wrote that the character budget over-counts by **~12%**, from "mono ≈ 0.6em". ⭐ **Parsing the shipped TTF says 0.540em: 5.70px at 10px, an over-count of ~1.8% — and 1.8% was enough**, because at both live widths the old `w / 5.6` overflowed by **exactly one character** (w=170 → 171.0px in 170px; w=280 → 285.0px in 280px). The conclusion survived; the number did not. The divisor is now `captionCharBudget()` at `w / 5.7`, **asserted against the font file itself** in a new guard with controls in both directions (5.6 fails the fit case, 5.9 fails the tightness case). The profile card's serial strip gained the same clamp: it fits today at 120 px of 130, but the widest serial+tier pair anyone actually holds is 28 characters — 166 px in a 130 px tile.

**Verification.** Full suite green (16,934 tests), `tsc` clean, `lint:ratchet` 715/716, coverage 92.13 / 80.23 / 94.3 / 94.2 against thresholds 91.8 / 79.4 / 93.6 / 93.85, the six `check-*` guards 0. ⭐ **The DB-invariant pin was run against a real throwaway Postgres in the sandbox** (`initdb` as an unprivileged user; it cannot run as root) rather than only type-checked, with both mutants failing on their own assertion. Post-flight on the live DB: the other session's guard still present, `has_function_privilege` anon/authenticated both false, `check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()` length 0, row repaired, zero broken rows left.

**Left open, deliberately.** The `/_next/image` transformation cost is stated as a **unit count (≈14.3k worst case), not a price** — there is no Vercel egress from this sandbox, so the rate and the Pro allowance were not re-measured; Trevor should glance at Usage → Image Optimization once. And no card was rendered end-to-end from here (no egress to our own domain either), so the optimizer leg is proven by Next's source and the unit tests, not by a fetched PNG.

Focus traps, transitions, animations, lazy mounts, IntersectionObserver will all present as confident, reproducible **false** defects. Recorded at the focus test itself.

## 2026-09-12 (evening PT, 17:0x–18:2x) · Claude Code (cloud) — official badge art on the share cards, the trophy card's missing scarcity facts, and Pinnacle art from the cache

**Input:** Trevor, *"Proceed with all"* against Cowork buildspec **2026-09-12 (d)** (which supersedes handoff (c) and its CORRECTION; the CORRECTION was then re-uploaded three times, byte-identical, and needed no new work). **Three pushes:** `054b2467e` (the four items), `04c475bc6` (a doc note), `01f345fc8` (the production fix below). Both feature deploys verified READY by `ready` diverging from `buildingAt`, `lambdaRuntimeStats` present and apex aliases attached — never by `state` alone.

**What shipped.** `lib/badges/official-art.ts` (platform tiering, **lifted** from the Trevor-directed `SpecialSerialGlyph.tsx` rather than re-derived) · `lib/og/official-mark-art.ts` (one deduped prefetch pass per card) · `lib/og/trophy-detail.ts` (the detail lines) · Pinnacle cache-first resolution in `lib/og/img-data.ts` · `scripts/check-badge-art-registry-drift.mjs` + `npm run badges:art:check` · 36 new tests. `lib/badges/glyphs.ts` is reduced to the **fallback tier** and its header rewritten, since its "ALWAYS, with no network" contract was no longer true of the cards.

🚨 **THE BRIEF'S CENTRAL NUMBER WAS WRONG AND THE WHOLE DESIGN TURNED ON IT.** Both documents said *"53 badges across the entire product — prefetchable once into a static map"*. Measured live: `badge_taxonomy` is 53 rows of which **9 carry an `icon_url`**. ⭐ **And there is a collection-aware `badge_art_overrides` table (7 Top Shot / 8 All Day) that NEITHER document mentions** — `get_badge_display_metadata` resolves `COALESCE(override, taxonomy)` per collection. That omission is the sharp one: **All Day and Top Shot share badge TITLES ("Rookie Year", "Championship Year") with different art**, so a title resolved without its collection draws the wrong league's badge on a named collector's Moment — the same class as the Durant-captioned-"Amon-Ra" bug fixed hours earlier, and just as invisible in a PNG. Every entry point therefore takes a collection.

⭐ **The cost argument that justified inventing glyphs counted MARKS, not DISTINCT URLs.** "Six Moments × four badges = 24 fetches" is not what a render pays: badges repeat hard across a case, Top Shot's special serials need **no fetch at all** (the paths were already in the repo), and the distinct set is bounded by 17. Pinned by a test asserting six "Rookie Year" marks cost **one** fetch.

🚨 **PRODUCTION FOUND TWO DEFECTS IN MY OWN CHANGE WITHIN THE HOUR, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT FOUND THEM WAS THE WARNING LINE I HAD ADDED FOR EXACTLY THAT REASON.** First live render of the card logged *"official art unavailable for 4 mark(s)"*. Reading the logs for the same request: **all 6 `/api/badge-image` requests returned 200 with `cache=MISS`, and 4 of 6 marks still fell back.** The proxy served every one; my client gave up first. ⚠ **Note the evidence shape — a 200 at the callee and a failure at the caller, with no error on either side.** Anything reading one of the two sees a healthy system.

⭐ **Defect 1: a timeout sized by ANALOGY to the wrong thing.** 2.5s was matched to the jersey-number read, "the other read whose failure costs a glyph" — but that reads *our own database*. This waits on a PROXY whose own upstream bound is a named constant in the file being called, `UPSTREAM_TIMEOUT_MS = 8_000`. **A client budget below the bound of the thing it waits on can only truncate a request the callee would have served.** Now 4s, and because the fetches are concurrent it bounds the whole PASS, not each URL.

🚨 **Defect 2, the worse one: the memo CACHED THE MISS**, documented as deliberate. The callee is edge-cached **24h**, so a failure is nearly always a cold-cache failure **and the act of failing warms it** — the retry a cached miss forbids is exactly the one that would have succeeded. One slow cold start would have drawn fallback glyphs on every card that lambda served for the rest of its life, looking perfectly fine throughout. ⚠ **A negative result is not a fact about the world; it is a fact about ONE attempt at ONE moment.** Cache the success, drop the miss. Promoted to `key-files-and-honesty.md`.

⭐ **LAYOUT WAS VERIFIED IN PIXELS, BECAUSE NOTHING IN CI CAN SEE IT.** The detail lines re-open the exact trap the badge row was pinned for — tiles are centred, so a Moment with one fewer line is a shorter column satori centres LOWER (Simba sat 10px low once already). Both cards were rendered to PNG locally and inspected: all six tiles share one baseline, Simba included with no badges and no series. `trophyDetail` returns a FIXED-SHAPE record whose fields are `""` rather than absent, which is what lets the caller reserve every line.

⚠ **AN OVER-BROAD MOCK HAD DELETED A TEST'S SUBJECT.** `api-og-share-cards-no-false-zero` mocked `ogImageDataUri` to return the slab's PNG for **every** URL; once badges resolved through the same helper each badge became a PNG that `drawnGlyphs()` filtered out as slab art, and two cases failed reading *"the badge vanished"* — a harness failure wearing the shape of a real regression. Fixed by making the mock answer differently per URL and then **strengthening** the case to assert the badge is the *official* asset.

⛔ **WHAT I COULD NOT DO, and the workaround that mattered.** The sandbox's egress is allowlisted to package registries, so the two Dapper CDNs **and `www.rippackscity.com`** all 403 — the briefs' "prefetch the SVGs into a static blob" is not buildable from here. ⭐ **But `mcp__Vercel__web_fetch_vercel_url` reaches production**, which is what made the live verification above possible at all; without it this session would have ended believing the feature worked. **Worth remembering as the standard way to verify prod from a locked-down sandbox.**

**Also filed:** #90 (both harvest caches two months dead, home-machine writers invisible from here, and the unresolved datacenter-egress contradiction between two files in this repo) and a 🟠 mitigation note on #89.

Verified the real thing from the live CI log rather than the badge.

The churn was the symptom — the disease is that whoever tripped it read the new number off the failure message. **A hand-maintained constant is the `fix-inbox-index-counts.mjs` shape in the costume nobody recognises.** Replaced with the claim it stood in for: **the symmetric difference between the flag-on and flag-off sitemaps is exactly the panini entry**. ## 2026-09-12 (evening PT, 16:5x–17:4x) · Claude Code (cloud) — "testing and CI improvements": the comment stripper is JSX-aware, and it is now pinned to the TypeScript compiler

**Input:** Trevor, open-ended — "Can you work on testing and CI improvements?" Picked register **#87** off the open list: the shared comment stripper blind on 10 files, the fix that shipped that morning being a reword. **One push, `0094e4e52`** (+ the ledger/docs commit `e0baa8ac8`).

**What shipped.** `scripts/lib/strip-comments.mjs` gains two states — `jsxTag` (inside `< … >`, where quotes ARE attribute delimiters and `{` opens an expression container) and `jsxText` (children, where quotes, `//` and `/* */` are all prose). Element depth and the `${…}` / `{…}` containers share one stack, so `{cond && <b>x</b>}` nests and `{/* … */}` needs no special case. **Census 10 files / 899 lines → 0 / 0**, both ratchets now bans at zero, the DEFECT 4 pins **inverted rather than deleted**.

⭐⭐ **THE DELIVERABLE IS THE ORACLE, NOT THE FIX.** `__tests__/strip-comments-matches-typescript.test.ts` parses every file with the TypeScript compiler and asserts its comment ranges are EXACTLY what the stripper blanks — both directions, two bans at zero, **3,008 / 3,008 files agreeing**. The argument generalises: **all five defects in this file were found by accident months late and then pinned by a fixture describing the case just found, and a fixture can only assert the defect its author already knows about.** The contract was never a list of cases. TypeScript is independent in the way that matters — it *defines* what a comment is and resolves JSX by extension, which this helper cannot.

🚨 **IT REFUTED THE DEFECT'S OWN SAFETY CLAIM, which both the header and the register had been repeating.** DEFECT 4 was documented as *"fails in the SAFE direction … never blanks code."* **False: 2 files, 46 characters of real source BLANKED** — DEFECT 3's direction, the one that hid a live P0. JSX text was never a string state; it was parsed as CODE, so `//` in a URL opened a line comment. `app/(analytics)/analytics/api/page.tsx:116` handed every guard `"            https:                      "` where the production base URL is printed on a public page. ⭐ **Why it stood 16 days: the census counted the `sq`/`dq` SYMPTOM, which really is safe — so an instrument built on it reports the safe half completely and is blind to the unsafe half of the same root cause by construction.** *Never derive a claim about a DEFECT from an instrument that measures one of its SYMPTOMS.*

⭐ **A FIFTH DEFECT fell out of the same sweep and has nothing to do with JSX.** `word` accumulated identifier characters across whitespace — `continue` on one line and `return` on the next made `continuereturn`, not a keyword, so the regex after it read as DIVISION. **An identical regex desynced or not depending only on what sat on the previous line.** It had desynced `helpers-client-directive.test.ts` for months, attributed to nothing, because its symptom (`dq`) is indistinguishable from DEFECT 4's. The code's own comment had described the correct behaviour all along while the code did the opposite.

⛔ **THREE THINGS I GOT WRONG, each caught by an instrument rather than by review — worth more than the fix.** (1) **My oracle was wrong twice before the stripper was**, reporting 2,945 then 905 confident false findings with real file names: a char mask cannot see blanking of characters that were already spaces, and a comment on the same line as the preceding token is TRAILING trivia that `getLeadingCommentRanges` excludes by design. ⭐ The tell was the CONTENT of the first finding (`" @vitest-environment jsdom"` is obviously a comment), not its size — *when a new instrument's first finding is surprising, establish the INSTRUMENT is right before believing WHAT it says.* (2) I sized the residual `<`-ambiguity by grepping `<tag>(`, wrote **"zero instances exist in this tree"** into the header, and the census then failed on `<> (last updated {d})</>` — a FRAGMENT the pattern could not match. ⭐ **A guard's population must be counted by the guard's own predicate, not by a regex that resembles it.** (3) The oracle imported `walk` from the census *test* file, which **re-registered that whole suite** — it ran twice under the wrong filename, caught by counting tests per file (48 where 37 were expected), not by any failure.

**Gates:** measurements re-run AFTER rebasing onto six concurrent commits (a reading taken while its subject changed is not a reading) — census and oracle both re-measured on the merged tree. `npm test` **1510 files / 16,842 tests, all pass**; `tsc` clean; `lint:ratchet` 716/716 baseline; register / retired-rule / inbox-index / memory-doc-link guards all clean; ledger guards 3 / 0. **CLAUDE.md 39,982 → 39,953** — the stripper bullet rewritten net-smaller, a displacement rather than a spend.

Root cause is `strip-comments.mjs` DEFECT 4, already documented and ratcheted at **7 files**. ⭐⭐ **DashboardClient was in NEITHER census: `endState` only sees a desync still OPEN at EOF, and JSX apostrophes come in pairs, so the machine re-syncs and reports healthy.** It was the largest instance in the repo — one contraction on line 1192, **1,076 lines** read as string, 63 comment lines handed to every guard as source. Three changes, none of them rewording source: `copyOf` makes the rewards guard independent of the stripper (**"prefer a check that does not NEED it right"**, made concrete, with a positive control); `stripCommentsWithState` returns `lineStates`; a second ratchet counts the symptom directly at **10 files / 1,764 lines**. ⚠ **My first cut counted 13 files — it was counting Cadence `//` inside template literals, which is source that must survive.** A census that over-counts gets its ceiling raised, which is how a ratchet dies. ⛔ **A concurrent session got CI green the same afternoon by rewording three comments; the file is still desynced.** Register **#87**.

⭐⭐ **PART B'S CENTREPIECE WAS REFUTED BEFORE A LINE WAS WRITTEN.** The handoff specified a migration (`profile_bio.favorite_teams text[]`, backfilled, capped at 3) plus a new team picker in `/profile/edit`, on the finding that `favorite_team` is NULL for all 25 users. **Both already exist in a different shape** — `user_favorite_teams (user_id, league, team_slug, is_primary)` inner-joined to `teams_master`, written by the "Fan Affinity" picker that replaced the free-text field, read by `/api/profile/teams`, already rendering as chips on the profile page.
