A cache would cost a writer and staleness handling to save 22 buffers on an edge-cached card. **`PACKS UNOPENED` held** — register **#88**.

⭐⭐ **THE BADGES: THE READ THAT DID NOT NEED TO EXIST.** The obvious build was one `get_edition_badges_unified` per Moment, six per card. `get_trophy_slab_data` **already** builds its `badges` array from that exact function. Measured over the whole live pinned population (22 trophies, 21 resolvable): the RPC's array vs a direct unified call agreed **21 of 21**, while the raw `trophy_moments.badges` snapshot disagreed on **9 of 21**. So the two trophy cards make **zero** extra badge reads and still take the canonical answer. ⚠ **Corollary nobody has acted on: the PDF route's own unified call is now redundant.**

⚠ **TWO WAYS TO DRAW A BADGE A MOMENT HAS NOT EARNED, both closed and both pinned.** `editions.jersey_number` is **0, not null**, for a player with no number on file (Damian Lillard's row reads 0) — the canonical `jersey > 0` guard is what stops a false jersey match. And `editions.external_id` is unique **per collection**, so the lookup keys on `(collection_id, external_id)`; unqualified it hands a Top Shot Moment an All Day player's number. ⭐ **A wrong badge on a shared card is a false claim about a named collector's property published where nobody can check it — absent beats invented — and the positive mirror is pinned too, or "never draw a jersey" would have satisfied every failure case.**

⭐ **Zero network on the card path, which is the whole design.** The glyphs are RPC's own monoline geometry as **percent-encoded `data:` URIs** — not Dapper's SVG art, which at six Moments × four badges is up to **24 unbounded third-party fetches on the path a social crawler holds open**, the exact defect `lib/og/marks.tsx` exists to have removed. A test decodes every glyph and asserts no `http` outside the `xmlns`. ⚠ **Percent-encoded, not base64: `/api/og/profile` is `edge`, and an encoder that works in vitest and throws in production is the worst failure mode available.** ⭐ **The glyphs MOVED out of the PDF route rather than being copied** — its neighbour imports `pngjs`/`jpeg-js` and cannot be imported from an edge route, and a card and a PDF of the same six Moments disagreeing about what a "Rookie Mint" looks like is precisely the drift a shared definition prevents.

**Two guards caught me and both were right:** `og-cards-bound-their-database-reads` (the jersey read was unbounded) and `read-only-api-routes-outside-the-honest-error-population-are-counted` (21 against a frozen 20) — one fix cleared both. ⭐ **The jersey read is bounded at 2.5 s, a DECORATION budget deliberately below both numbers already in the repo** (10 s for a card's data, 4 s for page-blocking badge art), because a timeout costs the jersey glyph and nothing else.

**Method that paid, twice:** every visual change was **rendered to PNG and looked at** before shipping — six states across the three cards. That is what caught the tiles losing their shared baseline when a Moment had no badge row (Simba sat 10px low), and the undeduped `POR · POR · DET`. And every pin was **proven non-vacuous by mutation**: dropping the special serials reds 11 tests across all three cards, dropping the edition badges reds 10.

**Gates on the final tree:** 1509 files / 16,794 tests pass · `tsc` clean · `lint:ratchet` 716 vs 716. Left open and filed: **#87** (stripper), **#88** (packs unopened), **#89** (Pinnacle art at 72% of the byte cap).

## 2026-09-12 (morning PT, 07:2x–08:0x) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — yesterday I made a self-heal's report honest; today the honest report proved it should never run, and the trigger I called the blocker was the thing protecting the data

**Trevor:** "Keep working on anything you can, exhaust your capabilities and make sure nothing is left unresolved, then update your memory and relevant documentation before I archive the thread"

**Opened by committing the 1am pass's stranded artifacts — the fourth consecutive no-push night** (Plan9 mount dead since the Sept-8 Windows update). Handoff, ledger entry and `metrics-latest.json` committed **verbatim**; one warning in it is stale and I verified it separately rather than editing their text (below).

⭐⭐ **THE SESSION'S FINDING, and it inverts my own entry from yesterday. `serial_number > circulation_count` on a Top Shot parallel is a MIS-KEYED SALE, not a stale circulation — so `raise_impossible_parallel_circ()` was pointed at the wrong table all along.** The canonical map `moments.nft_id → edition_id` **disagrees with the sale** on both adjudicable offenders and names the **BASE** printing (nft `52643519` serial 2042 → `258:9009`, not `258:9009::16`; nft `52549592` serial 140 → `270:8973`). And **every offender's serial fits inside its base edition's circulation, 6 of 6** — the extreme being a **99-print** parallel whose "serial" was **3002** against a base of 4,000. Atlas is internally consistent on each (`effective_supply + burned = circulation`), so nothing indicts Atlas's number.

⭐⭐ **So the `BEFORE` trigger had been silently reverting every raise, and the trigger was RIGHT — raising circulation would have corrupted an Atlas-verified figure by up to 30×. The trigger is the protection; the self-heal is the defect.** Yesterday's register entry (mine) called the trigger the blocker. ⚠ **A mechanism that keeps undoing your repair is a hypothesis, not an obstacle** — this one stated its authority in its own comment: *"Parallel: Atlas is the only per-printing authority, in both directions."*

🚨 **And the protection was incomplete in the revealing direction: where Atlas had NO row nothing clamped, and one raise LANDED** — `171:5972::16` now reads `circulation_count = 1270`, raised from 93, unverifiable (no Atlas row, nft absent from `moments`), serial fitting the base's 4,000. **A guard that only fires when a reference value exists leaves exactly the unverifiable rows unprotected.**

**Shipped `20260912144225` — pg_cron jobid 219 UNSCHEDULED**, function and pin kept so the analysis stays inspectable. ⛔ **Retired rather than guarded, with the number:** requiring canonical corroboration discriminates correctly (positive control — **249** sampled Top Shot sales, **121** nfts in `moments`, **120 agree**, 1 disagrees) but `moments` covers only **~49%** of sale nfts, so the guard fails closed on half; with the trigger already reverting every Atlas-covered raise, **a guarded function is a more elaborate way of doing nothing. 274 attempts, zero legitimate repairs.** ⭐ **The detector stays and gets relabelled:** `topshot_impossible_parallel_serials` is correct — it is a **mis-keyed-sales detector**. It stays breached either way; the job never moved a sales row.

⚠ **Mechanism worth keeping: `postgres` cannot touch a `cron_heavy` job, and the error lies about why.** `cron.alter_job` returns **"Job 219 does not exist or you don't own it"** — an ownership failure wearing a missing-object's wording, for a job sitting right there `active=true`. `postgres` is a **member** of `cron_heavy`, which can `unschedule`, so `SET LOCAL ROLE cron_heavy` → `unschedule` → **`RESET ROLE`**, proven first in a rolled-back `DO` block (the raise proves the privilege, the rollback protects the schedule). Register **#19**'s headline — *"NO session-reachable role can reschedule 42 of 93"* — is true of `alter_job` only and was **already superseded six days before it was written**; corrected in place, because a filed *"you cannot"* is the least re-checked claim there is.

**Three checks that stopped me doing unnecessary or wrong work.** (1) The nightly pass warned my `offers-sweep` disable was "NOT yet effective, still firing at 02:31Z" — **02:31Z is 19:31 PT on 09-11, before my 22:00 PT push**; last firing was exactly that one, nothing in the 12 hours since. (2) A concurrent session's migration looked **fileless**, and I was about to recover it — reading `check-migration-parity.mjs` first showed it **matches on NAME, not version**, so their `…143408` file legitimately covers prod's `…143514`. **Recovering it would have re-created the duplicate-migration problem that cost me time yesterday.** (3) I reported `check-migration-parity.mjs` as fail-open on missing env; it sets `exitCode = 2` — my `$?` after a pipe had read `tail`'s status, the exact trap CLAUDE.md names. Corrected before it reached a doc.

**Also verified, not assumed:** the 09-12 institutional-wallet snapshot **landed** (3 rows, `ok=true`) — but at **04:39 PT in 383,272 ms**, 6.4× the healthy 58–72 s, and at a time that matches the GHA backstop rather than the 03:07 PT primary. **So the cron-job.org primary has now missed two consecutive days**, which strengthens rather than closes the console item; and the near-wall duration supports yesterday's wall-clock-kill reading of the 09-11 failure.

⚠⚠ **A fourth thread that went wrong first and ended in a real fix.** A full-suite red on `api-allday-listings-indexer` (hook failure, 8 skipped, **zero** failed assertions) passed in isolation, so I diagnosed a cross-file env leak — `lib/supabase.ts` calls `createClient(process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL!, …)` at **module level**, and `api-og-cards-render-sweep` **deleted** that var in `afterEach` rather than restoring it — and shipped hardening. ⛔ **Two controls refused the fix claim:** the pair passed before and after, and a full suite with my change **reverted** passed too. ⭐ **Reading the error string instead of the code frame gave the real cause: `Hook timed out in 10000ms`.** `testTimeout: 30_000` (raised 2026-08-24) **does not apply to hooks**, which kept the 10 s default, while that file's `beforeAll` does `vi.resetModules()` + a dynamic route import and measures **16,396 ms** — and **`vitest-config-notes.md` §2 names this exact file** among those that red for being slow. Shipped **`hookTimeout: 30_000`** in all three configs. ⭐ **The causal proof is the skip count, not the green run:** `16,711 passed | 8 skipped` → `16,714 passed | 5 skipped`, exactly the 3 tests of the aborted block. ⚠ A separate **contended** run (>10 min against a ~160 s baseline) gave a different 8-file red whose files all passed together — that one was environment, and elapsed time was the tell.

⭐ **A final health sweep produced a false alarm I caught before filing it, which is the useful outcome.** `reconcile-saved-wallet-stats` reads 7/7 failing on `soft_deadline_reached_partial_sweep_committed`, and `saved_wallets` shows **29 of 119 caches stale >7 days, oldest 08-08** — on columns that back the dashboard, `/profile` and `/share` cards. The obvious conclusion is that a quarter of cards understate real collections. ⛔ **Wrong: every stale wallet has `cached_moment_count = 0`.** They are EMPTY wallets, not mega-wallets starved by the 45 s budget — the 39,927-moment wallet is in the FRESH bucket, so the starvation shape I expected is refuted too. `cache_updated_at` is a **write stamp, not a freshness contract**, and any staleness metric over that table overcounts by ~28% unless it excludes zero-moment rows. ⚠ **Bucketing by size was the whole discriminator; the aggregate age hid it.** ⓘ One real, unexplained signal does sit in that lane and I am leaving it stated rather than chased: `fail_count` was 0–1/day through 09-08 and is **12 / 12 / 7 / 16 / 11** from 09-09, with `rows_written` up from 34–77/day to 66–182 — more work done, more often unfinished, i.e. a load change rather than a break.

**Close-out:** ledger 1858 → 1859 (guards 3 / 0), register #82 re-framed 🟠 → 🟢 and #19 corrected, issues index regenerated, repo migration file written the same turn and **md5-verified byte-exact** against the applied statement (⚠ local length 5392 vs prod 5390 — Postgres counts characters, Node counts UTF-16 units, and the text carries two 🚨 surrogate pairs; the md5 is the authority). Lessons promoted to [database.md](../reference/database.md) and [cron-and-schedulers.md](../reference/cron-and-schedulers.md); one new memory plus the cron mechanism appended.

## 2026-09-12 (morning PT, 07:1x–08:5x) · Claude Code (cloud) — asked who actually consumes the database, and the answer was not on anyone's list

**Autonomous continuation:** "Keep going and doing what you can. Plan on working autonomously for the next few hours."

⭐ **THE MORNING'S METHOD, and it is the reusable part: rank pg_cron jobs by busy-seconds per hour in the UNCONTENDED window.** Every earlier pass at the saturation spells ranked jobs *during* a spell, which cannot work — in a spell every job's busy-seconds rise, so the ranking measures the spell. Restricted to 02:00–06:00Z, the fleet runs **1,857 busy-seconds per hour across 117 jobs** and **one job is 666 of them.**

🚨 **SHIPPED: `atlas_listing_verify_dispatch` grouped and sorted 261,531 nft_ids every two minutes to return TWO.** `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` back-to-back on the same warm cache: **57,176 buffers + temp read 838 / written 1,570 (5 batches, 7,624 kB spilled), 264,187 rows, 424 ms → 54 buffers, no temp, 500 rows, 2.2 ms — 1,059×.** Live: **seven consecutive ticks failing at exactly 120.0 s**, then 11.7 / 3.1 / 3.7 s, all succeeded. Matched windows: **14.6 % success before (89 ticks), 85.7 % after.** Migration `20260912143408`, register **#85**.

⚠ **AND THE SPELL DID NOT END WITH IT, which I checked rather than assumed.** The severe phase — 44 `job startup timeout`s in one 10-minute bucket — ended at **13:40Z, 52 minutes BEFORE the migration**, on its own. What the fix removed is the residual floor: 5–8 failures per 10 minutes from 13:40→14:32 (essentially all jobid 466's own 120 s timeouts), then **2, 2, 0**. ⭐ **A fix that lands into a recovering system will be credited with the recovery unless you go and find the minute the recovery started.**

⭐⭐ **WHERE THE DISK IO ACTUALLY GOES, and nobody had written it down: three tables holding 5.7 GB are 60 % of it.** `wallet_moments_cache` **33.0 %** (3,292 MB, read ~3,450× over) · `fmv_snapshots_2026` **13.9 %** · `sales_2026` **12.8 %**. Nothing else reaches 5 %. **They are one chain — `sales` → `fmv_snapshots` → `wallet_moments_cache` — so the FMV recompute path is ~60 % of this database's disk IO**, which quantifies CLAUDE.md's existing *"fmv-recalc owns the DB's #1 reader"* and names the #2 and #3 for the first time. Promoted to [database.md](../reference/database.md).

⭐ **#84's "fresh corroboration" REFUTED by the control it never had.** The item argued jobid 355 is a real cause from one day (fires 07:23:03Z, runs 601.8 s, burst at 07:25). But on 09-12 jobs **212 and 466 were already failing at 06:50:00Z** and 466 failed on **every tick** for the 33 minutes before 355 fired; and on **09-11 355 ran at 07:23 and SUCCEEDED in 9.8 s** with the spell starting at 07:28 anyway. Read as a distribution, 355 is **8–10 s whenever the instance is idle and 80–600 s whenever it is not, on work the item itself calls constant — a thermometer.** ⚠ **The item had already said "contended, not oversized" before the one-day sequence talked it back out of it**, and the control was one query away: the same job, the previous day. ⭐ **A "fresh corroboration" appended to an existing item is exactly where controls get skipped, because the item already has a conclusion and the new reading is only being asked whether it agrees.**

✅ **#76's own named test ANSWERED and PASSING: the backstop's schedule fires** — 10 `event='schedule'` heartbeats, first 09-11 07:28:40Z. 🚨 **But the rate it reveals is 8.1 %, not the 27 % the item assumes**, corroborated from a second instrument (the eight 15-minute lanes each ran **twice** in 6 hours; `alerts-send`, on ~5 minutes, also twice). 🚨 **And the user-facing cost, which nothing had measured: alert delivery latency 0.0 → 10.0 → 35.7 → 289.5 minutes across 09-09→09-12** (n=1/day, stated). Hand-fired the backstop at 14:52:24Z — relief, not a fix.

⚠⚠ **I WROTE "THE MISSING ARM IS A RATE ARM" AND WENT TO BUILD ONE. IT ALREADY EXISTED, SHIPPED 05:47Z THE SAME NIGHT.** `check_pipeline_cadence_collapse()` (`20260912054710`) is pinned, has positive controls both ways, and run live **inspected 99 pipelines and returned 11 degraded + 5 stopped** — every lane in #76 by name with its ratio. ⛔ **It has zero callers ON PURPOSE**, and the migration header says so with an exit condition: wiring it while nine lanes are legitimately dead makes it permanently red, the #25 trap. ⭐ **A decision not to act with a number and a trigger attached is a decision to respect, not a gap to fill** — and the only thing that stopped me shipping a duplicate on top of it was looking for an existing one first.

⚠ **A SECOND SELF-CORRECTION, on #85's own coverage claim.** I framed the verify lane as badly under-provisioned as a delisting detector. Measured: **`completed` IS set at volume — 575,965 completed vs 264,187 open, 295,760 purchased** — so the *firehose* closes listings and the probe does not have to; the oldest open listing dates to **2026-09-07 07:23Z**, the hour the firehose started, so the stock is five days of a real marketplace, not a leak; and `ts_listings` holds **55,409 rows** against a 0–24 h open bucket of **55,959**, so the reader surface is healthy. **The probe's real gap is CANCELLATIONS specifically, bounded at 24 h by the freshness cut. Narrower and much less valuable than I first wrote.**

⚠ **Two measurement traps paid for in full this morning.** `pg_statio_user_tables` sorts **NULLs FIRST** under `ORDER BY … DESC`, so a naive `heap_blks_read + idx_blks_read` ordering put 16 kB tables on top and buried the 11 TB one — `coalesce` every term. And I read recency off **`pipeline_runs_daily`**, which refreshes six-hourly; it reported eight lanes 191 minutes silent and `check-alerts` freshly dead, while the live table showed them all current. **CLAUDE.md already forbids the rollup for recency; this is what ignoring it costs — a fabricated twelfth-lane finding, caught only by a control.**

⛔ **Declined with reasons rather than caution:** `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` (1,826 GB / **208 exec-hours** / 4,211 calls — biggest statement in the estate, but a deadline-bounded drain *designed* to spend its budget, pinned, FMV-critical, already optimised several times; its cost is upstream over-production, 74 % of new snapshots identical) · a `REINDEX CONCURRENTLY` its own comment invites (bloat **quoted, never measured**, and a full rebuild's IO mid-spell) · wiring the rate arm (above) · #35's OFFSET pair (its blocker needs `get_edge_function`, which CLAUDE.md records as having burned a live gate key twice — an operator step, not a sandbox one) · tightening the GHA backstop cron (at 8 % per-slot delivery the extra fires buy little) · a pg_cron→Vercel restoration (would mean planting `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN` in `cron.job.command`).

⭐ **SECOND HALF (08:1x–08:5x PT) — two more ships, and the class sweep that found the second one.**

🚨 **SHIPPED: `dist_resolved` reported 96 % success for 2.8 % progress.** `backfill-pack-rip-metadata` read **484 / 484 / 488 of 500** on three consecutive runs. ⭐ **The tell was not the size of the number but that the three barely moved.** The value came from `RETURNING pr.dist_id IS NOT NULL` over an `UPDATE … SET dist_id = COALESCE(…)` — `RETURNING` yields the NEW row, so it counted every row that ENDED UP with a dist_id, including every row that walked in with one. First honest run: **466 already set / 14 newly resolved / 20 still null**. ✅ **End-to-end verified in production**: 18:53Z → 478/1/21, 19:53Z → 394/**70**/36, both summing to 500, and a failed 17:53Z run correctly writing all-null. ⭐ **The old field would have printed 479 and 464 — two numbers that look identical; the honest one prints 1 and 70. The variance IS the proof.**

🚨 **SHIPPED: two `fmv-recalc` telemetry fields were `const … = 0`.** `extra.blended` / `.ask_proxy` read 0 on every day since 08-31 (~1,700 runs, ~800,000 rows) while every other leg varied — because the Flowty blend and floor-ask paths were deleted **2026-05-24** and their counters were left behind, still shipped into `pipeline_runs.extra`. ⚠ **They are confidence-lifting method counters, so they sit exactly where an M1/M2 audit looks — and I chased them** (45-day baseline, an `_error`-pairing correlation) before finding the `const`. **That cost is the evidence they were worth deleting.** ⛔ **And my first hypothesis was WRONG in a specific, instructive way: the two dead fields were also the only two with no paired `_error` field — a tidy correlation pointing at "they fail silently", which is this repo's own `_error`-pairing rule staring back. A correlation that FLATTERS AN EXISTING RULE is exactly the one to check against the source before filing.**

⛔ **REFUTED, with a control, the mechanism shared by #73, #84 and go-live M11:** `max_worker_processes = 6` exhaustion. `cron.use_background_workers` is **off** (pg_cron uses client sessions; the cap is `cron.max_running_jobs` = 32), observed concurrency reaches **15**, and **09-12 13:00Z and 14:00Z have identical concurrency (max 11, mean 4.4) with 114 vs 0 startup timeouts.** ⭐ **What replaces it is better: `job startup timeout` is a SEVERITY MARKER of IO saturation** — every hour at ≥45 statement timeouts carries a burst, every hour at ≤43 carries none. 🚨 **So M11's metric is a CLIFF FUNCTION: it reads exactly zero while pressure climbs, then explodes — which is the shape it recorded ("09-03→09-08 all ZERO, then 09-09: 135"). Its zeros are not evidence of health.**

⚠⚠ **THE SESSION'S HARDEST LESSON, and it is about my own conduct: I DEGRADED PRODUCTION WHILE MEASURING IT.** Investigating #72 I ran unbounded 55-second scans of `pack_rips` (2 GB) on a 2-core IO-bound instance. `cron.job_run_details`: jobid 466 ran **3.6–6.2 s** all morning, then **120.0 s FAILED at 15:18, 15:20 and 15:22Z**, then **6.1 s** at 15:26 — **the failures start and stop with my scans.** ⛔ **And the `pg_stat_statements` delta then reported my own probes as the fix regressing (3.9 → 4.79 → 18.38 s/call); the natural reading, "the fix stopped working", would have triggered a revert.** ⭐ **CLAUDE.md says "freeze the tree, then measure"; the sharper case is that the probe and the subject share ONE IO BUDGET, so investigating a saturation defect MAKES the instance saturate.** Promoted to [database.md](../reference/database.md) and into CLAUDE.md's four-ways bullet (displacing the clock-sweep instance, which moved to database.md verbatim).

✅ **ALSO: a STEER block in `focus.md`** so tonight's 1am pass does not re-chase the five levers measurement closed today, and **#85's cleanest evidence is ATTRIBUTION rather than duration** — `atlas_listing_verify_dispatch` named **252 of 464** failure contexts before the fix and **0 of 5** after, with four of those five in `sync_cached_listings_from_atlas`, the target explicitly deferred.

**All five CI runs green (5187–5191); `npm test` 1,506 files / 16,718 tests, `tsc` clean; ACLs preserved on the replaced function and `check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()` array length 0 — ⚠ its raw `count(*)` reads 1 when CLEAN, the mixed-return-shape trap.**

## 2026-09-12 (overnight PT, 23:5x–02:0x) · Claude Code (cloud) — three register items were carrying diagnoses that measurement refuted, and in two cases the evidence had been sitting in our own database for weeks

**Autonomous continuation:** "I'm going to bed. Plan on continuing at this for the next 10 hours."

⭐ **THE NIGHT'S PATTERN: every item I picked up to ACT on turned out to be describing the wrong mechanism, and the correction was always cheaper than the original investigation.** Four register items advanced, three of them by refutation.

🚨 **#74's "cheapest real win" SHIPPED — and taken literally it would have shipped the defect it was preventing.** Every pack depletion and packs-remaining number on the site comes from a supply counter whose refresh lane is dead, with no age beside it. #74 said: render `topshot_pack_supply.updated_at` as an as-of. ⛔ **But `apply_topshot_supply`'s FAILURE branch bumps `updated_at` while leaving the stale counters in place** — it is an ATTEMPT stamp, so that would print a fresh age over 73-day-old numbers. ⭐ **The tell cost one query: `max(updated_at)` = 09-11 against `max(updated_at) WHERE supply_ok` = 08-26.** A sixteen-day gap between *newest row* and *newest GOOD row*. Shipped `pack_table_rows.supply_as_of` + `depletion_as_of` (branch-matched in SQL to the COALESCE that produces the number they date), then `topshot_pack_supply.last_success_at`, written by the success branch only. **Production now reads `97% · pool · as of 15d ago` and `97% · of all minted packs · as of 74d ago`, verified by rendered DOM on cache MISSes.**

🚨 **AND #74's DIAGNOSIS IS WRONG: the lane is not dead, it is a ONE-SHOT HYDRATOR.** `get_topshot_supply_backfill_targets` selects only dists that have NEVER succeeded, so its 2,083 rows are frozen **by the predicate** — ⛔ **fixing the #81 GraphQL outage would not unfreeze them.** ⭐ **The identical defect was diagnosed in writing eleven days earlier for All Day** (migration `20260901071258`: *"it selects on the very column it then fills… a ONE-SHOT HYDRATOR, not a refresher"*, and it **predicted its own refreeze**) — nobody connected them, because nothing indexed the CLASS. ⭐ **Free detector, promoted: `count(DISTINCT <stamp>::date)` on any hydrated table.** Top Shot reads 28 across 75 days; All Day reads **2**. A refresher cannot produce a 2. ⛔ **The predicate fix is deliberately NOT shipped and the ORDER is the point:** with the upstream failing, a re-walk flips every row to `supply_ok=false` while the counters survive, so the page would serve the same stale number **and lose the age it just gained**. Column first, predicate second, and only once the upstream returns.

🚨 **#81's central question — "gated query or origin error?" — is ANSWERED, and the item says it cannot be, from inside.** It asks for *"one authenticated call from a machine with egress"*. ⛔ **No egress was needed: `pipeline_runs_daily.last_error` stores the full upstream HTML body, and every 530 carries Cloudflare error reference 1033 with the page explaining itself** — *"the host is configured as a Cloudflare Tunnel, and Cloudflare is currently unable to resolve it."* ⭐ **1033 is the ONLY reference number in the entire retained history: 32 pipeline-days, 08-28 → 09-12.** It is raised by Cloudflare's edge **before the request reaches the application**, so it cannot be a per-query gate — and the same failure hits a different query on a different path. **So #81's proposed fix, "move the sweep onto a supported query", would not have worked.** 🚨 **WHY NOBODY SAW IT FOR FIFTEEN DAYS: the alert renders `left(last_error, 160)` and every triage used `left(error, 70..300)` — the body spends its first ~900 characters on a `<style>` block, so every truncation stopped before the diagnosis.**

⚠ **THE SAME SHAPE BIT ME IN THE OPPOSITE DIRECTION IN THE SAME NIGHT, AND THE RULE AGAINST IT ALREADY EXISTED.** I ran `left(command, 300)` on `cron.job` and printed a live gate key into this transcript. `tooling-gotchas.md` has said since 09-02: *"A truncating projection is not a redaction. `left(command, 140)` on one of these rows prints a PARTIAL key."* ⭐ **The failure was in the INDEX, not the content** — CLAUDE.md's one-line summary named only `get_edge_function`, so a `cron.job` query did not look like a secret-handling operation. Amended to name both. **When a documented rule is broken by someone who would have followed it, fix the pointer, not the rule.** *(Operator: rotate `TOPSHOT_PACK_SUPPLY_GATE_KEY`; it joins an already-recorded exposure rather than creating a new one.)*

✅ **#79 SIZED: 58 % of pg_cron edge dispatches leave NO trace in `pipeline_runs`.** Twelve active jobs dispatch 2,409×/24 h; **five of them — 1,392/day — have no counterpart in that table, ever.** ⭐ **The instrument worth reusing is a CADENCE search, which needs no name mapping at all:** two of these jobs dispatch 480×/day, and **zero lanes logged 440–520 runs yesterday**. ⚠ **I produced a false zero TWICE, the same way, before this came out right** — callee-name matching missed one lane, grepping `logPipelineRun("` missed another. **Both times the tool said *no evidence* and I nearly heard *evidence of no*.** ⭐ **The fix is the transferable part: a POSITIVE regex for ANY write path, evaluated SEPARATELY from name extraction, so "logs but I cannot name it" is a distinct answer from "does not log".**

✅ **#71's guard extended to the number HOISTED INTO A NAME** — the gap its own header called *"only literals can be judged statically"*, which had a live instance (`const CAP = 2000` on a plain PostgREST select). ⚠ **Gain recorded as ZERO, because it is:** that lane's pool is **5 rows of 56,646**, so the clamp never fired. Truth-in-source, not a save. ⚠ **My first resolver had a bug my own test caught** (`let CAP = 2000; CAP = 10` read as one definition), which is why that case is an assertion and not a comment.

✅ **#55 re-derived live rather than re-read:** both 2-hourly Routines still `enabled: false`, last fire **2026-09-01** — eleven days, up from the item's eight. ⭐ **And a live counter-example to its "cloud-only therefore cannot push": this cloud session pushed to `main` thirteen times tonight.** Cloud-ness is not the blocker.

**Every code push CI-green; `npm test` 1504 files / 16,708 tests, `tsc` clean, `lint:ratchet` 716 vs baseline 717; the full 188-file DB-invariant suite run locally against postgres-16, with 5 SQL and 7+7 TS mutations all killed.**

## 2026-09-11 (night PT, 21:4x–23:4x) · Claude Code (cloud) — a register item I was re-deriving turned out to be two phenomena, and the bigger one had been invisible for two days

**Autonomous continuation:** "I'm going to bed. Plan on continuing at this for the next 10 hours."

⭐ **THE NIGHT'S LESSON IS ABOUT INSTRUMENTS, NOT LANES, AND IT REPEATED FOUR TIMES.** Every finding below came from asking *what does this instrument actually measure* rather than *what does it say*.

🚨 **NINE LANES — BOTH USER-FACING ALERT LANES AMONG THEM — HAD RUN AT 1/12th CADENCE FOR TWO DAYS WITH THREE INSTRUMENTS GREEN.** `wmc-fmv-populate` 2,016 → 91 runs/day, `snapshot-pack-asks` 288 → 13, `alerts-send` 144 → 13, six more at 96 → 13. The step lands on 09-10, the Vercel spend-cap pause, and the mechanism is the one `dead-lane-backstop.yml`'s own header **predicted in advance**: cron-job.org auto-disables a high-frequency job after consecutive failures, so a 10-hour outage kills the 1–15-minute lanes and spares the 4-hourly ones. ⭐ **That the primary contributes ZERO was established without the console** (off-limits here): all nine lanes' runs sit inside single second-clusters with no run between them, and `pinnacle-listings-indexer` — an entry never disabled — is the live control at 72/24 h, gap avg **20.0**, max **20.1**. ⛔ **No autonomous fix exists and I checked rather than assumed:** all 13 `net.http_*` pg_cron jobs call edge functions behind `?key=`; **none** calls a `rippackscity.com/api/*` route, and those need `INGEST_SECRET_TOKEN`, which is not in the DB. **Nine entries for Trevor, with cadences, in the filing.**

🚨 **WHY NOTHING CAUGHT IT IS STRUCTURAL: a silence detector cannot see a cadence collapse.** `cron_silent` fires at **1,800 minutes**; a lane ticking every **177** is never 30 h silent, and every tick reads `ok: true`. **Liveness is a floor test, cadence is a rate test**, and only the first was instrumented. **Shipped `check_pipeline_cadence_collapse()`** — observed runs against each lane's **own** trailing median, no hand-maintained cadence table. ⭐ **Thresholds measured, not chosen:** across 100 lanes the 12-hour ratio separates with **nothing in between** (collapsed 0.000 and 0.028–0.111; healthy 0.625 lowest, bulk 0.92–1.05), so 0.40 has 1.56× margin; a 6-hour window was **measured and rejected** (two healthy 32/day lanes land at exactly 0.500 — jitter). ⭐ **It splits `degraded` from `stopped`, which is what makes it shippable at all:** merging them fires on four long-retired lanes and reads as noise (**#25**), so the split removes the need for a suppression list. **Shipped UNWIRED** on purpose — it would be red until the console fix.

⚠⚠ **AND THE ARM FOUND A LIMITATION IN ITSELF THE SAME NIGHT, which is the most useful thing it did.** `refresh_wmc_fmv_changed` sat in its `degraded` list at 0.028 — **and the work never stopped.** jobid 303 runs it every 10 minutes, 144 times in 24 h, and it is the estate's **#1 disk consumer** (1,816 GB over the 31-day window). The seven `pipeline_runs` rows land **exactly at the GHA backstop's tick times**: the table records the route path and is blind to **95 %** of executions. ⛔ **A rate arm over `pipeline_runs` measures the LOGGER, not the lane.** ⭐ **I had already drafted the opposite conclusion** — *"the collapse removed the top disk consumer; re-enabling restores ~59 GB/day"* — **a tidy story one query on `cron.job_run_details` destroyed.** Documented with the discriminator, and with the caveat that a NULL `cron.job` match proves nothing (it sees only what a job calls directly), which is why the sibling is recorded as **unresolved** rather than confirmed.

⭐ **M2's THIRD LEVER COSTED, AND THE CHECK THAT MATTERED CUT IT BY TWO THIRDS.** This morning's Dune filing proved All Day is missing an entire sale type (~24 % of volume) and deferred the sizing. **Top Shot carries the same lane from the same contract**, so the crossing rate to the ≥5 floor is measurable rather than assumed: **10.1 / 21.6 / 33.1 / 40.1 %** at 1/2/3/4 sales, and **897 of Top Shot's 4,747 floor editions would fall below it without the lane**. 🚨 **Then: at 3–4 sales, 365 of All Day's 553 editions are ALREADY HIGH/MEDIUM.** Naive **+3.2 pt** → real **+1.1 pt** (tight) / **+2.7 pt** (wide) — the *"eligibility is not gain"* rule biting on this same metric for the **third** time. **Largest code-side M2 lever measured to date, and still short alone.** ⚠ **The latency inverts which step matters:** M2 reads a **30-day** window, so a forward lane pays out in ~30 days — **the backfill is what moves the gate this month**, and it is Trevor's call. ⛔ **I did not ship the lane:** it is a **new writer into `sales`**, the FMV input and the gate's own measurement, and the payload shape is unverifiable from here (no Flow egress, nothing retains an All Day `OfferCompleted`). **Sizing it makes the decision cheap; shipping it unverified at midnight against the accuracy gate is the wrong trade.**

⚠ **A CORRECTION I ISSUED BEFORE IT COULD BECOME A CLAIM.** My hand-derivation read M2 at **29.7 %** against the published **25.6 %** — a tempting "+4 points overnight". I read `rpc_thp_leg_fmv_coverage`'s source instead of assuming: **the definitions are identical**, so the gap is **sweep position** — the same artifact behind the doc's already-retracted "28.3 %". **No single leg is a level for M2 while the sweep is mid-cycle.**

✅ **#78 REFUTED AND SPLIT, and its refutation was CIRCULAR.** The Golazos lane is not broken: the same filter matched **113** events on 08-31 and **25** on 09-11, and `blocks_scanned` is **~108,000/day against the ~86,000 Flow produces**, so no window was skipped. ⛔ **#78 cited `sales` rows as proof the market was live — written by a SIBLING LANE reading the SAME event stream at the SAME address. Two lanes sharing an upstream are ONE instrument; they go quiet together.** So 09-01→09-10 was a thin market with an honest lane; 09-10 onward is the cadence collapse. ✅ **#70's "coincident and unexplained" cadence drop is likewise answered and exonerated** — a 15→20-minute re-timing across five lanes (`pinnacle-sales-indexer` flat at 71 is the control), with the per-run-cap condition **not** met: `blocks_scanned` ~1,500 against ~1,200 produced, cursors at the head.

⚠ **One self-inflicted scare worth recording: I "found" a missing hourly tick and started diagnosing a regression in my own change.** The DB clock said **06:19Z** — the tick was due at 06:22, three minutes in the **future**. I had assumed the time instead of reading it, which is the trap this repo has recorded four times. **Read the clock, then reason.** The tick ran on schedule and `q4_targets_total` fell **7 → 6**, which is the convergence the fix predicted.

**Also verified rather than assumed tonight:** the cloud sandbox **has postgresql-16**, so the full DB-invariant suite ran locally (**188/188, exit 0**) and the new pin was **mutation-proven before the push** — "no Postgres on the box" is true of the desktop and had been generalised into "pins cannot be run before pushing". And **#61's lever (a) closes as already working**: 9 of 12 recent deployments CANCELED, the 3 READY ones exactly the code pushes.

## 2026-09-11 (late evening PT, 21:1x–22:0x) · Claude Code (cloud) — draining register #71 found a lane that had reported `ok` 63 times in a row while three of its four queues could not have succeeded

**Autonomous session:** "look for things to work on in the to-do list, known problems, audits, roadmaps… plan on working autonomously for the next 2 hours."

⭐ **The whole finding came out of re-deriving ONE number an open register item already carried.** #71 item (1) described `/api/cron/pinnacle-metadata-backfill`'s Q3/Q4 discovery pools as a false PostgREST bound over a pool of *">= 9,000"* rows, with a bounded blast radius. The pool is **56,440**, and at that size the consequence is not "some keys may be unreachable": **9 Q4 targets and 5 Q3 disagreements exist in the collection, and the lane could see 2-3 and ZERO respectively** — on every one of its 63 retained hourly runs. An open item rots in the reassuring direction, and this one understated itself by 6×.

🚨 **The lane looked perfectly healthy and was converging on nothing.** 63 runs, 63 `ok`, and `rows_written` **equal to `catalog_upserted` on every single one** — `rows_written` sums five counters, so the other three were 0 throughout. ⛔ **None of those three was published in `extra`**, which carried exactly the two counters that were moving. That is **#70 one level down:** a per-step count with no sibling count cannot show a step that never fires.

⭐ **Three causes, and the measurement order is what kept me honest.** (1) The unordered 1,000-row head. (2) Q1/Q2 re-buying the same structurally unfillable candidates hourly because **nothing records an attempt** — Q2's pool is **197 rows whose `last_seen_at` is 2026-05-07/08, four months stale**, read `order by last_seen_at desc`, so the same 50 are fanned out to Cadence forever. (3) ⭐ **I was about to write off Q4's hourly upserts as more of (2) and the live read said otherwise:** its completeness test was `character_name <> 'Unknown'` while its own writer turns an empty on-chain name into that literal — **a repair loop that cannot terminate, writing the exact condition that re-selects it.** Three rows with real mint counts (270/299/148) and `updated_at` stamps on recent ticks were the fingerprint. ⚠ **And my fix for (1) would have made (3) WORSE first**, evaluating all three every tick instead of occasionally — worth saying, because a coverage fix on top of a non-terminating loop amplifies it.

⛔ **THE OBVIOUS REPAIR WAS MEASURED AND REJECTED, which is the part I would want a future session to copy.** `DISTINCT ON` over the pool: **38,398 buffers / 8,076 ms**. The full `wmc × pinnacle_nft_map` join: **213,341 buffers / 12,110 ms** nested-loop, **145,054 / 28,145 ms** with `enable_nestloop = off` — ⚠ forcing a hash/merge halved the buffers and more than doubled the time. ⭐ **What shipped is a LOOSE INDEX SCAN: all 423 distinct keys for 2,154 buffers / 255 ms, 18× cheaper than the `DISTINCT ON` AND complete rather than a head** — on an index that already existed. Q3 then walks that list behind a persisted cursor at 25 keys/tick, **slicing on KEY boundaries because one key holds 1,510 rows** and a row-count page would let rows fall between slices (the `.range()` trap in a different costume). Full pass ~17 ticks / ~100k buffers: **bounded per tick and half the buffers of the one full join it replaces.**

⭐ **Positive control before trusting any of it:** successive calls returned **2 then 3 disagreements — the same 5 the full scan found** — then wrapped (`q3_wrapped: true, q3_pass: 1`). Work the old read reported as `q3_eligible: 0` on 63 consecutive runs. **Mutation-proven both ways** on the route: forcing the old "a failed read renders as an empty queue" branch reds the new null-payload case; deleting the three restored counters reds the counter case. ⚠ **The payload check rejects ARRAYS, and that was not belt-and-braces** — the test harness's default empty rpc result is `[]`, `typeof [] === "object"`, and an array body would have read as two empty queues.

⭐ **One verification worth repeating: the committed migration file's function body md5s IDENTICAL to the deployed `prosrc`** (whitespace-normalised). `apply_migration` applies first and the file is a manual follow-up, so the repo record is usually *a retyping* of production — this one is provably not.

**Left undone and named:** cause (2) needs an attempt stamp on a 2.3 M-row table, which is not a wrap-up edit, and the cheaper-looking alternative (deleting stale cache rows) is a data mutation on a user-facing holdings surface. Filed with its falsifier (borrow one of the 197 moments; if `PinInfo` returns, the hypothesis is wrong and the bug is in the apply path) — ⚠ unverifiable from here, this sandbox has no Flow egress.

**Durable half promoted** to [database.md](../reference/database.md): the loose-index-scan recipe with its measured comparison, and the writer-can-un-satisfy-its-own-predicate class with the free tell (read the writer's fallback, then ask whether the selector can reject it). CLAUDE.md untouched — 39,998 of 40,000 chars.

## 2026-09-11 (evening PT, 20:4x–21:1x) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — a monitor filed three items and named a suggested action for each; all three actions were wrong, and the one that mattered had already cost a day of data

**Trevor:** "Keep going until there's nothing left for you to do, then update memory and relevant documentation before I archive the thread"

**A fresh `rpc-daytime-monitor` filing (03:09Z) was sitting untracked** — its mount is down a third night — so I drained it and committed it on its behalf. Each item came with a suggested action. **None survived.**

⭐⭐ **HIGH — the suggested action was "check the cron-job.org console." That console is off-limits here (its bearer has leaked twice), and it was never needed.** The route writes `cron_heartbeats` *before* the work exactly so *never fired* can be told from *fired and died*: it read `edge_runwork_start` at **05:15 PT**. ⚠ **Then I attributed the fire before concluding** — 05:15 PT = 12:15Z matched the **GHA backstop** (12:15:15Z), not the 10:07Z primary. Skipping that step inverts the answer: "the primary fired, so the console is fine" would have been exactly backwards.

🚨 **The backstop got `202 {"accepted":true,…,"message":"queued","note":"Real results will appear in pipeline_runs within ~10-30s"}` — and they never did.** Four greens, every one about **acceptance**: GHA `success`, HTTP `202`, `edge_status: 200`, `ok: true`. **The only honest instrument was the outcome table:** `wallet_holdings_snapshot` holds 3 rows/day for 09-04→09-10 and **ZERO for 09-11**. ⛔ **And the gap is permanent** — `snapshot_at` is the UTC date *at run time*, so a re-fire writes today's row and silently leaves the hole. The mechanism is a **wall-clock kill of the `EdgeRuntime.waitUntil` task**, uncatchable by `try/catch` *or* `.catch()`; since the function writes a `pipeline_runs` row on all four terminal paths, **zero rows is the signature of a kill**, not a bug in a branch.

⭐ **The positive control refuted my own hypothesis.** From three fires with zero run rows I had concluded the backstop was structurally broken. A `workflow_dispatch` at 20:49 PT on a quiet instance returned **`ok=true, 3 rows, 58,639 ms, 257 pages`**. The path is sound — **the fire time is the defect**: nominal `29 7 * * *`, but eight consecutive fires landed **11:18–13:34Z (+3h49m to +6h05m)**, so it *trails* the primary into the morning IO window instead of leading it. **Shipped: the workflow's six-week-old STALE-RATIONALE note corrected with the measurements inline — comment only, `cron:` untouched, YAML re-parsed and the schedule asserted unchanged.** ⛔ **Deliberately not re-timed:** the delay spread is over two hours wide, so no nominal minute maps to a predictable fire. ⭐ Detection did *not* fail — the watchlist flagged it `high` unaided — and the impact is small and said so: 3 rows/day, 2 wallets.

⛔ **MEDIUM — "the upstream timeout is transient; a re-fire likely succeeds" died on the long baseline.** `pipeline_runs` retains ~73 h and could only show one failure; **`pipeline_runs_daily` is indefinite**: `match-topshot-players` failed **11 of 37 non-gated runs (30%)**, including **eight consecutive days 08-14 → 08-21**, always at 125.3–126.2 s — while its best *success* is **120,444 ms**. ⭐ **The success band's upper tail sits ON the ~120 s gateway wall**, so instance load decides the outcome, not the upstream. ⚠ Gating it to weekly turned a fixed 30% rate into **~1 week in 3 with no output** — lowering a cadence multiplies the cost of a failure rate rather than reducing it. ⛔ I could not re-fire it (edge function, no pg_cron job, no workflow — only the off-limits console or a secret), and said so rather than skipping it quietly.

⚠ **LOW — already fixed in shipped code, and my explanation for it was wrong.** `meta.errors: 0` on a live read settles that the empty board is a genuine empty, and the page already implements the third state. I then found `compute-topshot-pack-ev` last ran 08-30 and inferred a starved ranker — **checked instead of shipping**: Top Shot's newest `pack_ev_latest.snapshotted_at` is **20 minutes old** (the Atlas lane replaced it), and exactly **3 of 1,210** rows pass everything but freshness, matching the API's own `stale_count: 3`. ⚠ Filed not fixed: `public_board_empty_count = 0` — the MV is not on the monitored-empty list, so a public board can sit empty with nothing counting it.

⚠ **Two process lessons, both self-inflicted.** My conflict-marker pre-check used an unanchored `includes("<<<<<<<")` and refused a **correct** ledger resolution — **0 real markers, 11 prose matches**, and the ledger records a previous session making the identical mistake at line 11220. Anchor to `^<<<<<<< `. And a full-suite red (`migration-new-public-table-enables-rls`) was **not mine**: my checkout was 5 commits stale and `a79939686` had already fixed those two recovered migrations upstream. **A red run is not automatically yours** — after the rebase it passed 5/5.

⭐⭐ **Then a health sweep for 100%-failing lanes found the best item of the night, and it was nobody’s filing.** `offers-sweep` — 2 runs, 2 failures, `Top Shot GraphQL failed with 530` — is Top-Shot-only and calls the **decommissioned** `public-api.nbatopshot.com`. `pipeline_runs_daily` dates the break exactly: **209,521 / 215,517 / 209,808 rows on 08-25–08-27**, **145,056 on 08-28** as the host dies mid-day, then **0 every day from 08-29** at 70–72 runs/day. ⚠ The flat **36 ok / 36 fail** is not a fabricated green — it is the route’s own half-open 30-minute outage breaker logging `ok=true, skipped`. ⭐ **But the lane was already dead ON PURPOSE:** register **#65** re-pointed both halves to Atlas on **09-07** and made it INACTIVE — and `dead-lane-backstop.yml`, added **09-10**, was resurrecting it, on a comment that calls the 08-29 stop *“still unexplained”* four days after the register explained it. 🚨 **And the 🚨 claim justifying that step is false for a keepable reason:** *“ONLY WRITER of `edition_offers.highest_offer` — grep-verified across app/ and lib/”* — **the replacement writer is a DATABASE function**, `raise_edition_offers_from_chain()` on pg_cron `[34 * * * *]`, last six runs all succeeded. CLAUDE.md states that rule verbatim and this is its **second** instance after #81. ⚠ **I hit the adjacent trap first:** I read `max(updated_at)` = 21:47 PT and called it fresh — but `edition_offers` has **one shared timestamp** stamped by the `low_ask` writer, so it cannot vouch for `highest_offer`; the note I was replacing was right about that and I kept it. **Shipped: the step DISABLED (commented out, wiring preserved)** — safe independently of the open coverage question, because a decommissioned host cannot write either way. ⛔ **Left open and said so:** whether the on-chain writer matches the sweep’s coverage is **not measurable from this schema** (no per-column stamp), and re-pointing the route at Atlas is the real work.

**Close-out:** rebased onto a concurrent session's two commits (ledger + INDEX conflicts both re-spliced into upstream's copy at a line-start anchor, never hand-edited), both INDEX counts re-derived from disk rather than incremented, and the new `inbox-index-entries-sit-under-their-own-date` guard satisfied. Four memory files updated in place; the durable half promoted to [cron-and-schedulers.md](../reference/cron-and-schedulers.md).

## 2026-09-11 (into 20:5x PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — I asked for a double-check and got four of my own claims back, including the headline finding

**Trevor:** "Double check this" · "Keep going. Don't stop for another hour" · "Keep going until you've exhausted all you can do and there's nothing left unresolved" · "Keep going until there's nothing left for you to do, then update memory and relevant documentation before I archive the thread"

Everything durable is promoted — nothing below is the only copy. Homes: **#81** restated in [known-issues.md](../reference/known-issues.md) · the migrations-are-a-findings-archive rule in [cron-and-schedulers.md](../reference/cron-and-schedulers.md) · the fixer/presence-guard pair in [testing-and-ci.md](../reference/testing-and-ci.md) · the two git shapes in [ledger-discipline.md](../reference/ledger-discipline.md) · the fabricated-timestamp rule in [tooling-gotchas.md](../reference/tooling-gotchas.md) · the `*_at` retraction in [database.md](../reference/database.md).

⭐⭐ **"Double check this" cost me the previous session's headline, and it deserved to.** I had written that `offers-sweep` is Top Shot's only writer of `edition_offers.highest_offer`. I had established that by grepping `app/` and `lib/`. **Two writers live in the DATABASE**: `raise_edition_offers_from_chain()` (pg_cron 216, hourly, a **ratchet** — `GREATEST(...)`, can only raise) and `sync_edition_offers_from_atlas()` (via `atlas_listing_verify_tick`, pg_cron **466, every 2 min**, authoritative — sets absolutely and NULLs gone offers). ⛔ **A grep over the repo is not a census of writers; a trigger or a pg_cron function has no textual caller.** ⚠ **And my attempt to size the residue was an artifact killed by its own control:** "shows an offer with no open row in `offers`" read 329/1,338 = **24.6%** for the never-verified cohort but **2,273/5,912 = 38.4%** for the *verified* one — **higher**, so the predicate measured `offers`-table coverage, not staleness. **No measured user-facing wrongness.** What survives is the masking: both Top Shot marketplace-GQL lanes died 08-28 and two DB writers hid it for 12 days.

⭐ **What actually shipped, each one measured first.**

- **`check_zero_yield_lanes()` flagged a lane that wrote 378 rows** (`20260912012605`). The detector scored on `rows_found` alone; `rows_found` and `rows_written` have **different semantics per lane** (#70), so a writer-only lane reads as dead. Split into `candidates` (found-based) and `hits` (found-based **and** `written_recent = 0`), and published `excluded_by_writes` so the exclusion is **visible, not silent**. Same signature → **ACL preserved**; verified anon/authenticated EXECUTE false, service_role true, exactly **1** overload, `check_secdef_anon_exec_drift()` length 0. ⚠ **Then I over-claimed it:** at the sentinel's production threshold (`min_runs=50`) `excluded_by_writes` is **0** — the false positive was only reachable at `min_runs=3`. Scope-corrected in the same ledger section rather than left standing.
- **`offers-sweep` added as the eleventh backstopped lane**, placed **last** in the `ingest` job so it cannot starve the alert lanes. Idempotency checked before wiring, not assumed: cursored, and `.upsert(onConflict: "collection_id,external_id")`.
- **`scripts/check-lane-egress.mjs` + 18 mutation tests**, wired into `ci.yml`. It classifies every backstopped lane `pure-db` vs `needs-egress` by **transitive import walk**, counting **call sites, not URL strings**; the lane list is derived from the workflow file, and **zero lanes parsed is RED**. The naive check (grep the route file) returns 0 for four of eleven lanes and is **wrong on two**. Exactly **two** lanes reach no external network.
- **Two red `main`s that were not mine**: the anon-exec marker, and RLS missing from two creating migrations (live state verified first — `relrowsecurity=true`, 0 policies, anon/auth SELECT false — so the appended `ALTER TABLE … ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` is idempotent and already true).
- **`__tests__/inbox-index-entries-sit-under-their-own-date.test.ts`** — see the instrument lesson below.

⛔ **FOUR CORRECTIONS OF MY OWN WORK, which is the honest shape of the session.** (1) #81 above. (2) **I conflated Disney Pinnacle with Panini** — wrote that three backfills serve "the only `is_active=false` collection"; they serve **`disney_pinnacle`, which is LIVE**. Corrected in the filing, the INDEX and the ledger. (3) **My expression-index prescription was refuted by a sibling's quiet-window BUFFERS reading** — and ⚠ **I had the refuting evidence in my own Addendum 3** (job 218's plan shows the identical `lower()` demotion) **and shipped the prescription anyway**. (4) **Four fabricated timestamps in one stretch** (07:20/06:55/18:24/18:52 against real 06:42/06:46/18:21/18:51). ⛔ **Not a zone error — numbers typed without reading a clock**, which the PT rule does not cover and now [tooling-gotchas.md](../reference/tooling-gotchas.md) does. Fixed by interpolating `$(TZ=America/Los_Angeles date …)` rather than typing.

⭐ **`sales-counterparty-backfill` fully diagnosed, and the fix order inverted by the measurement.** 104,600 null-seller rows sampled, **zero eligible** — all `allday_studio_history_v1` / `ufc_studio_history_v1`; the cursor has been pinned at **2024-04-19** for months. In a genuinely quiet window (1 active backend, 0 IO waiters): `shared hit=192758 read=2806`, **`Rows Removed by Filter` 123,132 + 98,051**, **4,324 ms**, `actual rows=0`, **286×/day**. ⛔ **This confirmed my index prescription and produced a better finding than the index:** the lane is **permanently exhausted**, and the cursor only advances when rows are claimed. **An index alone makes the waste cheap and permanent.** Fix the exhausted state first.

⭐ **The instrument lesson, and it is the one I would keep if I could keep one.** My misfiled INDEX entry (PT date in my head, `2026-09-12T0117Z` in the filename) went under the wrong heading and **nothing went red**: the presence guard checks **membership**, and `fix-inbox-index-counts.mjs` recomputes each heading's count **from the lines under it**, so it derived a count that **agreed with the misplacement** and laundered it into consistency. ⛔ **Two green instruments are not two pieces of evidence when one consumes the other's output — and an auto-fixer is the form of that nobody recognises, because a fixer does not look like a monitor.** Shipped a guard that reads the **filename** instead: measured over **431** entries, headings from **2026-08-29** on are **127 entries, ZERO mismatches** (a ban at zero), with the mixed 08-22..08-28 window (**63**, every one exactly **+1 day** — a UTC/PT boundary, not a typo) held as a **shrink-only ratchet** so repairing history can never red it. Mutation-proven both ways.

⚠ **Two git shapes that each cost real work.** **In a `stash pop`, `--theirs` is the STASH** — the opposite of a merge — and it silently clobbered a concurrent session's INDEX entry; caught only by a **set comparison against disk** before committing, because I was adding an entry while destroying one and **the count held**. And a splice assertion **correctly refused a non-unique anchor while the commit ran anyway** — separate steps instead of `&&`-joined — producing a commit message describing content the tree lacked.

⭐ **Two estate questions closed on the way.** A sibling's unexplained **12Z burst**: 16 active six-hourly jobs converge on 0/6/12/18Z against `max_worker_processes = 6`, and four failed at **600–601 s — a cap, not a distribution**. And a live **saturation spell**: 34 active backends **all** `IO:DataFileRead`, a 39-minute autovacuum over 3.25 GB of `wallet_moments_cache` (1,025 passes, 4.2% dead), kill rates `panini-ingest` 46% / `fmv-recalc` 62%, and user-facing edition pages returning **status 0** on statement timeouts.

⛔ **`supabase/migrations/` is a findings archive nobody greps.** I measured two facts about the Pinnacle backfills that `20260818040426_…_cadence_cut.sql` had stated **24 days earlier**, verbatim, including *"the `LIMIT 50000` NEVER BINDS"*. The migration is committed and greppable; its **filename describes its DDL, not its finding**, which is why it goes unread. Grep the **body** before measuring any pg_cron job.

**#79 triage closed**: offenders **5 → 2** — one false positive fixed in code, two suppressed with evidence and re-check conditions, the remaining two both filed.

**Close-out.** 12 code/doc commits. Every ledger splice re-read from disk and anchored at line-start `^### `; guards **3** swallowed / **0** future-dated. ⛔ **`CLAUDE.md` was already at 39,998 of 40,000 chars, so nothing went into it** — the one rule it gained (`A TABLE's WRITERS the same — grep the DB`) **displaced** the over-stated `*_at` claim rather than spending room, which is the displacement rule working as intended.

## 2026-09-10 (close-out PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — I set out to backstop a second lane, the measurement said don't, and the thread ended on a 13-day user-facing staleness nobody's instrument could see

The June-834-vs-July-0 shape that looked like an outage was a since-deleted subscription.

6. **#81 — ~~the real finding~~ RETRACTED AND RESTATED 2026-09-11 02:1x PT, after Trevor said "double check this".** ~~`offers-sweep` is Top Shot's only writer of `edition_offers.highest_offer`~~ — **false.** I grepped `app/` and `lib/`; **two writers live in the DATABASE**: `raise_edition_offers_from_chain()` (pg_cron 216, hourly, a ratchet that can only RAISE) and `sync_edition_offers_from_atlas()` (via `atlas_listing_verify_tick`, pg_cron 466, **every 2 min**, authoritative — sets absolutely and NULLs gone offers). Atlas covers 5,912 of 7,250 offer-bearing rows inside 7 days, none older than 7 days. **And my attempt to size the residue was an artifact** — the no-change control read *higher* (38.4%) on the healthy cohort than on the suspect one (24.6%), so the predicate measured `offers`-table coverage, not staleness. **No measured user-facing wrongness.** ⭐ **What survives is better, and the backstop I shipped is what found it:** its 00:28:47 PT tick wrote `Top Shot GraphQL failed with 530`; `topshot-deal-floor-serials` last wrote 2026-08-28 too, so **both Top Shot marketplace-GQL lanes died the same day** — same helper, same URL, different QUERY (`searchMintedMoments` works, `searchMarketplaceEditions` 530s). ## 2026-09-10 (close-out PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — the sweep metrics I shipped at the start of the night answered the question the night started with, and the answer is "not with code"

**Trevor:** "Keep going and work through anything unresolved... then commit what you need to memory"

### The measurement that closes the thread

The thread opened with *"how do we keep driving the KPIs that gate the roadmap"*. With the sweep-completeness metrics live (shipped earlier tonight, migration `20260910230812`), the gated collections read for the first time **with their position attached** — 20:42 PT leg:

| | HIGH/MED (all) | swept 24 h | HIGH/MED among swept |
|---|---|---|---|
| Top Shot | 52.5% | 77.0% | **68.1%** |
| All Day | 25.8% | 55.2% | **46.7%** |

⭐ **In both, the unswept cohort is ~0% HIGH/MED**, so the headline is arithmetically `sweep% × fresh-rate`. **Which makes "finish the sweep" look like a free +21 points on All Day.**

🚨 **It is not, and I checked before writing it down.** Cohorted against the LIVE `sales` table (not the recorded `sales_count_30d`, which is as stale as the snapshot carrying it): **the stale cohort is 2,806 editions with 2 HIGH/MED and only 6 holding ≥5 sales in 30 days.** The tail is unswept *because it is illiquid*; sweeping it moves ~6 editions, **+0.1 pt**. And the other lever is bounded too — **91** fresh editions carry ≥5 sales/30d without being HIGH/MED, so a perfect rule-side lift is **~+1.5 pt → ~29%**, short of the bar. ⚠ 91 is a proxy (the real rule re-fetches thin editions over 90 d and corroborates asks at 3), so it is an order of magnitude, not a target.

⛔ **So M2 is gated on All Day sales volume, not on our code — both code levers together are ~+1.6 pt.** That agrees with the 09-09 sizing (173 eligible → 54 real → +0.9 pt) reached by a completely different route, which is the only reason I trust it.

⚠ **And M1 should be stated as a RANGE: 48–55%.** The same day produced 47.9 twice and 52.5–54.6 tonight on cursor position alone. **A single leg is neither a pass nor a fail**, and 68.1% is the ceiling the pricing logic can actually reach.

### What went into memory, and what it cost

CLAUDE.md is at **39,975 / 40,000**. One rule earned its place — the honesty **MIRROR**: *an `unknown` that is actually KNOWN is the same defect*. Tonight's watchdog said "cannot tell" seven minutes after the proof it needed had landed; the canon is almost always invoked against a failed read rendering as a fact, and the reflex that follows is to widen `unknown`. **Paid for by three trims, none of them a rule:** a stale TS reading (which also carried a UTC stamp in memory, against the PT rule), the Candy/Panini tail compressed to register numbers, and one over-long pointer.

⚠ **The link guard caught me immediately** — quoting a CLAUDE.md line verbatim into `docs/reference/` carries a repo-root path that resolves wrong from there. **That is the exact bug the guard was written for, and it has now bitten the same way twice in one session.**

### Also closed tonight

- **21 UTC stamps in `go-live-2026-09.md`** converted to PT-first with the UTC original in parentheses; the bare `HH:MMZ` forms that remain are **sweep-leg identifiers** (database row names, not reports of a time) and the file now states that convention explicitly.
- The daytime monitor's carried-forward watch — *does the GHA backstop self-fire?* — **closed with the bad answer** (dispatch-only; 0 of 27 slots), recorded in the inbox index and register #80.

## 2026-09-10 (late night PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — GitHub's scheduler went to ZERO for 2.8 hours, and the reason nobody knew is that every watcher in the estate runs on the thing that broke

**Trevor:** "Keep going"

### What shipped

- **`public.rpc_gha_schedule_watchdog()` on pg_cron `8,38`** (migration `20260911045500`) — the first watcher in this estate that does **not** depend on GitHub. Plus a `gha-schedule-watchdog` watchlist row so the GHA sentinel alarms if *pg_cron* stops: two independent schedulers, each watching the other.
- **`extra.event` on both heartbeat writers** (`pipeline-sentinel.yml`, `dead-lane-backstop.yml`) carrying `github.event_name`. ⭐ **This is the whole measurement:** a heartbeat recorded that a workflow RAN but never WHICH TRIGGER ran it, so a `schedule` tick and a hand-fired dispatch wrote **identical rows** — and on the night the only runs that existed were dispatches.
- **A tree-walk guard** (`__tests__/gha-schedule-watchdog-covers-every-tagged-heartbeat.test.ts`, 13 tests) asserting the watchdog's lane list and the workflow tree agree **in both directions**, and executing both shipped `run:` bodies rather than grepping them. Two mutations run, two caught.
- Docs: known-issues **#80**, the stall + watchdog design in `cron-and-schedulers.md`, one rule promoted into CLAUDE.md by displacement.

### What I measured

**Scheduled delivery was not degraded — it was OFF.** Baseline over the 7 hours to 18:30 PT: **~4.3 scheduled runs/hour**, hourly range 1–6. From 18:30 to 21:11 PT, ~27 slots across four high-frequency workflows: **zero**. Three controls, because "GitHub is broken" is the easy story: Actions itself was healthy (three dispatches and every push CI run succeeded in the window); `status=queued` held exactly **one** run and it was created **2026-08-19**, so ticks were dropped and not delayed; and **cron-job.org was alive** — the three `*-sales-indexer` lanes logged 8–9 runs each. ⭐ That pins the blast radius to exactly *lanes whose only live caller is a GHA schedule*.

A concurrent session on Trevor's box measured the long-run rate the same night — **27% of an hourly cron over 191.9 hours, median gap 4.06h** — which I used rather than the older 16-of-48 figure, and which **changed a decision**: see the threshold below.

### Three things worth keeping

1. **An alarm sharing a scheduler with its subject is not an alarm.** `pipeline-sentinel`, `ops-monitor`, `e2e-smoke` and `scheduler-liveness` are all GHA `schedule`s, so the lanes and every instrument watching them stopped together. A 2.8-hour outage of 13+ lanes was found by a session happening to look. **This is the rule that went into CLAUDE.md**, and it is the second time in one day a whole detection layer turned out to be downstream of the thing it watches (the first was #76, where a Vercel pause took out the HTTP detectors).
2. **A threshold is a false-alarm budget, so it is computed, not chosen — and mine was wrong first.** I wrote 3 hours. At 27% delivery and 4 slots/hour that is 12 slots, p = 0.73¹² ≈ 2.3% for an all-miss run, and with 96 starting positions a day **~2 false alarms every day**. 6 hours is 24 slots, p ≈ 5e-4. ⛔ The cost is stated rather than hidden: **a stall publishes ~6h in.** The tick counts are written every tick regardless — the flag is the alarm, the counts are the instrument.
3. 🚨 **The first tick rebuilt this repo's own honesty defect inside the instrument meant to prevent it.** A **total** stall writes no tagged heartbeat at all — nothing runs, so nothing writes one — which classified as `unknown`, and with `ok` keyed on `stalled` alone **the single worst case would have written `ok = true` forever** while the estate's only scan (`ok = false`) saw nothing. ⭐ **I found that by RUNNING it and reading the row, not by reasoning about the code I had just written.** Fixed: an unknown has a shelf life — deploy artifact for 12h, BROKEN PROBE after that.

### Two measurement notes

- ⭐ **The obvious predicate was the expensive one.** `WHERE pipeline LIKE '%-heartbeat'` cannot use `pipeline_runs_pipeline_started_idx` and seq-scans the whole table: **3,618 buffers / 147 ms against 11 buffers / 2.8 ms** for an `IN (...)` list — **329×** — and ⚠ **narrowing the time window does not help, because the seq scan reads every page either way.** Measured before scheduling, because this instance's saturation is IO-bound.
- ✅ **Proven it can see a failure rather than assumed:** a synthetic `schedule`-tagged heartbeat at −7h produced `stalled` / `ok = false` / an error naming 420 min / `rows_*` NULL, with the no-tagged-rows case as the negative control in the same pass. All five test rows deleted afterwards. ⚠ The 3-arg `log_pipeline_run` **COALESCEs `rows_*` to 0** — a fabricated zero for anything that measures nothing — so the 11-arg form with explicit NULLs is the only correct call for a heartbeat or a watchdog.

### Still open, and it is Trevor's

🚨 **Re-enabling the cron-job.org entries for the ten lanes is the only lever that restores a 5-minute cadence** — the watchdog **records**, it does not **deliver** (no Telegram/email: those tokens are not reachable from a sandbox) and it does not restore cadence. ⚠ And the mechanism behind the stall is **not established**: `githubstatus.com` is egress-blocked here and the repo is public (free standard runners), so a GitHub-side incident and repo-level deprioritisation under the night's heavy push volume are indistinguishable from this side. The defence is a caller GitHub does not throttle, which is what shipped.

## 2026-09-10 (PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — I shipped the one lever M2 had, then spent the rest of the session finding out that most of what I measured about it was a sampling artifact

**Trevor:** "How can you continue to work on driving and increasing our key metrics and KPIs" → "make your own decisions" → "Keep going"

### What shipped

- **Ask-corroboration reaches All Day** (`app/api/fmv-recalc/route.ts`, Step 2a-ter(c)). It was fed only by Top Shot's `edition_offers`, so 461 TS editions held MEDIUM on that path and All Day held **zero** — while the same All Day floor ask was *already* trusted in the same route to CAP a sales-derived FMV and to be the SOLE price signal for 1,560 ASK_ONLY editions. **The state was internally inconsistent; that was the finding, not the missing feature.**
- **The trust-health leg publishes its own sweep completeness** (migration `20260910230812`): `<c>_fmv_sweep_pct_24h` + `<c>_fmv_high_med_fresh24h_pct`, folded into the `DISTINCT ON` pass the leg already makes so it costs no extra scan.
- Docs: #76 (P0), #70/#78 notes, go-live M1+M2 rows, and three rules promoted into memory.

### The thing worth remembering: I was wrong three times, in three different ways

1. **I reported "M2 moved +4.9 points, read at the bar."** The next leg read 23.0 and the series went **22.59 → 24.07 → 28.3 → 23.0 in nine hours**. ⭐ The metric reads the LATEST snapshot per edition, and recalc walks the liquid head before the illiquid tail, so the whole-estate share swings 5–7 points on **cursor position**. 28.3 was a favourable sampling point. **The M1 row in go-live already documented this mechanism and I read a single leg as a settled gain anyway.**
2. **I over-corrected my own sizing.** Having claimed 449, I downgraded it to "somewhere between 223 and 449" on a mechanism I had not measured. Measured, the 30-day-map condition costs **~13 %**, not the ~50 % the 223 implied. **The pessimistic correction was as unmeasured as the optimistic estimate it replaced.**
3. **I re-made a misreading the ledger had corrected an hour earlier.** `get_project` → `live: false` plus both apex hosts missing from `domains` **reads identically on a healthy estate**; I reported the site down while it had been up 2.4 hours. **Verify platform state by making a request, not by reading a status field.**

### What actually measured the change

The **matched control** — treated (ask inside the ±25 % band) vs control (outside it), two groups differing only by the predicate the fix touches. It is immune to sweep position because both face the same cursor: **83.2 % vs 13.0 %**. ⭐ **The mechanism's effect was established; the gate's level was not, and I conflated them.** The honest decomposition: +317 editions lifted (+5.1 pts) against ~−4.5 pts of tail-correction = **net +0.6 points on M2**.

### Two things I declined, with the number that would reopen each

- **The 30-day-window second lever.** 173 editions looked eligible (+2.79 pts, clearing the bar) — **119 were already MEDIUM**, so the real gain is 54 and +0.87 pts. ⭐ **An eligibility count is not a gain count.** Reopen if the already-in-target share falls well below ~69 %.
- **A sentinel arm for the new metrics.** No non-noisy threshold exists (a low sweep % is information, not a fault); the one alertable shape needs a curated per-collection list; and the outage it would catch is already covered by the pipeline-silence arms. Reopen if a collection ever stops sweeping *without* those arms firing.

### And one P0 found by accident

A hard smoke failure appeared on my commit. It was not mine — smoke had been red for 7 hours — and reading the failing job found `DEPLOYMENT_PAUSED`. ⭐ **Three detectors, three blind spots, composing into silence:** the sentinel is itself an HTTP route so it 503s *before* `log_pipeline_run` and leaves no row (looking "silent", not "failing"); `alerts-dispatch`/`alerts-send` sit behind the same paused deployment; and `Scheduler liveness` is **daily**, last ran an hour before the event, blind for ~23 hours. **I did not unpause it** — the pause may have been the intent, against a $412/cycle bill and a "no infra spend pre-revenue" rule, and I could read neither billing nor prod. It recovered on its own; all three listing lanes are ingesting again, Golazos for the first time in 7.4 days.

### Promoted to memory

`verify platform STATE by a REQUEST, never a status field` · `an ELIGIBILITY count is not a GAIN count` · and into `tooling-gotchas.md`, **a run's START, not its row timestamps, decides which side of a deploy it is on** (a run that began 3 s before the alias switched wrote old-code rows into my "post-deploy" window and produced a 1-of-12 false refutation).

## 2026-09-10 (night PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — the alarm was mute, and every finding tonight came from RUNNING something rather than reading it

**Trevor:** "Keep going and plan on working autonomously for the next 3 hours"

### The one habit that produced everything else

I dispatched `Pipeline Sentinel` to verify an unrelated change end-to-end. That single real invocation produced **four** findings that no amount of reading the code would have surfaced: the Telegram 400, the fact that email has never delivered, the proof that the site was already back, and the lane split. ⭐ **Reading tells you what the code intends; running tells you what it does.** I keep re-learning this and it keeps paying.

### The alarm's delivery probability falls as the incident gets worse

The sentinel ran 18 checks, concluded **CRITICAL**, and got back `telegram-FAILED:http_400 … "message is too long"` with `email-FAILED:not_configured` beside it. **Both out-of-band channels failed on the same run.** The mechanism is the nastiest shape an alarm can have: the message is one line per check, `Pipeline Silence` names every silent lane, Telegram caps text at 4096 characters and **rejects rather than truncates** — so the message grows with the incident and the alarm is least likely to arrive on exactly the runs that matter.

Fixed as a class (three unbounded senders, bound applied **inside** each sender so no call site can be copied without it). The contract I held myself to was not *"it fits"* but *"it fits AND the reader can tell what was removed"* — dropped criticals are named, because a bare count lets someone believe they saw everything.

Then I built the arm that reads the delivery record the route has written **since 08-29 with nobody ever looking at it**. Its first finding: `email-FAILED:not_configured` on **18 of 18** runs. The estate has been single-channel this whole time. Setting the env is Trevor's — it is off-limits here — and until it is set, one Telegram failure is a total alerting outage.

### The arm shipped wrong once and the deep battery caught it

All 8 of my new cases passed. The full suite did not: 7 cases in `api-sentinel-deep` went red because the arm warned on an all-green fixture. I had **collapsed two different zeroes** — *no sentinel runs at all* (a real defect) and *runs with nothing to say* (a healthy estate). Warning on the second is the "must be satisfiable at a population of zero" shape; the arm was punishing its own success. ⚠ **`npx vitest run <file>` would have shipped it.**

### Two of #76's three P0 readings read identically on a healthy estate

The site came back on its own between 21:46Z and 00:01Z. Those were two of the three readings the P0 rested on. The only one that was ever evidence is the sentinel's **503 `DEPLOYMENT_PAUSED` body — a direct observation of a request.** The repo's "verify by rendered DOM, never HTTP 200" rule, one level up: **verify platform state by making the request, not by reading a status field.**

### Testing the stated exit condition instead of re-reading it

#76 left a test — *do the lanes resume on their next fire?* Two did, exactly on schedule. Eleven did not, and they are the ones on **1–15 minute** cadences: 43 to 641 missed ticks each. ### The one I got wrong: three code commits with red CI

I pushed three code commits tonight and **every one of them had a failing CI run**. I verified each with `npm test` (16,599 → 16,610 → 16,619 tests, green) and `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean), wrote "full suite green" in the commit message, and pushed. The docs-only commits in between passed, so the badge history looked alternating rather than broken, and I did not look until the end of the window.

**One job of nineteen was red: `ESLint ratchet`** — the one gate neither local command covers. `npm test` runs vitest and nothing else; `tsc --noEmit` is types only. The offender was a single `let` that is never reassigned, in a harness I wrote. And the ratchet is **per-rule, not per-total**: `prefer-const grew 15 -> 16` while the total read **717 against a baseline of 717**, so a total-only check would have waved the broken tree through.

⚠ CLAUDE.md already says *"`npx vitest run <file>` proves the FILE, not the tree — run the full suite before pushing."* I did run the full suite. **The full suite is not the full gate**: here it is `npm test` AND `npx tsc --noEmit` AND `npm run lint:ratchet`. The third existed the whole time and I never ran it once. ⭐ **And the deeper habit error is the one I want to keep: I read my own local green as CI's verdict instead of reading CI** — which is the same mistake, in miniature, as the `live: false` correction I wrote three hours earlier. **Verify by making the request, not by reading the status you expect.**

#75's open question came back **FROZEN, not climbing** — `n_dead_tup` sat at exactly the value my own ANALYZE set while ~600 rows aged out — so the scheduled job was required rather than optional.
