## 2026-09-09 (early morning PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — I retracted my own #69 a day after filing it, and shipped the two instrument fixes it exposed

**Trevor:** "Do it all"

### The retraction is the session

Yesterday I filed #69 as *"the client-error beacon's first REAL finding — a production defect nothing else would have seen"*: 17 unhandled `SyntaxError: failed to parse` rejections on `/nba-top-shot/collection`, across six deployment ids, in one day. I put it in the register, in CLAUDE.md and in the go-live doc.

Today, one `GROUP BY metadata->>'ua'` returned **one value for all 17: `Lightpanda/1.0`** — a headless automation client. No `Mozilla/` prefix, which every mainstream consumer browser has; constant `width: 1280`, which fits a headless default and not 17 screens. **It is almost certainly that engine's own incomplete JS support choking on our bundle, and there is no evidence a human ever hit it.**

⭐⭐ **The rule this cost: when an instrument's first finding is SURPRISING, establish WHO generated it before believing WHAT it says.** I grouped by message, path, stack and deployment id — four fields — and shipped a conclusion, **while the one field that falsifies it sat in the same JSON object I was already querying.** I had even listed `ua` in a `jsonb_object_keys` result and moved past it. ⚠ A `count(*)` over an open endpoint counts *requests*, not *readers*, and `/api/telemetry` is anon-public by design.

### Both fixes, and an honest note about the first one

**`sid`** — a random per-tab id in `sessionStorage`, so same-`sid` rows are repeat loads in one tab and distinct `sid`s are distinct tabs. Not localStorage, not a cookie: per-tab and cleared on close is a discriminator, a visitor id is a different thing with different obligations. Blocked storage *throws* rather than returning null, so it falls back to a module id instead of raising inside the error handler.

⚠ **But `sid` does not solve the problem that actually bit.** A crawler gets a `sid` like anything else. It answers *how many tabs* when the question that mattered was *is there a human here at all*. **A correct fix to the question I asked, and the wrong fix for the question I should have asked.**

**The alert arm** — this is the one that could have paged someone. `client_error_burst` counted every row with no UA predicate, firing at `paths >= 5 OR count(*) >= 25`; the crawler produced **17 of the 25 needed** to raise an alert on traffic with no human in it. Now filtered on a *structural* property (`ua NOT LIKE 'Mozilla/%'`) rather than a bot list, because a guard that names its instances dies on a rename — three have here.

⚠ **The polarity was the real design decision: a MISSING `ua` counts as HUMAN.** Unknown is not automation, and the other polarity fails *silent* — a future beacon change that stopped sending `ua` would switch the alarm off with every gate green. ⛔ And excluded rows are not hidden: the detail carries `(+N from non-browser UAs, excluded)`.

### The control that mattered was the negative one

Four post-flight controls run inside the migration's transaction, so a failure rolls the splice back. The one worth naming is **(2): 30 rows from `Lightpanda/1.0` must NOT fire.** Without it, "the arm stopped firing" is equally consistent with a correct fix and with an arm that can never fire again — and I would have had no way to tell which I had shipped.

### Two smaller method notes

- ⭐ **Mutation testing caught my test, not just my code.** The first version of the `sid` test asserted tab-discrimination via `pageSessionId()` directly, so hardcoding `sid` in the *payload* was caught only by a sibling assertion. The test whose **title** claims the property has to be the one that fails when the property breaks; rewritten to drive the posted body.
- ⭐ **I tested egress instead of asserting it.** #70 needs an upstream read, and rather than repeat "sandbox egress is blocked" from memory I ran it: `curl` to the Flowty and Atlas hosts returns `000` through the agent proxy. The claim now rests on a measurement.

### CLAUDE.md is structurally full

This turn's one new rule took the file **38 characters over** the 40,000 limit. I funded it by trimming the *reference index* — navigational descriptions, the least rule-dense content in the file — rather than shaving the rule itself to fit. **Two sessions in one day have now had to cut something to add anything.** 39,895 / 40,000. The next substantive addition needs a deliberate restructure, not another squeeze.

## 2026-09-09 (early morning PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — part 2 of the doc refresh: All Day gets its register row, and I correct my own measurement from part 1

**Trevor:** "Keep going"

### The gap I left was the finding

Part 1 identified All Day's confidence slide (M2, **27.7 % → 20.2 %** against a ≥ 30 % bar, a real numerator loss on a fixed denominator) and wrote it into two strategy docs — but **never gave it a register row.** This register's own rule says an item that matters and is still open should have one, and that its absence is itself the finding. **#70 filed.** It is worth stating why the gap happened: M1 has a Routine, a ledger entry and a retraction attached to it, so it gets re-read constantly; M2 had nothing, and slid seven points across four readings unattended. **Instrumentation attracts attention, and the un-instrumented metric is where the drift hides.**

### I violated a CLAUDE.md rule in the same pass that re-derived everything else properly

Part 1 stated the All Day decline as *"~333/day (08-19..24) → ~153/day (09-01..08)"*. That is **a delta between two hand-picked windows** — precisely what CLAUDE.md forbids: *a directional claim needs a DISTRIBUTION, not a snapshot*. I had re-derived every other number in that pass from the live system and still shipped the one shape the rules name.

The weekly series changes the reading. All Day: **2,578 · 6,772 · 2,984 · 2,626 · 1,609 · 2,479 · 3,012 → 1,291 (wk 08-24) · 938 (wk 08-31)**. That is an already-volatile base with a **STEP**, not a smooth halving — and the volatility matters, because two windows drawn from a series that swings 1,609–6,772 can be made to show almost anything. Corrected in both documents.

⭐ **The cross-collection control I then reached for was ambiguous, and recording it that way was the point.** On the two-window comparison *everything* is down (All Day −55.5 %, Top Shot −40.7 %, Candy −37.5 %), and Candy is genuinely independent — Solana, a separate ingest path sharing nothing with All Day's. That looks like corroboration for "market-wide". But Candy's weekly shape is a **monotonic decay from a late-July launch peak**, not a step, so **it does not corroborate**; it only shows a market-wide decline cannot be excluded. And Top Shot is unusable as a control at all here, because the #68 dedupe moved its baseline for an unrelated reason. **A control that fell for a different reason is not a control.**

### The instrument that actually settled something

`pipeline_runs` retains ~73 h, so it cannot see a change point two weeks back. **`pipeline_runs_daily` is indefinite**, and it carries `rows_found` alongside `rows_written`: for `allday-sales-indexer` the two **fall in lockstep** (~300–460/day through 08-24, then 51–264), with `rows_skipped` small and 0–2 failures a day. ⭐ **That splits the two hypotheses cleanly — the lane is finding less, not dropping what it finds.** ⛔ It does *not* reach the market: `rows_found` is what our query of the upstream returns, so an upstream coverage narrowing looks identical. That question needs a prod-side probe; sandbox egress is blocked.

⚠ Named rather than buried: the indexer's **run count fell ~100–110 → ~78/day around 08-26/27** and stayed there. Stated as a coincidence, not a cause — but it sits at the change point, nothing explains it, and if a per-run cap is being hit it becomes the cause.

### Displacement, done the safe way

The new CLAUDE.md rule (`rows_found` vs `rows_written`) was funded by carrying the #22 GC/rotate status and the #55 Routine detail down into the register. ⭐ **The load-bearing step was checking they were not already there** — they were not, so removing them from CLAUDE.md without adding them first would have destroyed both. **Displacement is only lossless if you verify the destination before you cut the source.** Headroom **39,805 / 40,000 (195 chars)**, against the 40 this pass inherited.

## 2026-09-08 (late evening PT) · Claude Code (cloud) — documentation refresh: M1 is at its bar for the wrong reason, All Day is sliding unwatched, and the beacon caught its first real defect

**Trevor:** "Can you refresh and update Claude.md, the roadmap, known issues, and any other stale documentation?"

A documentation pass, so the discipline was the point: **re-derive every claim the docs make, never re-quote it.** Three of the four things worth recording came out of that rule rather than out of the editing.

### M1 is right and misleading at the same time — the denominator moved, not the pricing

`topshot_fmv_high_med_share_pct` reads **50.7 %** against its 50 % bar, up from the 37.3 % the go-live doc still carried. Before writing that down I asked what moved it, and the answer was the 09-08 retirement of the non-canonical editions, not accuracy. **Measured against the retirement's own backup tables** — `audit_20260908_ts_noncanonical_fmv_snapshots` holds **6,426 priced editions, only 163 of them HIGH/MEDIUM** — so it took 6,426 off the denominator and 163 off the numerator. Rebasing 09-01 onto today's population (19,771 → 13,345 priced, 7,885 → 7,722 HIGH/MED) against today's 14,015 / 7,186: ⭐ **the count of Top Shot editions with a trustworthy price FELL by ~536 in the week the published share rose 11.4 points.**

The retirement was the right call and the denominator is now honest. But a **launch gate reading at its bar because dead rows left the denominator** is exactly the reassuring-direction rot this repo keeps paying for, and no surface said so. It now does — in the M1 row, in CLAUDE.md's go-live line, and in a new roadmap-status headline block. ⚠ Stated as a reading, not a trend: the post-drain sweep is 63 % complete and the +425 editions from the recovered Atlas sales are still propagating.

### All Day is the regression nobody was watching

M2 is **20.2 %** against a ≥ 30 % bar — 27.7 → 25.4 → 24.1 → **20.2** across four readings, and unlike M1 it is a **real numerator loss** (1,574 → 1,252 on an unchanged 6,190 denominator). ⭐ I measured the cause instead of asserting one: All Day **sales volume roughly halved** (~333/day 08-19..24 → ~153/day 09-01..08) while **every All Day lane is green** — `allday-sales-indexer` 156 runs / 156 ok / 348 rows in 48 h, tracking the observed volume. ⛔ That rules out a broken lane. It does **not** establish that the market halved: an upstream coverage change looks identical from inside, and it is the wrong direction for the start of the NFL season. **The next probe is upstream, not another pipeline read.**

⭐ **This is the finding the pass would have missed had it only re-read the number everyone watches.** Top Shot's M1 is the metric with a Routine, a ledger entry and a retraction attached to it; All Day slid five points across three documented readings with nothing naming it.

### The beacon's first real catch — and it cannot size its own finding (#69)

The client-error beacon shipped 09-07 as B2's answer. Its entire history is **19 rows**: 2 synthetic probes and **17 real `unhandledrejection` / `SyntaxError: failed to parse` on `/nba-top-shot/collection`**, 01:17Z → 22:31Z on 09-08, across **six distinct deployment ids** — so it survives redeploys and is not a bad build. Nothing had read the table; I found it re-deriving a go-live row.

⚠ **Then the instrument defect, which matters more than the bug:** every row carries the literal `wallet_address = 'anon'`, with no session or visitor key. **17 events is consistent with one browser retrying seventeen times and with seventeen readers hitting it once** — a ~17× range on the only number that decides urgency. ⭐ **A detector that cannot attribute its events to distinct actors reports a COUNT that reads like an INCIDENCE.** M7's bar ("a detector exists and has caught a synthetic error") was met by an instrument carrying that hole, and it only became visible the first time the detector caught something real. Fix the discriminator first, or the parse-error fix's own falsifier — does the rate fall? — is unreadable for the same reason.

### #67 rotted in four hours, which is the fastest recorded here

Its item (2) told the reader the ~74,297 historical Atlas sales were "paced on jobid **481**" and to "retire 481 when `extra.remaining` reads 0". **481 was unscheduled the same night** after its first working tick caused an IO spell. Live: no matching `cron.job` row, 6,589 `atlas_backfill` rows and no more, `remaining` frozen at 74,297. ⭐ **Both halves were written by sessions that were correct when they wrote — the register carried the first and never the second**, leaving a reader waiting on a job that no longer exists and watching a number that can no longer move.

### Method notes worth keeping

- ⭐ **A share moving is not a numerator moving.** Both M1 and M2 changed this week; only one of them was about pricing. **Reconcile a rate against its own denominator's change before reporting a direction** — the backup tables the retirement wrote made that a one-query check.
- ⭐ **A sweep must be able to return "still open".** #8 (`sync-nba-projections`, 16 runs / 0 ok in 48 h) and #55 (both Routines `enabled: false`, last fire 09-01) were re-read and left open with evidence, not tidied away.
- ⚠ **CLAUDE.md's headroom is real.** The three corrections cost 191 chars against 67 available; they were paid for by **displacing** the ISR and `/sitemap/3.xml` instance details verbatim into `key-files-and-honesty.md`, keeping the rules in place. Final: **39,950 / 40,000**, measured with `node -e` + `.length`.

## 2026-09-08 (evening PT) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — 81,337 Top Shot sales were already in our own database, the gap was rarity-biased, and the drain I scheduled for the historical half had to be pulled

**Trevor:** "Do what you think is best. Exhaust all of your capabilities and leave nothing unresolved that you can handle yourself. Make decisions yourself based upon your best judgement along with what's best for RPC long term and for our user base."

### The decision that mattered: check the free sources before buying reads

#67(2) proposed a per-edition Atlas history walk — ~14 K reads, ~2 days, competing with live lanes already running a 16–23 % Cloudflare 403 rate. ⭐ **Before spending any of it I asked what we already held, and `topshot_atlas_market_events` had 81,337 completed nba listing sales with no `sales` row**, every one already mapped to an `rpc_edition_id`. The firehose had paid for them; nobody had converted them.

🚨 **The gap was RARITY-BIASED, which is the finding — not the row count.** Share of each tier's sales that had been missing: **COMMON 19.1 % → FANDOM 28.5 % → RARE 34.5 % → LEGENDARY 46.3 % → ULTIMATE 55.9 %.** The mechanism is plain: the on-chain indexer resolves editions through the catalogue, and the rarest and newest editions are the least catalogued, so their sales fell through to `unresolved` and were dropped. ⭐ **A collector pricing a Legendary or Ultimate was seeing an FMV computed on barely half its evidence — on exactly the Moments where a pricing error costs the most real money.** It also retroactively raises the value of the parking fix shipped earlier: the sales that indexer was discarding were disproportionately the expensive ones.

**Result: 6,589 rows recovered into the live FMV window; editions at MEDIUM-or-better 4,301 → 4,726 (+425, +9.9 %)**, computed both ways from one query by excluding `source='atlas_backfill'`.

### Three things that would have done damage, all caught before they did

⛔ **The obvious dedup would have undone another session's work.** The live lane's ±10 min guard let **4,644 candidates through that matched rows the #68 dedup had DELETED hours earlier** — Atlas's `purchased_at` aligns with the deleted `topshot_gql` timestamp while the surviving on-chain row sits further away, so a ±10 min probe finds nothing live and the candidate looks free when it *is* the same sale. ⭐ **"No matching row exists" is not "this row belongs" — a row can be absent because someone deliberately removed it.** Key re-derived by measurement on 2,000 of exactly those: `(nft_id, price)` **77 %**, `(nft_id, sold_at::date)` **100 %**. The day key ships, deliberately conservative — **under-counting our own accuracy is safer than inflating it.**

⛔ **A units error would have looked identical to the rarity skew.** `atlas_backfill` median 2.76 vs on-chain 0.30 could have been `price_cents` holding dollars. Verified: median backfill/other ratio **1.429** (not ~100), 3,777 of 3,929 within 5x, and the tail checks row-by-row (**$25,000 ← 2,500,000 cents, ULTIMATE, serial #1**).

⛔ **My `REVOKE … FROM PUBLIC` orphaned the cron job that calls the function.** First tick: `permission denied`. `SECURITY DEFINER` governs what a function RUNS AS, not who may CALL it. 🚨 And it presented as **silence** where anyone would look — the error fires before `log_pipeline_run`, so `cron.job_run_details` showed `failed` while **`pipeline_runs` showed nothing**. Rule now in `database.md`: pair the REVOKE with a GRANT to the job's role in the same migration, and for any "scheduled job wrote no row" the discriminator is `cron.job_run_details`, never the pipeline table.

### And one I did NOT catch until it was live

🚨🚨 **I scheduled the historical drain, it caused an IO spell, and I pulled it.** jobid 481's first working tick ran **246 s in `IO / DataFileRead`** and took the instance **1 active / 0 IO-waiters → 10 / 8**. Cancelled and unscheduled at ~00:52Z; back to **1 / 1 / 0** within three minutes.

⭐⭐ **Root cause is a rule this repo already has, and my own verification is what hid it: a `LIMIT` bounds a query's OUTPUT, not its COST.** The candidate CTE evaluates `NOT EXISTS` against `sales` for all ~209 K events *before* `LIMIT 1500` applies. My manual runs measured **38 s** — but only because I passed `p_since = now() - 30 days`, which bounded the scan. **The scheduled call passes no `p_since`. The one argument that made my verification cheap was absent from the thing I scheduled.** ⚠ **A verification that does not use the SAME ARGUMENTS as the scheduled caller is not a verification of it.** (Its `SET statement_timeout` did not save it either — function-level `SET` is inert on pg_cron, so it ran under `cron_heavy`'s 600 s.)

⛔ **Nothing was lost and nothing rolled back.** The FMV-window half is banked; only the ~74,297 historical rows are deferred, and those affect edition-page completeness rather than FMV — **not worth an IO spell.** The correct bounded design (a date-slice cursor, or a `p_until`) is specified in the unschedule migration's header so the next pass is a pickup, not a re-investigation. ⭐ Draining the FMV window *first and separately* is what made this recoverable rather than a rollback.

### Also this stretch

Declined to loosen the `pack_table_rows` slow-arm after refuting its candidate by measurement (the whole sweep was slow at that tick: 44-board average **662 ms vs 232–283**; an isolated re-probe of the exact query returned **166 ms**). **The arm did its job; tuning a safety threshold from one benign event is how instruments erode.** Documented the one-query discriminator in `trust-board-and-safety.md` instead, with the principled fixes named in order should it ever become genuinely noisy.

**Closing state:** job gone, 6,589 rows kept, instance 3 active / 1 IO-wait, security invariants `[]`, secdef drift 0, the only stalled pipeline the pre-existing `topshot-catalog-backfill` info item. Suite green (1,491 files / 16,528 tests), tree clean.

## 2026-09-08 (07:0x–12:0x PT) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — a register staleness sweep, the two indexes a monitor never named, and a wrap-up that found a live CLAUDE.md fact had quietly stopped being true

**Trevor:** "Keep going" ×3, then "Wrap up and handle anything unresolved, then commit what you need to memory and refresh relevant documentation."

### The register was rotting in the REASSURING direction

Went looking for a permanently-red instrument to fix and found there was nothing left to fix — **four times**. ⭐ The durable finding is the *shape*: a stale CLOSED entry gets caught the moment someone needs the thing, but **a stale OPEN entry looks like diligence**, so nobody re-checks it and every reader inherits the wrong priority. Three of the four had been fixed by *other* sessions whose work never came back to the register.

| item | claimed | live read | disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| **#38** | fails 99.6 % of ticks | **zero runs** in the whole retention window; jobid 16 `active = false` for 10 days | closed |
| **#31** | authoritative arm "never once produced a result" | reads **38 of 38** bundles, 0 failed, since 08-30 | closed |
| **#23** | 25 edge fns running non-`main` code | **6**, and all six deliberate (2 deferred-by-decision, 4 do-not-redeploy) | closed |
| **#21** | codify the hydrator's cron | worker logs **nothing**; pg_cron does the work (339/339, 99/99 ok) | **updated, not closed** |
| **#11** | named sub-item is a ghost | ghost confirmed — but it carries a REAL remainder | ⛔ **checked and LEFT OPEN** |

**#21 was deliberately not closed:** Cloudflare is unreadable from a session, so *retired* and *running-but-dying-before-its-first-log-write* are indistinguishable — the same ambiguity that item's own "24–49 of ~144 ticks log" finding described, now at 0 of ~144. Both states need the same operator action, so the action could move without resolving it. ⛔ **#11 is the half of a sweep that usually goes unrecorded** — an old item is not a stale item, and **a sweep that cannot return "still open" is a closing spree.** Both facts are now in the register's stamp and as a standing rule near the top of the Open section.

### The five-lane finding that fell out of closing #38

The 2026-08-29 dead-host mitigation was applied to **one** job and never swept across its siblings. **Five lanes still fire into `public-api.nbatopshot.com`** — `ingest`, `topshot-subedition-circulation-backfill`, `ingest-topshot-challenges`, `topshot-pack-supply-backfill`, `topshot-misattrib-drain` — at **0 ok and 0 rows written each** over the FULL retention window, `last_success` NULL throughout. ⛔ **Paused nothing:** `pack_distributions` is provably fresh (1,995 of 2,099 in 24 h), so nothing is going stale, but **the path keeping it fresh could not be identified** (ruled out: pg_cron, the DB writers' in-DB callers, this box's Task Scheduler) — and pausing something whose live path you have not mapped is how a retry arm disappears. Filed with per-lane disposition. ⭐ **Transferable: a mitigation applied to ONE job is a claim about every sibling sharing its upstream — sweep by UPSTREAM** (`error ILIKE '%530%'` over full retention, one query).

⚠⚠ **A method trap that nearly became a false finding of mine:** grouping `net._http_response` by `status_code` and sampling with **`max(content)`** returns the **lexicographically largest** body. It surfaced `product is required` (**2 of 1,635**) and buried the real **1,633** — benign Flow REST `Error Code: 1101` moved-Moment responses, already attributed. I burned several queries chasing a phantom Atlas bug. **Group by `left(content, N)` and count.**

### #56 — the two indexes the monitor never named

Its two headline claims were stale (four reindex jobs exist; the verify ran green 09-06) but **its rate was right** — and I nearly filed it as falsified. `idx_wmc_cohort_cover` read 326.4 MB on 09-02 and 269.9 MB today, which reads as *"the bloat stopped"*; **a REINDEX ran in between.** Split on that change point: **148.4 → 269.9 MB ≈ 60 MB/day**, matching the item. ⭐ **A rate measured across its own fix is not a rate**, and it lied in the reassuring direction.

🚨 **The real gap was one neither claim named:** the verify hardcoded a **four-name** `v_targets` array and was structurally silent about every other index on the table — and **the two it never named are the two that grew largest**: `idx_wmc_lock_wallet_coll_cover` **433.5 MB / 44.48 %** and `idx_wmc_wallet_coll_ek_fmv` **401.6 MB / 37.18 %**, both under the verify's *own* 60 % threshold, against 83.7–90.5 % on the four covered. ≈**430 MB** recoverable; the second is the table's second-hottest index (18.4 M scans) on an IO-bound instance. ⭐ **The unnamed objects grow worst BECAUSE they are unnamed** — everything in the list is maintained on a schedule, everything outside it accumulates without limit, so a curated list actively selects for its blind spot being the worst. Checked first that all 19 wmc indexes are USED, so there was no drop to make instead.

**Shipped** jobs 477/478 on the existing one-statement `REINDEX INDEX CONCURRENTLY` / `cron_heavy` pattern, `v_targets` extended to six, verify moved 03:23 → 04:03 (load-bearing: it would otherwise collide *and* measure the new targets before their rebuild, reporting a false red weekly). **#56 marked 🟠 partial, not closed** — the new targets first run **2026-09-13** and the change was verified structurally only, because running the verify now costs ~1.9 GB of reads, the exact cost that item declined to re-spend. **Watch: 09-13 ~04:03Z, six entries in `extra.indexes`, `invalid_left` empty.**

### Two self-inflicted problems, both caught

🔁 **I created repo/prod drift and it would have survived every gate.** `apply_migration` takes SQL inline, so the applied text is hand-assembled while the file is written separately; I trimmed an in-body comment out of the MCP call (`5c45be6d` deployed vs `540afbb7` committed). Nothing misbehaved — **the committed file simply stopped describing production**, which is a migration's only job. ⛔ `migration-parity` matches on NAME and this function is not in `PINS`, so **green parity + green drift guard is compatible with a file that does not match prod.** Now a rule in `tooling-gotchas.md`: md5 `pg_proc.prosrc` against the committed body after every `apply_migration`.

⚠⚠ **And my md5 extractor reported a FALSE drift** because the new file names the dollar-quote tag *in its own prose*, so splitting on it returned the wrong slice (1390 vs the real 3034). Anchor on the LAST marker pair. ⭐ **A verifier that can be confused by the text it verifies is not a verifier.**

### Wrap-up: the documentation refresh found live drift

Committed the weekly data-quality sweep left untracked by a NO-PUSH session (read in full first; its offer-reconciliation item independently reaches the same conclusion as my 0530Z filing — the offer-sanity set is ~100 % sub-serial, which is why it cannot drain). Its one documentation ask was **regenerate `schema-truth.md`, 17 days stale** — and doing so found **real drift in the row that file exists to protect**: `public.collections` is no longer 5 active / 2 inactive but **6 / 1**, because `candy_mlb` flipped to `is_active = true` when Candy went live (#63). **CLAUDE.md still read "both are the `is_active=false` rows".** Corrected — and the replacement is **27 chars shorter**, so it reclaimed headroom (39,960 → 39,933 of 40,000). Other counts re-read: public tables 376 → **423**, views 136 → **137**, functions 668 → **731**, active cron jobs 99 → **133**; invariants all hold (0 RLS-off, 0 rows from both security checks), all four enums byte-identical. ⚠ Stamped as **NOT a full regeneration** — column inventories and RLS detail are still 08-22 vintage, and the file's *"this file wins"* precedence is only as strong as **that** stamp. ⭐ **The drift found today is the argument for a real regeneration rather than a sixth spot-check: a spot-check only re-reads the facts someone already thought to list.**

**Left for Trevor, stated rather than dropped:** the five dead-upstream lanes (operator/cron-job.org, whose console is a secret-leak hazard) · #56's 09-13 verify row · `MEMORY.md` still ~800 bytes over with no trimmable slack, so getting under it means retiring memories, which is a judgement call about which facts matter.

## 2026-09-07 (late PT) → 09-08 (07:0x) · Claude Code (cloud) — the final free Dune cycle: 84,868 sellers recovered, and five of my own load-bearing claims died to measurement

**Trevor:** "What would be good use of our final monthly dune credits before they close off their free query accounts?" → "start consuming credits responsibly to move towards our goals and positively impact key metrics" → "do what you think is best for RPC long term and for our users"

**Outcome.** 84,868 `nba_top_shot` seller addresses recovered below the 2023-11-08 spork wall, where nothing free reaches. Below-wall gap 909,970 → 825,102; 2023-08 filled 19,565 → 98,639. 1,034,812 datapoints of a 1,000,000 cap, 30 executions, ~300 est. credits of 2,500. Audit clean: 0 malformed addresses, 0 self-trades, 8,976 distinct sellers, `sales_counterparty_recovered` matching exactly. The lane stopped by its own gate — five consecutive ticks `ok: true` / `budget_stopped` / ~400 ms.

⭐ **The session's real output was the measurement, not the rows. My opening recommendation would have bought ZERO.** I proposed spending the cycle on 210,801 pre-2021 counterparties as an "irreplaceable archive". Dune returns **nothing** for 2020 — ten clean windows over 2020-11-27..2020-12-17, every one `rows_returned: 0`. Four more claims died the same way: 699,169 of the rows I called Dune-only are served **free** by Atlas (floor bracketed at ~2021 across 9 probed Moments, 7 carrying 2020 sales, zero 2020 records); credits were never the binding meter (~0.34/window actual vs the 10/execution our state table estimates); #67's ten-day sales hole was **self-healing** at a measured ~150–190 rows/hour; and the UFC "prize" I reserved 450k datapoints for is a market that **closed 2026-05-13**.

⚠ **The one repeated error, and it is the transferable lesson: VALUE ASSERTED FROM VOLUME.** Twice in one night I sized a prize by row count without reading who consumes it — 778,032 UFC/Golazos rows (market closed), then the backfill itself. The consumer check afterwards: **of 109 `saved_wallets`, exactly ONE gained sell-side history — 10 rows, $120.** With ~1 WAU the near-term user impact is ~nil; the value is archival and latent. **A row count is not a benefit. Measure the consumer overlap FIRST, then size the spend.** The rule worked when finally applied: on the Golazos question I ran the consumer check first (1 of 2,586 rows touches a saved wallet) and it reached the right verdict before any feasibility work.

⚠ **Four execution-level corrections, each recorded.** A single `QUERY_STATE_FAILED` read as an era/schema fault — it was a transient, and the same era ran clean an hour later (**one failed execution is not a diagnosis**). A stranded 100k datapoints blamed on the wrong lane's cap before reading `dune_budget_status` — the real term was `day_datapoint_cap`, and **there are TWO day caps** (`day_row_cap` AND `day_datapoint_cap`); raising only the first leaves the second silently binding. A sunset job scheduled 09-11 that was **both redundant and harmful** — redundant because the spent cycle cap already stops the lane in ~400 ms without reaching Dune, harmful because it would have killed the 14-day Plus trial window; moved to 09-23, ahead of the real risk at the 09-24 cycle reset. And `ownership-sync-dune` described as "fires weekly, next Monday 11:40Z" when it has **no caller at all** (absent from `vercel.json` and `.github/`, last run 08-24) — a live cadence inferred from four historical runs.

⭐ **The durable artifact is the counterparty source-floor map, now in `docs/reference/apis-and-cadence.md`.** Three of the four sources hide their floor behind a SUCCESS: Flow REST answers **200 with an empty body** below the spork wall, Dune returns **`rows_returned: 0`** below ~2021, Atlas simply holds nothing pre-2021 (and hard-rejects `product: 'ufc'`/`'golazos'` with 400 — not the silent-firehose fallback an unknown *key* produces). Flowty carries no counterparties at all. **Consequence: 2020 Top Shot counterparties — 79,160 sales — sit below every floor and are permanently unrecoverable.** Nobody should fund that chase again, and no surface should imply provenance for that era.

⭐ **Also resolved, with a controlled probe: the `*_studio_history_v1` hash question — and the answer DIFFERS BY COLLECTION.** Randomised sample, all **above** the wall so a pruned empty-200 could not be misread as "not a purchase", with a positive-control arm of already-filled rows. All Day filled **3/3** real purchases, All Day null **2/3**, UFC null **0/3**, Golazos null **0/3**. The reusable discriminator: a **purchase** carries `ListingCompleted` + an NFT `.Withdraw` at **62–81 events**; a **listing creation** carries only `ListingAvailable` at **4–6**. Both answer `Success`/`Sealed` on a 200 — the event set is the only signal. ⇒ UFC/Golazos studio rows point at LISTING transactions and are structurally undecodable; All Day's are mixed and owned by a dedicated lane (`allday-buyer-backfill`, 16/16 ok, 1,920 rows/48 h). **This RETRACTED my own filing that Golazos's absence from `claim_sales_counterparty_batch` was "a real gap" — it is deliberate and correct.**

⚠ **Two guards read `docs/sessions/**` (`check-retired-rules.mjs`, `check-memory-doc-links.mjs`) and BOTH exclude it as frozen history — which is also why session entries carry no markdown links.** Grepping for the guards that read a file before editing it is what surfaced that.

**Left running:** nothing. pg_cron **jobid 475 `rpc-dune-free-tier-sunset`** (`0 12 23 9 *`) pauses the budget ahead of the 09-24 cycle reset. `ownership-sync-dune` sits at `cap_datapoints = 0` with its reservation freed (it has no caller either way). Reverts for every DB change are in the 09-07/09-08 ledger entries.

## 2026-09-07 (late PT, 19:0x–20:2x) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — the indexer was dropping ~740 sales/day, and fixing it opened two problems I had to go find

**Trevor:** "Keep going"

**Took #67 item (1), the one open item blocked on nobody.** `app/api/sales-indexer/route.ts` hit `unresolvedIds.push(nftId); continue` when it could not resolve an edition — the sale was never written to `sales`, never parked in `unmapped_sales`, and Step 7 advanced the cursor past its block anyway. **Nothing revisits a block below the cursor.** The route's own comment had described this for weeks. ⭐ **Re-derived live rather than trusted: six consecutive ticks dropped 41 sales in ~80 minutes (~740/day), every tick `ok: true`.** Every FMV reads `sales`.

**Shipped:** Step 6b parks unresolvable events **before** the cursor advances, with a per-tick dedup guard (`unmapped_sales` has no natural-key constraint and 1,985 duplicate groups already exist there from other collections, so the guard lives in the route). Serial parks as **NULL, not 0** — the edition lookup is what failed, so the serial is genuinely unknown. Drain is pg_cron jobid 474, **not** an inline call: this route is `maxDuration = 120` inside an `after()` body where a kill cannot be caught, against a promoter measured at p95 196 s.

⭐ **Not speculative, which is what made it a safe pickup:** `unmapped_sales` already held **24,583 nba_top_shot rows, all 24,583 resolved**, and the promoter is collection-agnostic (resolves via `nft_edition_map` / `resolution_hint` / `wallet_moments_cache`, all continuously hydrated by jobids 468/469).

✅ **VERIFIED IN PRODUCTION, split on the change point.** Three pre-fix ticks carry NULL for the new fields and dropped **112 / 8 / 3**; the first post-deploy tick reports **25 unresolved → 25 parked, `unresolved_unparked` 0** — and I checked the **outcome table**, not the self-report: 25 rows with serial NULL, price > 0, tx hash and hint present, covering exactly the window the prior tick would have discarded.

⚠ **THE HONEST RATIO, which is not the headline one: only 4 of the 25 are resolvable today.** The other 21 wait on the hydration queue. **The claim this earns is "no longer LOST, and recoverable" — not "recovered".** Stated in the ledger that way on purpose.

🚨 **TWO THINGS WENT WRONG, AND BOTH WERE FOUND BY LOOKING RATHER THAN BY BEING TOLD.**

**1. My change fired a destructive branch retroactively.** jobid 474's *first* tick reported **`archived: 24583`**. `promote_unmapped_sales` carries a 7-day retention `DELETE` alongside the promote, scoped to its collection argument — and **nothing had ever called it for Top Shot**, so eight months of resolved rows were reaped the instant a drain existed. ✅ Safe, and checked rather than assumed: every deleted row was already promoted, and `sales` holds **73,660** rows across exactly that window. ⭐ **Rule now in `database.md`: a collection-scoped shared function is not dormant for an unused scope — it is a LOADED ARM whose predicate has simply never been evaluated there, and the blast radius is that scope's entire history.**

**2. My change opened a duplicate-write race, and another session caught it within minutes.** Parked rows carry a `transaction_hash`; the Atlas lane writes the same sale with a **NULL** one; the promoter's only guard is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` on a **PARTIAL** index (`WHERE transaction_hash IS NOT NULL`), so the two **can never collide** and a second row would have landed. Duplicated sales are fabricated FMV volume — *the same class as the defect I was fixing*. I verified dedup WITHIN `unmapped_sales` and never asked who else writes that row. ✅ Zero escaped (control run: the single ±10 min twin found is a **real** flip — same nft, 94 s apart, $0.30 then $1.00). ⭐ **Rule now in `database.md`: when a change creates a NEW writer of an existing row, enumerate every OTHER writer and read each dedup index's `WHERE` — a partial unique index guarantees uniqueness only across rows satisfying its predicate.** Recorded in **my** ledger entry, not left in theirs.

⭐ **Then closed the instrument I had just created.** `unresolved_unparked` exists only to alarm at `> 0`, so I checked what reads it: **nothing** — no `v_rpc_trust_health` arm among its 37, no `get_pipeline_alerts_core` rule, no detector keys on a `pipeline_runs.extra` field. That is the *instrument nobody keys on* trap, which my own code comment had named two paragraphs earlier. **Rather than build a watcher, the route now THROWS** — the outer catch writes `ok:false` with `cursorAfter: null`, the cursor holds, the next tick re-reads the range. ⚠ The direction is a cost argument and the stall vector was **checked**: no NOT NULL column on `unmapped_sales` can fail permanently (`toIsoTimestamp` falls back to `new Date()`), so a park failure is transient infrastructure — exactly what a cursor hold repairs. **Negative control run: with the throw disabled EXACTLY ONE test reds, while the `CONTROL: a successful park still advances the cursor` case stays green**, so the guard is not the degenerate "never advance".

⭐ **Sibling sweep, so the class is bounded rather than assumed fixed** (CLAUDE.md: grep the EXPRESSION, not the file): of the **six** sales indexers, **Top Shot was the only one that dropped.** All Day / Golazos / UFC park in `unmapped_sales`; Candy parks via its own `park()`/`closeParked()`; Pinnacle writes the sale with `edition_id = null` for later backfill. Three different correct answers — and this route had a fourth that was not one.

**Context for whoever takes #67 (2):** Top Shot sales/day ran **4,426** on 08-24, troughed at **954** on 08-30, and is back to **2,220** on 09-07 — the Atlas lane alone went 35 → 881/day. Still ~1,700/day short of baseline; ~740 of that is what was being dropped (now recoverable), and the rest is the 08-28 → 09-06 history gap, which needs Trevor's Atlas-budget call.

**Suite 16,521 green; CI green; tree clean.**

## 2026-09-07 (evening PT, 17:2x–18:0x) · Claude Code on Trevor's box — a fabricated zero that vetoed the flag it sat beside, and a clean tree

**Trevor:** "Look through the roadmap, to do list, known issues, pipelines, etc and find things to work on" · then, mid-turn: "also, make sure you work through everything in the tree, and that we have a clean tree when you're done"

**Swept the queues first and picked the one item that was blocked on nobody.** `/api/best-offers` is half-refuted by its own author and its surviving half is a product call; the ~19 anon-public soft-404s need Trevor; the `offsan` candidate filed at 00:10Z is explicitly a *trend* question with no baseline. The pickup was inbox `2026-09-07T2135Z` — `refresh_atlas_pack_ev()`'s fabricated `total_unopened = 0` — which had deferred itself for coordination with #65's live lane. **That lane shipped overnight, no `.lock`, no `FREEZE.md`, so the deferral had lapsed.**

⭐ **Re-derived before touching it, and the filing was stale by three hours in the direction that matters: 333 → 573 rows.** Still zero of them positive-EV, against 12 positive among the 100 rows other writers leave NULL.

**The defect is CLAUDE.md's top class one table apart.** The success branch hardcoded `total_unopened = 0, depletion_pct = NULL`; `pack_ev_latest` overrides `is_positive_ev` when `total_unopened IS NOT NULL AND <= 0`. The view's rule is right — a sold-out pack cannot be +EV — and it was being fed **a fabricated `0` meaning "I did not compute this"**. The eight documented honesty properties did not cover it: property 4's `0/100` is the *failure* branch and is deliberate. Real supply was already in the joined `pd` row (`total_sealed > 0` on 57/57), so the `0` was **wrong on every row**, not merely unknown.

**Shipped `20260908003056`** — cursor selects `pd.total_sealed, pd.depletion_pct`, success INSERT writes them, NULL passing through as *unknown* rather than being coalesced. Two safety claims made **structurally rather than off today's data**: the `SELECT DISTINCT` cannot multiply rows because `pack_distributions_dist_collection_unique` is a UNIQUE index on (dist_id, collection_id), and the types match with no cast.

⭐ **Verified by SPLITTING ON THE CHANGE POINT, not by re-reading a total.** Same 57-dist sweep: the 23:25Z and 00:25Z ticks each wrote **42 fabricated `(0, NULL)` pairs**; the post-fix run wrote **0**, with 42 rows carrying real supply. Repo body and live `prosrc` byte-identical (md5 both sides). ACLs by `has_function_privilege`, not acl text; secdef drift array **length 0**.

🚨 **AND THE PIPELINE'S SELF-REPORT NEVER MOVED.** `pipeline_runs` reads `ok=true, rows_written=57, extra.rows=57` on **every** tick — the broken ones and the fixed one are byte-identical in telemetry. A textbook [[green-pipeline-blind-to-its-own-work]]: the only instrument that could see this was a query against the *outcome* columns.

⚠ **Chased an apparent survival rather than reporting around it, and it was the view.** `pack_ev_latest` still shows 15 atlas rows at `total_unopened = 0` with a NULL `depletion_pct` — which reads exactly like the defect persisting. It is not: the view NULLs `gross_ev`/`depletion_pct`/`fmv_coverage_pct` on the sentinel shape (`gross_ev = 0 AND edition_count = 0`) while passing `total_unopened` through RAW. **The defect signature was an artifact of reading a masked column beside an unmasked one.**

⭐⭐ **THE REAL FINDING IS THE PIN, WHICH WAS GREEN FOR THREE WEEKS.** A 455-line honesty pin asserted `total_unopened` **nowhere** and `depletion_pct` only on the failure branch — structurally silent about the two columns that can **veto its own subject**. Repointed with a column-level fixture audit and four assertions in *opposite* directions: real supply published · a genuinely sold-out pack still writing a **measured** `0/100` · unknown withheld as NULL · the fabricated pair banned at population zero. Two new fixtures (`D-SOLDOUT`, `D-NOSUPPLY`) make the second and third non-vacuous, and `D-FAIL` now carries real supply so the sentinel is proved to *ignore* it.

⛔⛔ **The verification harness lied in BOTH directions first, and that is the session's transferable lesson.** No Postgres on this box, so the pin was replayed against prod in a `pg_temp` harness. Calling the sweep inside the **same statement** as the assertions meant every sibling sub-select read the pre-call snapshot: **15 FAIL / 4 PASS, every cell meaningless** — the failures because the reads were empty, and the "passes" because they were `count(*) = 0` checks *passing on invisibility*. **A mixed scoreboard is more convincing than a uniform one, which is exactly why it is dangerous.** Sweeping in its own statement: **19/19, including a positive control that rows were written** — the one cell that can separate "clean" from "cannot see". Then the negative control on the OLD body: **all four new assertions FAIL, both must-not-move controls PASS.** Recorded in [[probe-null-results-need-a-positive-control]] as its fourth shape.

### The lesson worth the file: three compounding queue walks in one day, all mine, all caught by `duration_ms`

The Atlas edition dispatcher's per-candidate seq scan (morning), the chain hydrator's newest-first re-scan of everything it had itself named (69K buffers after six hours), and the wmc hydrator's "N unresolved rows" page (would have walked the whole 640K-row index every tick at convergence). Same shape each time: a walk that starts at the top of a queue whose top is what the walk resolves. Fixed the same way each time — a bounded slice of the INDEX behind a cursor, filters applied to the page — and then the second-order trap: a temp-table page has no statistics, so a set-form query probes every exclusion for every row before its LIMIT (5.5 s → 1.0 s walked row by row). Promoted to CLAUDE.md by displacing the timeouts bullet into `database.md`; the cases are in `cron-and-schedulers.md`.

### Also learned, and recorded where the next session will read it

- A new pg_net lane must be wired into `check_edge_fn_http_failures()` by request_id in the migration that creates it — mine paged as an unknown edge-function 400 two hours later. Session probes get `__probe__` rows the moment they are sent.
- Atlas addresses carry no `0x` (40,438 of 40,438) — `flow_addr_0x()` normalises; 1,773 owner rows my Atlas arm wrote unprefixed were repaired.
- The Atlas tick's ~8 MB of WAL per tick is full-page images of ~1,400 index pages — 0.5 % of the IO budget, not a saturation risk; two never-read `cached_listings` indexes dropped. Two `pg_stat_statements` snapshots joined on `queryid` alone double-count.
- The seed-wallet-refresh wave (:59 of 13Z/19Z/23Z) is the cause of the Atlas tick's 50–110 s outliers — every step slows together; nothing fails. Known band (#48/#61), recorded rather than chased.
- The cloud shell's clock runs behind the DB; `select now()` only, and read the tick you waited for by its own row.

**Open for the next session:** #67's route half (park unresolved Top Shot sales in `unmapped_sales`; the 08-28 → 09-06 gap for editions without an open listing; retire the `/api/ingest` GHA step); the moments hydrator worker's console entry when the queue reads ~0 (#21); the still-Trevor items (gate-key rotation, `e4tib3`, Sentry, WNBA Gold paragraphs, #50's pool source, the scorecard regex).

### The last stretch: what a green guard cannot see

Started from CLAUDE.md's standing question rather than a symptom. `no-fabricated-divisor-ratchet` has read green since it was written; it anchors on the `/`, which is exactly what keeps a legitimate `parseInt(x) || 1` legal — and therefore it matches nothing once the denominator is **named on a previous line**. Three live sites were inside its own roots:

- `components/analytics/WalletProfile.tsx` published a measured **"0%"** collection-mix share. `flowty_analytics_wallet_detail` does `COALESCE(ROUND(SUM(principal_usd),2), 0)`, so on a wallet whose funded loans all carry NULL the total *and every part* go to zero together and the substituted $1 denominator turns an undefined share into a measurement. **2 of 16** borrower wallets (`0x005ec64f25575b48`, `0x9f86ab4a834904e7`).
- Both `Sparkline` copies used `max - min || 1`, sending every point of a FLAT series to `(v - min) / 1 === 0` — drawn hard along the **bottom** of the box, visually identical to a portfolio that fell to the low of its window and stayed. **The same copy-paste pair this ban was written for, one expression down**, so both are fixed and both pinned.

⭐ **The rule promoted to CLAUDE.md: a guard anchored on an OPERATOR is blind to the hoisted spelling of its own class.** Ask it of every operator-anchored guard here. Full case in `testing-and-ci.md` ("A THIRD AXIS") and `key-files-and-honesty.md`.

**Guard widened on three axes**, each proved by injection into real files and reverted: the clamp spelling `x / Math.max(y, 1)` (added at population zero), the hoisted spelling, and the roots — curated four → a walk that also covers `scripts` (with `.mjs`, where one instance lived) and `supabase/functions`, whose exclusion rested on nothing since edge fns sit outside the coverage gates. ⚠ Hoisted enrolls only a **non-zero** literal fallback (`?? 0` divides by an explicit zero → `Infinity`/`NaN`, loud not plausible), and its first cut had two false positives that were **both the identifier inside a URL path** (`"/api/fmv/demo"`, `"/serial-premiums"`).

⚠ **The opt-out's fixed 3-line window swallowed BOTH suppressions I wrote** — a justification naming the measured population, why the fix is elsewhere and the exit condition does not fit in three lines. Now those 3 lines UNIONed with the contiguous comment block above, which stops at the first non-comment line. *An escape hatch that quietly does nothing is worse than none: the author believes it took.*

### Two honest limits, recorded rather than implied

1. ⛔ **The production half of the verification was not available.** `/analytics/wallets/[address]` is behind the sign-in funnel, and my anon fetch returned **HTTP 200 carrying the `/login` shell** — the "rendered DOM, not HTTP 200" trap, live. Evidence is the component pins (each re-run with the defect restored, and each fails there) plus CI 4983, not a production DOM. It also *lowers* the severity a bare "2 of 16 wallets" implies.
2. ⛔ **The `pack-supply-parse` pair is suppressed, not fixed.** At `totalCount === 0` it writes `drop_weight 0` for a whole distribution — a "never drops" claim. Measured **latent** (0 degenerate `gql_historical` dists), and left out because the fix needs both hand-copies **plus an edge deploy**, which would destroy the attribution if bundled into a display fix. Marker carries the exit condition.

ⓘ **One dead end recorded so it is not swept a third time:** the sibling shape — `?? 0` on a supabase count — is **drained**. 39 sites across `app`/`lib`/`components`/`workers`; opened one by one, every one branches on `error` first or fails closed. ## 2026-09-06 (evening) · Cowork (cloud + device VM), Trevor present — the Top Shot feed is back, from Atlas, read through the database

**Trevor's brief:** scrape hardcourt.io, Dune `flow/d23-tradeboard`, `pbc-challenge-tracker` for intel; look for Diamond's dashboards; "continue to look for ways to get our top shot feed back live… dig deeper into Atlas." ### What was found

- Watching what nbatopshot.com itself calls (real Chromium on an edition page) then re-probing FROM THE DATABASE with pg_net: `MarketplaceService/SearchMarketplaceTransactions` unfiltered is a platform-wide firehose (listings, sales with buyer/seller/fee/proceeds, offers; 200/page); by `editionId` it is the open book price-ascending; by `nftId` one Moment's history (a verification-by-listing check with no dead host); by `sellerAddress` a wallet's listings; `ProfileService/SearchUserProfiles` is the username resolver. Unknown keys are silently ignored.
- **pg_net sends only after the enqueuing transaction commits.** The first `apply_migration` failed its own in-transaction positive control (`atlas_timeout` on a call that answers in 4 s from `execute_sql`); the probe's queue row was gone after rollback. So a synchronous post-and-poll SQL function is impossible; every live read is two RPCs (`*_begin`, `*_collect`) — `lib/chains/flow/atlas.ts`.
- **Cloudflare's challenge on the Supabase egress is burst-sensitive.** ~5–15 % base `403 Just a moment` on the badge lane; my 60-request header A/B drove it to 100 % for ~4 min, every header variant alike. Do not A/B in bursts; read `topshot_atlas_market_requests.error` before blaming code.

### What shipped

- Migration `20260906203504`: `topshot_atlas_market_events` + `_requests`, `atlas_market_dispatch()/drain()` on pg_cron every 2 min (pipeline `atlas-market-feed`; first drain 400 rows), `atlas_resolve_username_{begin,collect}`, `atlas_verify_listing_{begin,collect}` (service_role). Drift check clean.
- Code: `lib/chains/flow/atlas.ts`; `resolveTopShotUsername` Atlas-first (dead-GQL ladder only on a read failure); `lib/verify-wallet-gql.ts` over Atlas; `verify-challenge/check` seller-matched — the verification badge works again (#59 option b) with the gate still dropped.

**Four code batches and five migrations to `main` in one day, every one of them found by looking at a real 390 px Chromium rather than the extension window.** Full detail per batch is in the ledger (four 2026-09-06 entries); this is what a future session should carry.

- 510 entity pages + 32 surfaces × 5 collections + the 12-path signed-in onboarding walk: 57 sitemap player URLs that 404'd (three classes — team Moments, legacy players with no `players` row, diacritics), "Unknown · Squad Goals" pack subjects, 394 dead AllDay thumbnails and 45 dead Golazos tiles rendering as broken glyphs (an `onError` that fired BEFORE hydration), a 46-second `get_collection_stats`, $0 tiles on a 15,284-Moment wallet (username sent to an address-only RPC, zeros under HTTP 200), All Day tiles wearing Top Shot art (id collision in `getThumbnailUrl`), an anon 307→405 class at the proxy, and **the same wallet headlined at four different numbers on four surfaces** (raw vs total − stale).

### The new-user walk (account `qa0906`, created and deleted the same day)

Create account → trophy case → save wallet → favourite teams → Collection / Market / Sniper × 5 collections → toggle. Found and fixed: dashboard tiles read **$0 / 0** for a brand-new account with no wallet (now "—" + "Add a wallet above"); profile saved-wallet rows linked EVERY collection to the Top Shot tab and printed $0.00 for market-closed ones; **wallet verification is dead platform-wide** (the listing host is gone since ~08-28) and the route said *"No matching listing found"* — a false claim about the reader's own account. That last one is a go-live blocker that needs Trevor's decision (#59); the route is honest now.

### Later the same day — Trevor delegated the needs-Trevor list ("decide what's best for RPC long term and our users")

Decided and shipped in one push (`092f886`): the verification gate is gone (history reads every SAVED wallet — the check had no data source, so "verified" meant "nobody"); a free `window.onerror` beacon with its own alert arm closes the client-error blind spot; **Candy MLB is live, thin** (overview only — the tabs are Flow-dispatched and a dead tab is worse than none); Panini is the WC Prizm plane (bridge not started). The trap the Candy flip exposed: `published` had been standing in for "Flow" in five fan-outs — each now filters on `dbChain`, and the guard that used to say "every published collection is Flow" now says "a published non-Flow collection is overview-only until its tabs have a dispatch". Left for Trevor because an agent structurally cannot: the two gate-key rotations, the #22 branch, cron-job.org cadence, the Sentry bill.

### Strategy

`docs/strategy/go-live-2026-09.md` is new: the measured state (24 accounts / 1 WAU; TS HIGH/MED 37.3%, down from 54.5% while the Atlas migration is incomplete), five blockers, eleven go-live bars with the instrument that reads each, the Candy checklist (~1 week, data plane complete, product plane missing — thin tabs recommended), and Panini's P0 decision (WC Prizm plane recommended). The roadmap carries a 09-06 stamp pointing at it. Vercel: 75% of $412 was Build CPU on a Turbo 30 vCPU machine — switched to Standard + queued with Trevor's approval (#61).

### Lessons promoted

- **A component that server-renders an `<img>` misses its own `onError`** when the load fails before hydration — replay the event from a mount effect, never a second state path. Now in `PackThumb` and site-wide in `DeadImageGuard`.
- **Headline = total − stale, disclosed, on EVERY surface that prints a wallet total** — there were four surfaces and two RPCs, and each had been "fixed" once. Fix per PANEL.
- **A test account is a data mutation** — enumerate every `user_id` table (19 + `follows` + `user_profiles` + `auth.users`; `public.*` has no FK to `auth.users`) and delete in one transaction; verify with a 404 on the public profile AND an orphan count.
- ⚠ **`left(cron.job.command, N)` prints gate keys** — two `?key=` values were echoed into this session's transcript while enumerating pack-opens jobs. Select `jobname` only; the keys are listed for rotation in the needs-Trevor doc.
- Two hourly pipeline-alert emails were a 40-minute Flow-host outage on 09-04 (rolled off by the 2-day window) and a single bad wallet failing a whole run (fixed in code): both suppressed with self-expiring `pipeline_alert_suppression` rows — the predicate lives in the `reason` column so the next reader does not re-derive it.

## 2026-09-05 → 09-06 · Claude Code on Trevor's box — a long autonomous stretch whose main output was four corrections to its own published claims

**Three code ships, two owed watches closed, and — the part worth reading — four numbers I published and then had to correct.** Every correction was caught by running a control, never by review.

### The corrections, because the pattern is the finding

1. 🚨 **A "90% reduction" that was window-selected.** I discharged the `ipfs-media` gateway-race watch on a matched-hours comparison (17.6/h → 1.71/h). Running the filing's *own* falsifier on a **second** window of the **same two days** gave **223% WORSE**. The series is burst-dominated — 26 events in ~3 minutes, silence for hours, then 32 in 45 — and **154 of 09-04's 194 daily events sat inside the one window I picked**. ⚠ **A matched-hours control corrects for traffic and does nothing about bursts.** Full-day, the only window-proof summary: **194 → 84, ≈51%.** ⭐ **The MECHANISM survived** — a positive control shows 100% of served requests moved to a different, faster upstream. **Magnitudes move; mechanisms tend to hold.**
2. 🚨 **A p-value computed against one day's baseline.** Step 6's "p ≈ 0.032" used the **highest** of four daily rates (3.25–8.67%). Corrected *weaker* to a 0.02–0.24 range. Only n≈200 made it baseline-insensitive.
3. 🚨 **An attribution read off a smeared field.** "The DEP0169 group's `routes` lists exactly the two importers" — one of the two imports **no Flow SDK at all**. Root-caused properly later (§below).
4. 🚨 **A units error that nearly became an alarm.** `get_edition_market_bundle` looked like ~3× the platform's top reader at 34.5M "blocks" — that was **read + hit**; by **physical reads** it is 1.93M and **11th**, 94% cache-served. It also nearly became a *fifth* correction: I wrote that a `get_runtime_errors` window bug "affects every number in this repo", then checked whether it generalised — **it does not**, and narrowing it confirmed an earlier filing was right.

### Shipped

- **`resolve-topshot-stubs` was losing 2.5% of ticks** to a 30 s wall while its own code declared a 120 s fetch bound. 3 kills, each matched 1:1 to an edge run over 30 s, **work succeeding every time**. Wall → 120, plus a tree-walk guard (455 routes, 181 with walls) banning "declared budget > wall"; mutation-proven, md5-restored.
- **A ban at zero on sitemap discovery** in `entity-smoke`. All eight arms are fail-soft, and `fetchSitemapLocs` returns `[]` on any non-200 — so **a broken sitemap made the ENTIRE client-side detection surface report success having rendered nothing** (Sentry has been dead since 08-18). Proven green (8/8) *and* red (0/8) against the real site.
- **The suite's most frequent flake** (red in 2 of 3 full runs, pre-existing) stabilised, with both dropped hypotheses kept in the file rather than inventing a cause.

### Root-caused: DEP0169, the platform's #1 error group

Not the Flow SDK. **`@onflow/fcl → cross-fetch@4 → node-fetch@2`**, which calls `url.parse()` on **every request** — so it fires on **use, not import** (importing fcl is silent, which is why "every cold start logs one" was the wrong model). Two static greps had failed at this; **patching `url.parse` to name its own caller settled it in three lines.** A version bump is foreclosed (latest fcl still pins `cross-fetch: ^4.0.0`), and the native-`fetch` shim was **probed locally with a matched control** — real Flow read OK, 0 `url.parse` calls, vs 2 and the warning in the control. ⛔ Not shipped: it sits under every Flow read, and the probe covered one plain GET, not the body/redirect/streaming paths where undici and node-fetch differ.

### Watches closed

- **Step 6**: 198 runs, 0 timeouts, 961 editions touched. Null excluded at *every* plausible baseline.
- **pack-reality / api/packs**: 0 events over 26.3 h. ⚠ Its positive control was re-run **at close** — because the same query gave a **false all-clear** the day before, when `routes: "/insights/pack-reality"` matched nothing (the real path is `/api/public/insights/pack-reality`) and *"No runtime errors found"* read as good news.
- **The stubs wall** closed in **hours, not the ~5 days a rate would have needed**, because the watch was built around **the one observation the broken version could not produce** — a run exceeding the old wall that still writes its terminal row. ⭐ At a 2.5% base rate, waiting for the *absence* of kills would have died of `pipeline_runs`' ~73 h retention before it could conclude.

### Housekeeping worth knowing

**Four orphaned filings committed on other sessions' behalf** — `rpc-daytime-monitor` and the night pass write to the mount and cannot push, and *"could not commit"* is indistinguishable from *"has not committed yet"* in `git status`. Each was read in full first. One had a **wrong supporting sentence** (`badge-sync.yml`'s schedule "was stopped" — it is still scheduled and ran 4× that day); its *conclusion* held, because the pipeline it names is written by a **different route**. ⚠ A **0-byte, 76-second-old** `index.lock` blocked commits — the recorded recipe says "0 bytes and hours old is stale", so age alone would have said *leave it*; the discriminator that worked was **no git process + a frozen mtime across two samples**.

## 2026-09-03 (04:10Z → 19:40Z) · the CI estate audit, drained in five passes, and the fleet alarm acknowledged instead of silenced

A single cloud session (`session_016PmC2SrSHtZuyZ9XpaPwyx`), started from "audit our CI and similar practices" and run autonomously overnight and through the day on "keep going". Everything shipped to `main` directly; every ship has a ledger entry with a watch and a revert. Report: the *RPC CI Estate Audit* artifact (status paragraphs cover passes 1–4).

**Pass 1 (c3f65841c) — every High and nine Mediums.** The push-time smoke measured the previous deploy (`sleep 45` against a 110–124 s build) and now fires on `deployment_status`; the worker tree gained a typecheck job (`workers/rpc-mcp-proxy` had no tsconfig at all); `run-db-tests.sh` asserts it found files; ten workflows had a bare `cat` that killed the step before its own warning on a connection failure; 401/403 became fatal where the workflow is a route's only caller; `allday-ingest.yml` (72 asks/day into a stub) deleted; timeouts, `permissions: contents: read`, inputs through `env:` everywhere.

**Pass 2 (198a89adc + 79c8ab453) — the docs-only path filter.** 58% of the last 500 commits touched only docs and ran 13 jobs each. A fail-safe `changes` job classifies the pushed RANGE (`github.event.before..HEAD`, never `HEAD~1`; a zero or unresolvable base is `code=true`), ten code jobs gate on it, every guard job runs unconditionally, and a docs push still runs the 107 tests that read docs. ⭐ The classifier is pinned by a test that EXECUTES the step body in a throwaway git repo (ten cases) — a regex would pin the spelling. Also: the Flow CLI install as a composite, the Playwright browser cache, the scheduler-liveness false positive on a brand-new workflow, and 1,600 lines of case history moved out of the three vitest configs verbatim.

**Pass 3 (e52a2ba01, 3776a3206, 06bdc8acd) — sharding and the composite.** The primary coverage gate runs as two shards plus a merge that applies the ratchet once to the merged report; shards zero their thresholds on purpose (a half-suite's 55% is not a number about the codebase). Merged coverage byte-identical to a single run, wall clock 6:12 / 4:51 / 4:55 against a 476 s median. One `rpc-call` composite replaced ten hand-copied curl blocks across six ops workflows, verified by a nine-case stub-server harness and two dispatches. ⚠ Two self-inflicted reds: the curl-guard test's floor did not know the composite existed (fixed by walking both roots and asserting the second root CONTRIBUTES), and the sha-pin test asserted something a composite cannot satisfy — shipped red for four minutes because the push chain echoed test output instead of gating on it. Both in the ledger as written.

**Pass 4 (00f38211e) — SHA pins, then current majors.** Every `uses:` pinned to a 40-char sha with a `# vX.Y.Z` comment under a ban-at-zero test, then all five actions moved off the deprecated Node 20 majors (checkout v7, setup-node v7, upload-artifact v7, download-artifact v8, cache v6) after reading each repo's breaking-change notes over anonymous `git clone --depth 1` (the releases page is API-gated from the sandbox; the repo is not). Verified by CI, three dispatches, a Playwright cache hit under cache v6, and a job log read end to end with no deprecation line; a scheduled ops-monitor run at 18:36Z on the new pins succeeded.

**Pass 5 — the fleet alarm, by decision.** The Pipeline Sentinel had paged CRITICAL hourly since 08-30 for `edge-fn-drift`'s 12-run streak, a red engineering cannot clear (4 of 6 drifted functions wait on `*_GATE_KEY` secrets, 2 are `DEPLOY_DEFERRED`). Trevor chose "build and ack, 30 days" via `AskUserQuestion`: `sentinel_threshold_config` gained `ack_reason` + `ack_expires_at` (pair CHECK), the route downgrades a critical with an unexpired ack to **warn — never ok** — and renders the reason and date ahead of the finding; an expired ack pages again with `[ACK EXPIRED …]`. ⚠ Migration applied BEFORE the code push, because the route's wider select against the narrower schema would have failed the config read — and that read was itself silent: `const { data } = …` dropped every override and every ack on a returned error with a report identical to a healthy one. Now a visible `Threshold Config` warn arm (R86). Verified on the real caller twice: WARN, banner intact, no config arm.

**Lessons promoted** (to `testing-and-ci.md`, `tooling-gotchas.md`, `cron-and-schedulers.md`): a test that waits for the fetch CALL and asserts the render synchronously is a race that passes locally 3/3 and reds a loaded runner; two concurrent sessions claimed the same register id twice in one day (R83, R84) — re-fetch and re-check the register in the same command as the push; the GitHub MCP run-list output exceeds the tool limit at any page size and job logs 404 until the job completes; `send_later` check-ins are bound to the session and die with it, so a pending read must be written down, not scheduled.

**Owed, written down rather than scheduled (this session archives):** the 09-04 ~12:5xZ scheduler-liveness report is the first fully post-deletion window for R61's shedding experiment (baseline 39 / 48 / 48 fires for the seven remaining high-frequency workflows; per-repo budget predicts a rise toward ~54+, per-workflow cap predicts ~48; one day is one sample). The positive half of R86 (the config arm appearing during a real `PGRST002` window) shows up in the sentinel's own `pipeline_runs.extra` when it happens. Detector Health's ack re-surfaces 2026-10-03; set the secrets or re-ack with a fresh reason.

**Still Trevor's:** the `*_GATE_KEY` secrets (R64/R21), the email alert channel (R62), retiring one of three `/api/smoke-test` concierge callers (cron-job.org console), and the pre-purge public branch (known-issues #22).
